# Review log: Data lifecycle operations do not terminate and erase every session and store they cover

## Standing context

### [remedy.2026-10-16.erasure-entry-points]
DECISION (2026-10-16, remedy after the `redesign` introspection verdict): the erasure entry points and the legal-hold preflight are converged as one table, the non-spec **Erasure entry-point table** (rows E1 erase without override, E2 erase with `acknowledgeHoldOverride`, E3 retry, E4 crash resume, E5 operator clear, E6 the runner's `initiated` phase), verified row by row against `pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go` (`handleEraseUser`, `handleRetryErasureJob`, `handleClearErasureRestriction`), `pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/legalhold.go` (`heldResourcesForUser`), `pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go`, and the staged CODE-6 and CODE-7. The table is the single normative statement of the mechanism inside this proposal, and every other site cites it — BECAUSE in rounds 2 and 3 every confirmed finding was a defect in text this run's own fixes had written, all in this one mechanism. The diagnosis: §12.8 step 0 and the §16.5 `ErasureJobFailed` `legal_hold_preflight` clause framed the preflight as a job phase that writes a failed job, while the tree, the §15.1 erase row, D15, Edit 2a, and Edit 2g framed it as a handler check that writes nothing, and Edit 2a with D15 made step 0's failed-job record unwritable. The retry decisions lived only in this log — ALTERNATIVES: a further fix round on the individual findings (rejected; each round produced the next contradiction from the same split framing); the job-phase framing (rejected; a blocked erase cannot write a failed job without the flag under the one-transaction `Start` of D15).
DECISION: what changed. Spec staging: new Edit 2j rewrites step 0 to the handler framing (the erase and retry handlers run the preflight before they write or restart the job; a blocked request writes no job, changes no job, leaves the flag as it was, and increments no counter; the event's `job_id` is present only for a retry; a retry applies no override; the job re-checks holds after the drain). New Edit 4h replaces the §16.5 `ErasureJobFailed` `legal_hold_preflight` clause with a pointer to step 0. New Edit 4g adds the step-0 pointer to the §15.1 retry row. Edit 2g now states only the single in-progress job and the retry order, and cites step 0 for the blocked outcome. Edit 2h adds the re-check to the `initiated` resume rule. Edit 2i replaces the §24.12 sentence with the same step-0 pointer as Edit 4g. Edit 4f adds the `job_id` qualifier to the §16.7 payload list in the same words as Edit 2j. Edit 4e is unchanged. The §15.1 erase row needs no edit. The CMP-026 label enumeration never listed `legal_hold_preflight`, so removing the label from step 0 and §16.5 narrows the spec and needs no CMP-026 edit. Non-spec staging: CODE-7 items 1, 6, 7, and 8 (exported primary-reading hold enumeration, a shared block helper with `jobId` on the retry path, `Runner.WithHoldCheck`), the CODE-6 `Restart` comment, T8.5 rewritten over rows E1, E2, E3, and E5, new T8.9 for E6, and REC-1 item 10 for the step-0 coverage divergence. Summary: D15 cites the table, new D23 (retry rules) and D24 (runner re-check), the Non-goal on re-issuing the erase now separates crash resume from the legal-hold recovery path, and D10, D11, and D20 are confirmed 2026-10-16 under the owner's standing delegation, with D20 sending pieces B and C and the full_revoke fixes to the follow-on proposal P5b after P1 (0093).
DECISION: OPEN [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1] (a hold set between the handler preflight and the runner's drain or `DeleteByUser`) is decided: the runner re-checks holds after the drain and before the first eraser, and a hold its own override does not exempt fails the job with `failure_phase: store_delete` and leaves the flag set (D24, row E6, Edit 2j) — BECAUSE the owner's standing preference on compliance paths is fail-closed, and the re-check does not reopen the job-phase framing: the request-time preflight still writes nothing, and the re-check is an ordinary failure of a job whose flag `Start` already set, the outcome Edit 2d gives a drain failure — ALTERNATIVES: a `legal_hold_preflight` label (needs a CMP-026 enumeration change, a §16.5 clause, and a code constant); a runner-emitted `gdpr.erasure_blocked_by_hold` (the tick has no request principal, and `ErasureJobFailed` already alerts); no re-check (fail-open on a preservation order).
CORRECTS [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN]: decided above.
CORRECTS [spec.2.review-mechanism.1 OPEN]: step 0 no longer sets a job `failed` on a block; Edit 2j reconciles it with Edit 2a's one-transaction start.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1 OPEN on spec/16 `legal_hold_preflight`]: Edit 4h removes the clause.
CORRECTS [spec.2.fix-G1.1 WATCHOUT and spec.2.fix-design-G1.1 WATCHOUT on the erase block path writing no failed job]: the divergence is now resolved in the spec by Edit 2j rather than filed separately.
WATCHOUT: step 0 (Edit 2j) is the one spec site that states the preflight rule. Edits 2g, 2h, 2i, 4e, 4g, and 4h cite it, and Edit 4f repeats only the payload qualifier in the same words. A later round that changes the preflight changes the table first and regenerates these edits from it.
DIRECTIVE [non-spec loop, CODE-7 item 4]: replace the `StartedAt + Deadline` context in `RunPending` with a per-tick budget, keep the SLA alert-only (`ErasureJobOverdue`), make every `fail` write use a context that is still live, and add a tier-1 test in which a job past its deadline is retried and completes. This deferral was filed three times ([spec.1.review-reliability.1], [spec.2.review-performance.1], [spec.2.review-reliability.1]). The next non-spec loop applies it as its first change rather than filing it again; non-spec Edge case 1 changes with it.

### [remedy.2026-10-16.creation-path-table]
DECISION (2026-10-16, second remedy after the spec-round-8 `redesign` introspection verdict): the creation fence's per-path behavior is converged as one table, the non-spec **Creation-path table** (rows C1 `POST /v1/sessions`, C2 MCP `create_session`, C3 `POST /v1/sessions/start`, C4 built-in adapter implicit create, C5 `type: mcp` implicit create, C6 single-shot binder fallback, C7 mcptools minimal fallback, C8 derive main insert, C9 derive-failure audit row, C10 replay, C11 delegated child), each cell derived from the tree (`sessionserver` `createAdmissionGates`, `claimMintPersist`, `createAndStartGates`, `mintClaimStartPersist`, `handleDerive`, `deriveChunkedCheckpoint`, `persistDeriveFailure`, `handleReplay`; `translator` `noopSingleShotBinder.BindSingleShot`, `writeSingleShotError`; `cmd/lenny-gateway` `sessionSingleShotBinder`; `mcptools` `create_session`, `requireActiveDelegator`, the `delegate_task` mapping; `delegation.Service` `buildChildSession`, `materializeExport`, `insertChildSession`, `Delegate`; `mcpruntimes.Handler`) and from the staged CODE-1 and CODE-2. Edit 1a's per-path sentences, Edit 1d, CODE-1 items 1, 3, and 7, CODE-2, T1.1, T1.6, T2.1 to T2.3, X1, X2, and summary D3, D4, and D5 are regenerated from it — BECAUSE the confirmed findings stopped falling (0, 3, 0, 2) after the erasure remedy, and the churn moved to the fence's per-path behavior: a late design defect (the derive crash residue) and staged code that contradicted D3, D4, and D5 (the `ParentSessionID` classifier, a tenant gate ahead of the owner gate) — ALTERNATIVES: another fix round on the open items (rejected; each per-path statement lived at several sites); folding the paths into the erasure table (rejected; a different mechanism with different columns).
FACT: the tree has no `type: mcp` session insert. `cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go` mounts `mcpruntimes.New(w.runtimes, nil)`, and the handler writes no session row, so row C5 records the absence. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcpruntimes/mcpruntimes.go; a grep for session-store `.Create(` calls under pkg/ and cmd/ finds eight sites, which rows C1 to C11 cover (C1 to C4 share two of them).
FACT: a delegated child's file export writes durable objects under the child's ID before `insertChildSession` calls `Create` (`buildChildSession` mints `childID` and runs `materializeExport`, whose `exportwire.BlobSink` persists into `w.blobs`). This is the same residue class as derive's copy, and no earlier round named it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go `buildChildSession`; cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go `export.NewMaterializer`.
FACT: the built-in adapters set the session owner from the request's OpenAI `user` field, and `POST /v1/sessions` and `/start` set it from the body's `userId`, so the owner the fence reads is client-supplied on C1 to C4 and can differ from the principal. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/translator/open_responses.go and openai_chat.go `SingleShotSpec{UserID: req.User}`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go `validateAndBuildCreateRow`.
DECISION (derive crash residue, closes OPEN [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]): a path that writes data keyed to a new session ID before its insert (C8, and C11 with `fileExport`) first reserves the ID for its owner through `store.Reserve`, which runs the fence and records the ID in a new `session_reservations` table (MIG-2); the erasure lister returns reserved IDs with the owner's session IDs; `Create` ends the reservation; a refusal runs the undo and then releases it (D25) — BECAUSE erasers enumerate the owner's session rows, so a copy with no row is unreachable after a crash, and the owner's standing preference is fail-closed with no unerasable residue for an erased user. The reservation also moves derive's fence check ahead of the copy, and it keeps the partial copy of a failed derive reachable — ALTERNATIVES: accepting the residue as a REC-1 residual with a bounding fallback (rejected; no bound exists for the chunked copy, the legacy object's TTL is 7 days, and the residue outlives the erasure receipt); keeping the derived manifest partial until the insert so the §12.5 backstop sweeps it (rejected; covers only the chunked path and only after the receipt); a `user_id` column on the checkpoint manifest (rejected; the legacy copy and the export have no manifest, and the manifest eraser is P5b's); a waiting erasure step that holds `initiated` until reservations clear (rejected; a crashed reservation never clears without a copy deadline the tree does not have).
DECISION (independent review of this remedy): reserving paths insert through `CreateReserved`, which deletes the reservation with `DELETE ... RETURNING` and refuses with `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` when it is gone — BECAUSE without it a derive copy still running when the erasure completed and cleared the flag passed the fence and inserted a live session of the erased user holding pre-erasure data — ALTERNATIVES: erasure keeping reservations until their writer finishes (rejected; a crashed writer never finishes); a dedicated error code (rejected; only the owner's erasure removes a held reservation, and a catalog row for a race outcome adds surface without value).
FACT (same review): C8 and C10 emit the isolation-downgrade audit event before the gate or insert, so rows C8 and C10 list it as a kept side effect; a C9 row ends C8's reservation only when its `source.UserID` is the derive's owner; `blobstore.Store` has no `DeleteBySession`, so the C11 wiring type-asserts it as `buildSessionScopedErasers` does; the checklist now lands MIG-1 (0186) at S5 before MIG-2 (0187) at S6; Edit 1a states the residency placement as a requirement on the `StorageRouter` rather than as a description of **Data residency**.
WATCHOUT: the reservation residual is narrow but real: a write still in flight when the owner's session-scoped erasers run, followed by a crash before its undo, leaves data no eraser reaches, because the erasure deleted the reservation. REC-1 item 11 files it, and non-spec Edge case 4 states it. Do not re-file it as a new finding.
DECISION (delegated-child predicate): `IsDelegatedChild` is `Session.DelegationDepth > 0` (D4, Creation-path table column rule) — BECAUSE only `delegation.Service.buildChildSession` writes a non-zero depth (`DelegationDepth: uint32(adm.depth + 1)`), while `handleDerive`, `persistDeriveFailure`, and `handleReplay` all set `ParentSessionID` to the source, so the staged `ParentSessionID != ""` admitted a soft-disabled owner's derive or replay at the fence — ALTERNATIVES: an explicit flag on `sessionstore.Session` (rejected; a second field for a fact the depth column already records); `RootSessionID != ID` (rejected; the insert computes the root in SQL, and a derive inherits the source's root). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go `buildChildSession`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go and replay.go row literals.
CORRECTS [spec.8.review-docs-alignment.1 UNVERIFIED on `IsDelegatedChild`]: confirmed and fixed by the decision above.
DECISION (gate order): every creation gate evaluates `CreationMarkers.Refusal` over the tenant row and the owner's user row in one call (`requireCreationAllowed` on C1 to C4, `requireActiveDelegator` on C11, and `Reserve` on C8 and C11), replacing the staged `requireTenantState` plus `requireOwnerNotRestricted` sequence; `requireTenantState` is deleted; C6, C7, and C10 get no gate because they write nothing before the insert (D3, D5) — BECAUSE separate tenant and owner checks in sequence answered `TENANT_SUSPENDED` for an owner the fence refuses with `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`, and replay's staged owner gate guarded no side effect — ALTERNATIVES: reordering the two staged checks owner-first (rejected; two reads that can still drift from the predicate's order as rules are added); a gate on every path including replay (rejected; it adds a read for a path with nothing to protect).
DECISION (row E5): the operator clear refuses a job that is not `failed` with `409 ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED` and a flag whose `erasure_job_id` names another job with `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`; Edit 4i states both in the §15.1 clear row (D26) — BECAUSE a clear during an in-progress job reopens the fence after the drain, so a session created then survives the erasure, and §15.1 and §24.12 scope the clear to "after a failed erasure job"; the other-job rule closes the same hole for a failed old job beside a new in-progress one — ALTERNATIVES: keeping E5 as the tree has it (rejected; fail-open on a compliance path); refusing with `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` for a non-failed job (rejected; E3 answers the same condition with `ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED`, and REC-1 item 14 files that code's missing catalog row).
CORRECTS [spec.8.review-mechanism.1 DEFERRED on row E5]: applied by the decision above.
DECISION (D2 placement): SPEC-1 *Creation fence* now states a placement rule: a tenant's row, its users' rows, and its session rows are on one Postgres instance, and a `StorageRouter` that routes a residency tenant's Postgres writes region-locally places them together; a router that splits them must replace the fence before deployment — BECAUSE §12.8 **Data residency** item 2 routes a residency tenant's Postgres writes to the region-local endpoint without saying where the tenant registry row lives, and a split would fail the user half of the fence open (no user row read, insert admitted). The tree does not split them: `cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go` builds `sessionpg`, `tenantpg`, and `userpg` over one `pool`, the only regional Postgres routing is `StoreRouter.PlatformPostgresForRegion` for platform-tenant audit, and both `sessions.tenant_id` and `users.tenant_id` reference `tenants(id)`, so the tenant half fails closed on any instance without the tenant row. No claim-register row is added, because no deployed path departs from the rule; REC-1 item 13 files the unbuilt residency routing — ALTERNATIVES: a bounded departure with a claim row (rejected; nothing departs); leaving "as they do in v1" (rejected; it does not say what residency routing must do). EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.8 **Data residency** and **Multi-region reference architecture**; migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql and 0003_users.up.sql.
DECISION (`tenant.suspended`): Edit 4a keeps the audit claim without naming an event and cites §11.7, and §16.7 gets no entry — BECAUSE §16.7 lists the Section 25 audit events rather than every admin-operation event, the current §15.1 suspend row already states the audit without an event name, and the event name stays a code detail of CODE-4 — ALTERNATIVES: a §16.7 entry for `tenant.suspended` (rejected; wrong list, and no sibling admin event such as resume has one).
DECISION (§15.1 erase row): Edit 4j adds a pointer from the erase row to the **User-level erasure invocation** paragraph, where Edit 2g states the in-progress-job answers, including the override duplicate's `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` — BECAUSE the row says "Returns a job ID" and a reader of §15.1 alone would not learn of the `409`; the earlier map entry "None" did not account for that answer.
DECISION: summary D15, D23, and D24 are now one-line citations of rows E1 to E6 with their reasons, and the watch-out on the E6 override exemption cites the row, so the table holds the only phrasing of that rule.
DECISION: the standing DIRECTIVE on CODE-7 item 4 is applied: `RunPending` runs under a per-tick budget (`--erasure-tick-budget`, default 10 minutes), a budget expiry leaves the job in its phase, the SLA deadline only raises `ErasureJobOverdue`, `fail` writes on `context.WithoutCancel`, and T8.10 pins a past-deadline retry that completes; non-spec Edge case 1 changed with it. Checklist S1 now reads "SPEC-1. Lands every SPEC-1 edit.", and MIG-1 and MIG-2 are steps S5 and S6.
CORRECTS [spec.8.fix-G1.1 UNVERIFIED and spec.8.fix-design-G1.1 UNVERIFIED on "derive (both branches)"]: row C9 states that `persistDeriveFailure` has no response writer, and CODE-2 item 4 now adds the refusal branch only to the main derive insert.
CORRECTS [spec.7.review-mechanism.1 WATCHOUT on Edit 1a's 403 sentence]: Edit 1a now scopes the `403` to the session a request creates and states that a refused derive-failure row changes no response.
FACT: every staged spec anchor, including new Edits 4i and 4j, was checked by script on 2026-10-16 to match its target spec file exactly once.
WATCHOUT: the **Creation-path table** is the only rule site for the fence's per-path behavior. A later round that changes a path changes the row first and regenerates Edit 1a, Edit 1d, CODE-1, CODE-2, the T2 tests, X1, and summary D3 to D5 from it, as the erasure table's watch-out requires for erasure entry points.

### [remedy.2026-10-18.executor-resource-table]
DECISION (2026-10-18, third remedy after the non-spec round-7 `redesign` verdict): the erasure executor's resource discipline is converged as one table, the non-spec **Erasure executor resource table** (rows EX1 sweep run, EX2 salt rotation, EX3 retry, EX4 lease loss, EX5 shutdown, EX6 leaked lock, EX7 two or more replicas; columns pool connections held, locks, context and deadline, job write, alert), each cell derived from the tree (`pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go`, `saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go`, `billing.go` `pseudonymizeLocked` and `RotateErasureSalt`, `sampler.go`; `pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure_salt.go` `handleRotateErasureSalt`, `erasure.go` goroutines on `context.Background()`; `cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go` `startLeaderElectedSweeps`, `tenantdeletion.go` `tick`, `adminrouter.go` `saltlockpg.New(w.pgPool)` and `NewSampler(..., 72*time.Hour, ...)`, `main.go` deferred `watchdogCancel`; `pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go` `Gate.Run`; `pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go` `ErasureJobOverdue`) and from the staged CODE-6 and CODE-7. D16, D27, D28, CODE-6 item 7, CODE-7 items 4, 5, and 11, REC-1 item 14, T7.5, T7.7, T7.8, T8.6, T8.10, T8.16, and X2 are regenerated from it — BECAUSE rounds 6 and 7 were wholly self-inflicted and all fell in this mechanism, whose policy had been patched one facet at a time six times (deadline context, per-tick budget, ctx-ended exemption, own sweep, global limit with a MaxConns check, tenant rule) — ALTERNATIVES: another fix round on the carried defects (rejected; each patch exposed the next facet, because the root, the salt lock's hold-and-wait, stayed in place).
DECISION (saltlockpg, root fix chosen): `WithSaltLock` opens a dedicated connection with `pgx.ConnectConfig(ctx, pool.Config().ConnConfig)` (the `pgnotify` precedent), takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock` inside a transaction on it, runs `fn`, commits, and rolls back and closes on `context.WithoutCancel` (CODE-7 item 11, D27) — BECAUSE a waiter then holds no pooled connection, a holder holds one pooled connection at a time for `fn`, and a transaction-mode pooler pins one server connection from `BEGIN` to the end, so the lock cannot leak and no pool-size relation remains. This deletes the `MaxConns` startup refusal, the pooler-leak residual (old REC-1 item 15, now a finding closed by 0094 as item 14), the salt-rotation-outside-the-limit defect (a rotation no longer holds a pooled connection while it waits), and D16's pool reasoning — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the shipped concurrency and filing the hold-and-wait for P5b (rejected; a root fix is one function in a package this proposal already wires, and the filed path kept three dependent rules alive); `SET LOCAL lock_timeout` with an xact lock on a pooled connection (rejected alone; it bounds waiters but `MaxConns` distinct-tenant holders each waiting on a pool acquire still deadlock, so the `MaxConns` check would stay); `pg_try_advisory_lock` polling with the connection released between attempts (rejected; the holder still holds a pooled connection with a session-level lock that leaks under the pooler); a separate small pool for lock connections (rejected; a second pool, size flag, and lifecycle for what one dedicated connection per holder does); running `fn` on the lock transaction (rejected; `tenantstore` and the billing store open their own transactions, the reason `issuedtokenstore` gives for the same pattern).
FACT: the lock connection cost is one Postgres connection per concurrent pseudonymize or rotation, bounded per replica by `--erasure-max-concurrent-jobs` plus concurrent rotation requests. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pgnotify/pgnotify.go `pgx.ConnectConfig(ctx, b.pool.Config().ConnConfig)`.
DECISION (alert, carried defect): the sampler publishes `lenny_erasure_job_overdue_seconds{tenant_id, job_id}` (age minus the job's own `Deadline`), the unlabelled deadline gauge goes, and `ErasureJobOverdue` is `max by (tenant_id, job_id) (lenny_erasure_job_overdue_seconds) > 0` (CODE-6 item 7, D28) — BECAUSE `scalar()` over one deadline series per replica is NaN, and the constant 72-hour deadline also missed T4's 1-hour deadline, which §12.8 **Erasure SLA enforcement** requires the alert to honor — ALTERNATIVES: `max by (tenant_id, job_id) (age) > scalar(max(deadline))` (rejected; fixes NaN only, keeps 72 hours for T4, and `pkg/alerting/inproceval` cannot evaluate `scalar()` of an aggregation); a labelled per-job deadline gauge compared vector to vector (rejected; the in-process fallback reports label-matched comparisons unsupported, which would lose the alert where Prometheus is unreachable). The spec names no expression for the alert and no age or deadline gauge, so no spec edit follows.
DECISION (X2, carried defect): X2 seeds a live session per case and asserts that a primary listing of the scope returns no non-terminal session at the moment the tenant-deletion job leaves Phase 2 or the erasure job leaves `initiated` — BECAUSE "stays in Phase 2 until the drain succeeds" passed when a drain that left a live session reported success.
CORRECTS [remedy.2026-10-16.erasure-entry-points DIRECTIVE and remedy.2026-10-16.creation-path-table DECISION on the per-tick budget]: superseded in non-spec round 5, as the ledger's CORRECTS entries record. The table's **Context and deadline** column states erasure's context as the leader context with no per-run deadline; `--lifecycle-tick-budget` bounds only the CODE-5 Phase 2 drain. Do not reintroduce a budget for erasure.
CORRECTS [non-spec.6.fix-G1.1 OPEN on the salt-rotation route outside the limit; non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN and non-spec.7.review-performance.1 OPEN on the saltlockpg pooler leak; non-spec.7.fix-G1.1 UNVERIFIED on the leaked-lock residual; the ErasureJobOverdue NaN OPENs at non-spec rounds 2 and 5]: closed by the decisions above.
DECISION (OD4, owner decision 2026-10-18): a derive or replay whose source session's owner has `processing_restricted` set is refused with `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`, through `requireSourceOwnerNotRestricted` in the **Gates** cells of rows C8 and C10, run after the source session loads and before the isolation-downgrade audit event and any reservation (D29, CODE-2 item 9, Edits 2b, 2l, and 4k, T2.6, X5). Row C9 is unaffected, because the refusal precedes the copy, so no derive-failure row follows it. The check is separate from `CreationMarkers.Refusal`, so D3 holds; it runs before the fence's gates, so a derive or replay of a restricted user's session answers `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` whatever the new owner's markers.
DECISION (OD3, owner decision 2026-10-18): a user whose `processing_restricted` flag is set cannot resolve interactions and gets `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`, matching `requireActiveUserForAction` (D30, Edits 2b and 4k, CODE-2 item 1, T2.7). CODE-2 item 1 folds the action clause into that message, because the shipped text names session creation on the interaction path.
DECISION (OD1, decided 2026-10-18 under the owner's delegation): the terminal-tail residual gets an `ABSENT` claim-register row (`spec_anchor` `#2854-inter-replica`, `deferral_id` `R19`) beside REC-1 item 6, through REC-1 item 8 (D9) — BECAUSE §28.4 gives every normative mechanism statement a claim row, and §28.5.4 is the heading that records no channel between gateway replicas; R19 is the plan step the remediation plan names as successor for interim off-holder behavior.
DECISION (OD2, decided 2026-10-18 under the owner's delegation): Edit 4l adds the §15.1 `ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED` row (409, `PERMANENT`), Edit 4i names the code, CODE-7 item 10 adds the `errorclassify` entry, and T8.14 covers the E3 and E5 cells; old REC-1 item 14 is dropped (D31).
FACT: every staged spec anchor, including Edits 2b, 2l, 4i, 4k, and 4l, was checked by script on 2026-10-18 to match its target spec file exactly once.
WATCHOUT: the **Erasure executor resource table** is the only rule site for the erasure runner's resources (connections, locks, context, job writes, and alert). A later round that changes any of them changes the row first and regenerates D16, D27, D28, CODE-6 item 7, CODE-7 items 4, 5, and 11, REC-1 item 14, and the tests the table names from it, as the other two tables' watch-outs require.
FACT (independent review of this remedy, 2026-10-18): anchors, rows EX1 to EX7, the derive and replay order, the claim-register fields, and the in-process evaluation of the new alert expression were confirmed against the tree. Fixed: the `gatewaymetrics_erasure_test.go` retarget now covers the deleted deadline gauge, and the summary's problem statement and `errorclassify` watch-out count `ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED`.
WATCHOUT: `issuedtokenstore.Store.WithSubjectLock` keeps the shipped pattern and cites the old saltlockpg idiom in a comment. REC-1 item 15 files it; it is outside this proposal's code.

### [remedy.2026-10-18.article18-refusal-table]
DECISION (2026-10-18, fourth remedy after the spec-recheck round-15 `redesign` verdict): the Article 18 restriction refusals are converged as one table, the non-spec **Article 18 refusal table** (rows A1 create with an owner from the request, C1 to C4; A2 fallback create, C6 and C7; A3 derive, C8; A4 derive-failure row, C9; A5 replay, C10; A6 delegated child, C11; A7 REST interaction resolution; A8 MCP `lenny/respond_to_elicitation` and `lenny/dismiss_elicitation`; columns caller check, fence, source-owner check, order and answer, serialization point, refusal surface), each cell derived from the tree (`sessionserver` `user_gate.go` `requireActiveUserForAction`, `create.go`, `create_and_start.go`, `derive.go`, `replay.go`, `interactions_resolution.go`, `service.go` `CreateSessionService` and `ServiceCall`; `mcptools` `client_tools.go`, `mcptools_register.go` `registerInteractionTools`, `elicitation.go` `resolveElicitationTool`, `mcptools.go` `requireActiveDelegator`; `cmd/lenny-gateway` `mcpsurface.go` and `credentialsurface.go`) and from the staged CODE-1 and CODE-2. The **Creation-path table** gains the column **Marker rows read in the commit transaction, in lock order**, and the **Lock discipline** paragraph is now a pointer to it. D29, D30, new D32, D1, D5, Edits 2b, 2l, 4c, and 4k, new Edit 1e, CODE-1 items 2 and 7, CODE-2 items 1 and 9, MIG-2, T1.6, T2.6, T2.7, Edge case 4, summary lines 11, 12, and 162, and both spec-site maps are regenerated from it. BECAUSE spec-recheck rounds 14 and 15 were wholly self-inflicted, and every finding was a further facet of the refusals that OD3 and OD4 added, each fixed by a clause in a new place (the reservation rule, CODE-1, Edit 2b, Edit 4k, D29). ALTERNATIVES: another fix round (rejected; each clause exposed the next precedence or serialization facet); folding the refusals into the **Creation-path table** (rejected; interaction resolution inserts nothing, and the table's columns are per insert site).
DECISION (general rule, stated once in the table's **Serialization point** column rule): a refusal derived from a lifecycle marker that guards residue is evaluated under a lock that conflicts with the marker writer, inside the transaction that commits the guarded effect, and a handler pre-check is only an early-out. The fence and the reservation-ended refusal guard residue. The caller check and the source-owner check guard none, so each runs once at admission.
DECISION (`session_reservations.source_user_id`, dropped): the column, its index, the source-owner `FOR SHARE` reads in `Reserve` and `CreateReserved`, the source-owner erasure of reservations, and the T1.6 cases that pinned them are removed. The source-owner check runs once at admission (D32, A3, Edit 2b item (2)). BECAUSE a derive admitted before its source owner's flag commits copies into a session reserved for and owned by its new owner, whose erasure and the tenant's deletion reach it, through a crash too. That is the outcome of a derive that completed before the erasure started, which OD4 does not chase, so the column closes no residue that fail-closed requires. Replay and an owner-defaulted derive are already serialized by the fence, because their new owner is the source owner. ALTERNATIVES: keeping the column and moving its spec sentence into Edit 1a (rejected; it adds a second reservation owner, two locked reads, an index, and an erasure path for no residue the new owner's erasure misses); refusing a cross-owner derive outright (rejected; outside OD4). This reverses [spec.14.fix-G1.1] and [spec.14.fix-design-G1.1].
DECISION (OD3 on MCP, closes DEFERRED [spec.14.review-client-surface.1] and its UNVERIFIED): `lenny/respond_to_elicitation` and `lenny/dismiss_elicitation` are mounted on the client `/mcp` server that also serves `create_session` (`cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go` sets `Deps.Interactions`; `mcptools.Register` calls `registerInteractionTools`). CODE-2 item 1 adds the caller check to `resolveElicitationTool` over the principal's subject (`authmw.FromContext`), after the session lookup (row A8, T2.7). BECAUSE OD3 refuses interaction resolution by a restricted user, Edit 2b item (3) keys the refusal on the requesting user, and fail-closed is the owner's standing preference. ALTERNATIVES: recording the MCP tools as exempt (rejected; Edit 2b item (3) and Edit 4c would be false on MCP); checking `row.UserID` (rejected in the independent review; the tools never compare the principal with the session's user, so it admits a restricted principal resolving another user's elicitation and refuses an active principal resolving a restricted user's, contradicting Edits 2b, 2l, and 4k).
DECISION (C6 and C7 scope): Article 18 item (3)'s caller refusal holds on every request path `cmd/lenny-gateway` mounts. C6 and C7 run only when no single-shot binder or `SessionCreator` is injected, and `cmd/lenny-gateway` always injects both, so only in-process tests and `tests/testinfra/lifecycleharness` reach them. They gain no gate (D3), as the table's **Scope of the caller refusal** column rule states.
DECISION (OPEN [spec.14.fix-design-G1.1] on the **Gates** label): `requireActiveUser` is now named the caller check of the Article 18 table, which implements §11.4 for the requester and §12.8 Article 18 item (3), and CODE-2 item 1 has `requireActiveUserForAction` cite both sections.
DECISION (UNVERIFIED [spec.13.review-docs-alignment.1] on the §12.8 erasure-scope `SessionStore` cell): new Edit 1e adds the session-ID reservations to that cell, because the scope table is the DSAR-facing list of what erasure deletes and Edit 1a makes erasure end the owner's reservations.
DECISION (runbook DEFERREDs): DOCS-1 items 6 and 7 add `docs/runbooks/erasure-job-failed.md` (the drain and re-check causes; Step 3 no longer says a clear lets the controller retry) and `docs/runbooks/tenant-deletion-overdue.md` (the Phase 2 drain stall).
CORRECTS [spec.15.review-mechanism.1 DEFERRED on "whatever the new owner's markers"]: dissolved. Without the source-owner read in `CreateReserved`, the only source-owner refusal is the admission check, which precedes every fence evaluation.
CORRECTS [spec.13.review-single-source.1 DEFERRED on the **Gates** column rule restating the code order]: the rule now cites CODE-1 item 2.
CORRECTS [remedy.2026-10-18.executor-resource-table OD4 and OD3 DECISIONS]: the owner decisions stand as decided. Their mechanism is now rows A3, A5, A7, and A8, and D29 and D30 are one-line citations of those rows.
FACT: every staged spec anchor, including new Edit 1e, was checked by script on 2026-10-18 against the worktree's spec files and matches its target exactly once. Against `main`, the §15.1 anchors of Edits 4e, 4g, 4i, and 4j do not match, because `main` has rewritten those rows since this branch's merge base.
WATCHOUT: the **Article 18 refusal table** is the only rule site for which refusal each request receives from the caller check, the fence, and the source-owner check, their order, and their serialization point. A later round that changes any of them changes the row first and regenerates D29, D30, D32, Edits 2b, 2l, 4c, and 4k, CODE-1 item 2, CODE-2 items 1 and 9, T2.6, and T2.7 from it, as the other three tables' watch-outs require.
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a source-owner read in `Reserve` or `CreateReserved`, or a `source_user_id` column, to make Edit 2b item (2) hold for a derive admitted before the flag. Item (2) states that such a derive is refused only by the fence, and D32 records why.

FACT (independent review of this remedy, 2026-10-18): anchors, rows A1 to A8, the lock column, the absence of `source_user_id` leftovers, OD3 and OD4, and D32's residue argument (owner-defaulted derive, replay, crash mid-copy, ownerless rows, tenant deletion, C9) were confirmed against the tree. Fixed: row A8 and CODE-2 item 1 read the principal rather than `row.UserID`; the C8 lock cell and its column rule scope the reservation `DELETE` refusal to a row with an owner; the A2 verification note names the callers exactly; spec Edge case 13 names derive alone, because a replay's new owner is always the source owner.
OPEN: replay appends the source transcript after its insert (`replay.go`, `s.transcripts.Append` after `store.Create`), so a replay admitted before the flag can append transcript rows after the user's erasers ran. The race predates this proposal and is independent of `source_user_id`; a later round decides whether it is in scope.

## Ledger

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: every suspend request emits `tenant.suspended` with an `already_suspended` detail key decided under the row lock (SPEC-4 Edit 4a "Every suspend request is recorded in the audit trail"; CODE-4 items 4 and 7; T4.2, T4.5, X1; D18) — BECAUSE a repeat suspend re-drains and force-terminates sessions, §11.8 **Audit trail** requires every admin operation in the audit trail with operator identity, and the DrainSessions seam emits no per-session `session.force_terminated` row — ALTERNATIVES: a new event type (catalog constant plus IsKnownEventType entry for one bit); repeat suspend as a no-op (removes the retry path after a listing failure, undoing F-15.1.50); emitting without the key (T4.5 and X1 lose the discriminating assertion).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce "The operator repeats the request to retry the drain." in SPEC-4 or a "One pass reaches every session" sentence in SPEC-1 *Drain*. A tenant resume committing during a suspend drain clears the marker, so a post-resume session can be terminated or land in `failedSessions` while the marker is unset (edge case 4). Both sentences were deleted for that reason; the repeat-suspend sentence is the only home of the retry rule and D8 is the home of the one-pass reason. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go handleResumeTenant clears `Suspended` with no in-flight-drain check.
WATCHOUT: locked newness (`errAlreadySuspended`) stays even though both branches now audit: it keeps a repeat request from overwriting the first suspender's SuspendedAt, SuspendedBy, and Reason, and it supplies `already_suspended`. — EVIDENCE: non-spec CODE-4 item 2.
FACT: the "all admin operations ... with operator identity" rule is the **Audit trail** bullet under spec §11.8 Security Incident Response, and spec/ never names the `tenant.suspended` event (it lives in pkg/observability/audit/catalog.go and the admin router). — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md §11.8 **Audit trail**
UNVERIFIED: non-spec docs item 5 (`docs/api/admin.md` suspend entry) says "a non-empty `failedSessions` means the drain is incomplete and the request can be repeated"; after a concurrent resume a repeat re-suspends. The design marked it not-a-site; the non-spec loop should judge whether "can be repeated" needs the resume caveat.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: An erasure retry re-enters the job at `initiated` and re-sets `processing_restricted` in the one transaction that moves it out of `failed` (Edit 2g); the drain comes from Edit 2h's `initiated` clause and every eraser re-runs — BECAUSE after an operator clear the fence admits new sessions and new user data into stores whose phase already ran, so any resume at a later phase (with or without a drain) leaves that data unerased while the receipt claims erasure; today's handleRetryErasureJob already resets to `initiated` (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go handleRetryErasureJob doc comment) — ALTERNATIVES: drain-then-resume-at-persisted-phase (a failed job's persisted phase is `failed`, needs a PhaseLog computation, leaves window data unerased); drain at the top of every Run (same hole, contradicts T8.1 crash-resume counters); a separate SPEC-5 for the spec/24 row (needs a checklist step this loop cannot add).
WATCHOUT: Crash resume and operator retry are different paths. Crash resume continues at the persisted phase and re-runs no earlier step (CODE-7 item 1, T8.1); retry always restarts at `initiated`. Do not "unify" them into one resume rule. Restart must clear `ErasedAt`, or CODE-7 item 2's ErasedAt short-circuit skips the drain and erasers on the retry — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 item 2, CODE-6 Restart doc comment.
FACT: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md §24.12 retry row said "Clears transient errors and resumes from the last persisted phase", which contradicted the retry semantics; SPEC-2 Edit 2i deletes that sentence. docs/operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md, docs/api/admin.md, and docs/runbooks/erasure-job-failed.md say only "Retry a failed erasure job" and stay true — EVIDENCE: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:144.
DECISION: Deleted "The flag is never cleared before the receipt is written." from Edit 2e — BECAUSE the operator clear-processing-restriction endpoint clears it without a receipt, and the preceding sentence already fixes the job's receipt-then-clear order — ALTERNATIVES: rewording to "The job never clears the flag..." (rung 3 where a delete closes it); an "except via the operator clear" clause (hair).
OPEN: Should `Restart` reset `Billing.Disposition` so the pseudonymizing step re-runs on a retry? CODE-7 item 1 skips `Pseudonymize` when the disposition is recorded; billing rows created during an operator-clear window would then go unpseudonymized. Left unchanged this round by design.
DEFERRED [0094_fix_data-lifecycle-user-erasure-tenant-deletion-and-suspension-f.implementation-checklist.md]: S2 "Lands every SPEC-2 edit" now also covers Edit 2i in spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md §24.12; if the checklist names the spec files a step touches, S2 must add spec/24.
UNVERIFIED: Edge case 6's last sentence "The restriction is never lifted without a receipt (SPEC-2)." still carries the absolute claim; G3's edge-case reduction was expected to delete it. If G3 did not, the next round should scope it to the job.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a repeat suspend emits `tenant.suspended` (same event type) on every request, with a detail key `already_suspended` carrying the newness decided under the row lock — BECAUSE the repeat now force-terminates sessions and §11.8 **Audit trail** requires every admin operation in the audit trail with operator identity; the drain's `ForceTerminate` seam emits no `session.force_terminated` row (that event is emitted only by `handleForceTerminateSession`), so nothing else attributes a re-drain — ALTERNATIVES: a distinct event type (new catalog constant plus IsKnownEventType entry for one bit of information); keeping repeat suspend a no-op and retrying via per-session force-terminate (loses the retry path after a listing failure, which is the F-15.1.50 fix); no flag (two concurrent first-time suspends then leave two indistinguishable rows, and T4.5/X1 lose their discriminator).
DECISION: the suspend Description drops "The operator repeats the request to retry the drain." and narrows "a non-empty `failedSessions` means the marker is set" to "the drain is incomplete"; SPEC-1's "One pass reaches every session in the scope, because the fence admits no new session once the marker commits ..." is deleted — BECAUSE a concurrent resume clears the marker mid-drain, and the reason for one pass already has its home in summary D8, which names the concurrent-resume case. Edge cases 4 and 7 stay true through SPEC-1's success predicate and SPEC-4's repeat-suspend sentence.
FACT: the `tenant.suspended`/`tenant.resumed` event names live only in code (`pkg/observability/audit/catalog.go` EventTenantSuspended, excluded from Catalog() because §16.7 does not enumerate them); spec/ never names them, so detail keys are a code-level contract — EVIDENCE: pkg/observability/audit/catalog.go:67, :484-488
WATCHOUT: "When the drain cannot list the tenant's sessions ... the suspension marker stays set" (Edit 4a) is also only true absent a concurrent resume; it means the suspend does not unwind the marker. Not filed here; a later round may raise it — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edit 4a; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go handleResumeTenant does not check for an in-flight drain.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: an erasure retry moves the failed job to `initiated` and re-sets `processing_restricted` in one transaction (Edit 2g); the existing Edit 2h `initiated` rule then re-runs the drain and every eraser — BECAUSE an operator clear opens a window in which new sessions, and new user data in any store, can be created; resuming at the last non-failed phase (CODE-7 item 7 as staged) either skips the drain or, even with a drain, never re-erases data written to stores whose phase already ran, while the receipt still claims erasure. It also deletes mechanism (no resume-phase computation, no `phase` parameter on `Restart`) and matches the tree, which already resets to `initiated` — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "re-run the drain, then resume at the persisted phase" (needs a retry-only drain hook the runner cannot see without a new flag, and leaves window data unerased); draining at the top of every `Run` (same window-data hole, and two primary listings per active job per tick).
FACT: today's retry handler already resets a failed job to `initiated` and re-runs the whole sequence, relying on step idempotence — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:285-290, :316-321
WATCHOUT: CODE-7 item 2's `ErasedAt` short-circuit skips straight to the receipt; a retried job that keeps a set `ErasedAt` would skip the drain and the erasers, so `Restart` must clear `ErasedAt` — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 item 2
WATCHOUT: spec/24 §24.12 retry row says "Clears transient errors and resumes from the last persisted phase"; it contradicts a retry that re-enters `initiated`, so the design deletes that sentence (Edit 2i in SPEC-2). Row must stay one physical line — EVIDENCE: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:144
DECISION: "The flag is never cleared before the receipt is written." (Edit 2e) is deleted rather than reworded — BECAUSE the preceding sentence already fixes the order (receipt, then one transaction completes and clears), and the absolute form contradicts the operator clear kept in the same paragraph. The same absolute claim in edge case 6, summary D17, and the summary Goals bullet is narrowed to the job.
OPEN: a retry after an operator clear skips `Pseudonymize` when `Billing.Disposition` is already recorded (CODE-7 item 1), so billing rows written while the restriction was cleared are not pseudonymized. Whether `Restart` should also reset the billing disposition (and what that does to the salt and the receipt counts) needs a reviewer on the billing eraser.
UNVERIFIED: CODE-7 item 2 sets `ErasedAt` "in the Update that records the final billing outcome"; if that is the Update advancing to `verifying`, a crash in `verifying` resumes with `ErasedAt` set and skips the `Verify` re-run Edit 2h requires. A mechanism-lens reviewer should check the ordering.
OPEN: spec/16:504 says a `legal_hold_preflight` failure sets no `processing_restricted`, while SPEC-2 Start sets it at initiation and a retry re-sets it. Not caused by this group; a later round should check the legal-hold preflight against the one-transaction start.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
FACT: The drain's terminal pipeline runs on the draining replica, synchronously inside ForceTerminate (sessionAdminAdapter.onTerminal = sessionSrv.OnSessionTerminal → recordSessionCompleted: seal, signals, audit, billing, pod release/reclaim). Only a pod binding held on ANOTHER replica trails, released by that replica's coordination Sweeper via ReleaseTerminalBinding, which seals nothing and writes no row. A session made terminal by another replica just before the drain listing can still be in that replica's post-terminal hook when the erasers start. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go (ForceTerminate, onTerminal doc), pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go (OnSessionTerminal, recordSessionCompleted), pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go (ReleaseTerminalBinding doc)
FACT: No spec section names the Sweeper, ReleaseTerminalBinding, or the remote-binding release; §24.11 says only "transitions immediately to failed and the assigned pod is released to the pool". The SPEC-1 *Drain* sentence is therefore the one home of the trailing-release rule. — EVIDENCE: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md §24.11 force-terminate row; grep Sweeper spec/ → only "gateway sweeper" in §28/§29 REG-COORDMIRROR
DECISION: SPEC-1 *Drain* sentence names two residuals (another replica still running terminal steps it wrote, and a binding-holder's pod release) rather than only the pod release the reviewer's wording names — BECAUSE the REC-1 item 6 bound includes maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds, which only the first residual explains — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's pod-only sentence (drops the seal residual the bound was written for)
DECISION: §12.6/§16.5 configuration-only contradiction closed by one pointer clause in §12.6 and a pointer-replace in §16.5, staged as SPEC-1 Edits 1b/1c — BECAUSE §16.5's sentence restates §12.6's claim and an exception in both would be a second copy — ALTERNATIVES: identical exception clause in both (duplicate); deleting SPEC-1's StoreRouter sentence (hides the dependency D2 chose to state); new SPEC-5 (needs a new checklist step as well)

### [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: `Store.Restart` clears the recorded `Billing` outcome along with `ErasedAt` (CODE-6 doc comment, T7.3), and T8.8 now starts from a job failed in `verifying` and asserts `Pseudonymize` runs again before `Verify` — BECAUSE CODE-7 item 1 skips `Pseudonymize` whenever `Billing.Disposition` is recorded, so a retried job would never pseudonymize billing rows written during an operator-clear window and `Verify` (CountUser > 0) would fail every retry — ALTERNATIVES: making the `pseudonymizing` skip conditional on "not a retry" (adds a second resume rule; the fix-G2 WATCHOUT says keep crash resume and retry separate); leaving it OPEN (the staged design claimed every eraser re-runs, so it was a design defect).
FACT: A second `Pseudonymize` after a retry is safe: `pseudonymizeLocked` generates a fresh salt when `tenant.ErasureSalt` is empty and destroys it afterwards — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing.go:164-171, 199-205. `Verify` fails while the salt survives or `CountUser != 0` — EVIDENCE: billing.go:256-270.
FACT: No CODE-7 failure path leaves a job `failed` with `ErasedAt` set (receipt and `Complete` errors keep it in progress, CODE-7 item 3), so a retry test must start from a pre-`ErasedAt` failure such as `verifying`.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1 OPEN]: answered; Restart resets `Billing`. The OPEN line is closed.
USEFUL [spec.1.fix-G2.1 WATCHOUT]: the crash-resume vs retry split told me where the fix belonged (Restart's reset set, not CODE-7's phase dispatch).
FACT: Citer sweep for `Restart`, `ErasedAt`, `Billing`, T7.3, and T8.8 across summary, checklist, deviations, and problem statement found no hit falsified; summary line on `Runner.Run` re-running from the top concerns crash resume and stays true. Spec staging Edit 2g ("A retry moves a failed job to `initiated`...") needs no change.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: The documented Tier 4 topology puts session rows and tenant/user rows on different shards (sessions by routing prefix via SessionShard, tenant metadata via TenantShard), while §12.6 and §16.5 both say the multi-shard StoreRouter swap is configuration-only. SPEC-1's "must replace this mechanism" sentence collides with both. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:387, :578; spec/16_observability.md:603
FACT: sessions.tenant_id already REFERENCES tenants(id), so every session insert already takes FOR KEY SHARE on the tenant row; the fence's FOR SHARE adds conflicts only with plain UPDATEs (SoftDelete). tenantstore/userstore Update callers are all admin or lifecycle paths, not hot paths. Tier 3 = 200 creates/s, so tenant-row lock occupancy is about 1 and no hot-row finding stands at Tier 3. — EVIDENCE: migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:76; spec/16_observability.md:595
FACT: pgstore.Create is the only session INSERT site in the tree, so the fence's lock hold time is one short transaction. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:174
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: D16 relies on the tenant-deletion tick's advisory lock to give "One executor advances a given job at a time" (Edit 2h). That lock is session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` on a pooled connection, and the spec mandates PgBouncer `pool_mode = transaction`. The lock therefore does not reliably exclude a second replica: it is reentrant on a reused backend, and the unlock can land on a different backend. After a Postgres failover, reconcileOnce also keeps running on fresh pool connections while another replica takes the lock. The fix is code: use a transaction-scoped lock or a `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claim on the job row. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:228-241; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:41; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:29
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: The drain's listings call pgstore.List with no state filter. That loads every retained session row of the tenant, terminal rows included, from the PRIMARY twice per drain, and Phase 2 repeats it every 30s tick while a drain fails. The fix is code: add a non-terminal state predicate to the listing, which the spec text does not forbid. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:624-667; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go:184
WATCHOUT: sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate runs onTerminal (workspace seal, pod release, audit, billing) inline. A sequential drain of a large tenant therefore takes the sum of the seal times, and inside the shared lifecycle tick it delays erasure jobs that carry the 1h T4 SLA. This is code-owned (D16 and the drain loop), not spec. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:88-94; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The §24.11 row says only "The session transitions immediately to `failed` and the assigned pod is released to the pool"; no spec section states how a terminal written on one replica reaches a binding held on another (the Sweeper/ReleaseTerminalBinding path exists only in code) — EVIDENCE: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:137; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:12-41; grep of spec/ for "Sweeper|ReleaseTerminalBinding" finds only spec/28_communication-channels.md:138 (REG-COORDMIRROR writer)
FACT: §10.1.1 says a session in `created`, `finalizing`, or `ready` has no coordinating replica; the forced transition's post-terminal hook runs on the WRITING replica (it reclaims a pre-running claimed pod by name) — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:28; cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:21-28,88-93
FACT: sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate returns (row, state, false, nil) for an already-terminal row (ErrSuperseded), so concurrent drains and a natural terminal racing the drain do not land in DrainResult.Failed — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:64-84
FACT: the tenant-deletion single-flight lock is a session-level pg_try_advisory_lock on one pooled connection; the tick's work runs on other pool connections and never re-checks the lock, so a dropped lock connection (or primary failover) lets a second replica run the same jobs concurrently. Consequences for erasure are bounded (phase steps idempotent, duplicate receipt already accepted), so not filed against SPEC-2 Edit 2h — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:226-243
FACT: admin audit emit (`r.emit` -> EmitAdminEvent) returns no error and every admin action commits state before emitting, so the suspend audit-after-marker loss window is the platform-wide admin pattern rather than something 0094 introduces — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenants.go:607-628; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go:92-118
DEFERRED [0094_...non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-7 item 4 runs each job "under a context bounded by the job's remaining deadline (StartedAt + Deadline)". Once a job is past its SLA (T4 is 1h), that context is expired on entry, every store call fails, and Runner.fail uses the same ctx (pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:184-197), so the job can neither advance nor fail: it stays in progress forever, retry refuses it (not `failed`), and the MIG-1 unique index makes every later erase return the stuck job's id. §12.8 **Erasure SLA enforcement** (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811) only fires ErasureJobOverdue past the deadline; it does not stop the job. What is true instead: bound each run by a per-tick budget (or no deadline) and never by StartedAt + Deadline; any fail() write must use a context that is still live.
WATCHOUT: Edge case 5 in the spec-changes file says SPEC-1 owns the remote-bound terminal tail, but SPEC-1's sentence attributes all remaining terminal steps to "the replica that coordinates the session" and cites §24.11, which does not say that — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:22,35

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: a retry runs the legal-hold preflight in `handleRetryErasureJob` before `store.Restart`, and a block rejects through the extracted `handleEraseUser` block branch and leaves the job `failed` with `processing_restricted` unchanged (SPEC-2 Edit 2g; CODE-7 item 7; T8.5; docs item 4) — BECAUSE §12.8 step 0 says the flag is not set when the preflight blocks and the preflight precedes any destructive operation, and §16.5 ErasureJobFailed routes every failure_phase, legal_hold_preflight included, to the retry endpoint — ALTERNATIVES: see the WATCHOUTs below.
WATCHOUT: do not move the preflight into `Run`'s `initiated` phase (Edit 2h or CODE-7 item 1). `Restart` has already set `processing_restricted` by then, so a block leaves the flag set, which contradicts step 0 and §16.5, and the first run would also execute the preflight twice. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md step 0 "The `processing_restricted` flag is **not** set on the target user when the preflight blocks"; spec/16_observability.md ErasureJobFailed row.
WATCHOUT: do not let a retry inherit the original job's `OverrideReceipt` to skip holds it listed. That adds an exception predicate, and §16.5 already names a new erase request with `acknowledgeHoldOverride` as the override path. Blocking the retry is the fail-closed choice.
WATCHOUT: the tree's erase block path creates no `failed` job with `failure_phase: legal_hold_preflight`; it answers 409 and emits `gdpr.erasure_blocked_by_hold` with no job row. That is a pre-existing divergence from step 0, outside G1, and Edit 2g's "leaves the job `failed`" describes the retry case where a failed job already exists. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go handleEraseUser block branch (holds > 0 and no override → writeError, return).
FACT: today the preflight runs only in `handleEraseUser`; `handleRetryErasureJob` resets Phase to `PhaseInitiated` with no preflight and starts `runErasureToCompletion` in a goroutine, and the erasurejob runner has no hold check. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go handleEraseUser (`heldResourcesForUser` call) and handleRetryErasureJob (Update setting PhaseInitiated).

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the erasure retry runs the §12.8 step-0 legal-hold preflight in the retry handler, before `Restart` sets `processing_restricted`; a block rejects the retry and leaves the job `failed` and the flag unchanged — BECAUSE only a handler-side preflight keeps step 0's "flag is not set when the preflight blocks" true and puts the gate ahead of the drain — ALTERNATIVES: a preflight inside `Run`'s `initiated` phase was rejected because `Restart` has already set the flag by then, which contradicts step 0, and because it runs the first-run preflight twice.
DECISION: a retry does not inherit the job's recorded hold override (`OverrideReceipt`). A retry blocked by a hold the operator overrode earlier is resolved by a new erase request with `acknowledgeHoldOverride`, which is the path §16.5 `ErasureJobFailed` already names — BECAUSE the retry endpoint has no body to carry a fresh justification, and fail-closed is the owner's preference on security paths — ALTERNATIVES: exempting the holds the override listed was rejected because it adds an exception clause (hair) and a new predicate.
FACT: the only legal-hold preflight in the tree is `handleEraseUser` -> `Router.heldResourcesForUser` (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/legalhold.go `heldResourcesForUser`). `handleRetryErasureJob` resets to `PhaseInitiated` without a preflight, and the erasurejob runner has no hold check. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:104-131, :308-328
WATCHOUT: when the tree's erase block path finds a hold, it writes 409 and returns. It creates no `failed` job with `failure_phase: legal_hold_preflight`, although §12.8 step 0 and §16.5 `ErasureJobFailed` both say it does, so §16.5's "retry" guidance for that failure_phase has no job to retry. This divergence predates 0094 and is not in its scope. File it separately. — EVIDENCE: erasure.go:119-131, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:838, spec/16_observability.md:504
OPEN: a hold set between the synchronous preflight and the runner's drain or `DeleteByUser` is not re-checked, on either the first run or a retry (TOCTOU). Step 0 says "the erasure job enumerates ... before any destructive operation". A later round should decide whether the runner re-checks holds.

### [spec.2.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: Edits 1b and 1c anchor text matches the tree verbatim (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:387 "adding a Tier 4 implementation requires no application code restructuring — the swap is a configuration-only change at the store-router level."; spec/16_observability.md:603 "The interface boundaries in [§12.6]... is needed."). No other spec site claims the multi-shard StoreRouter swap is configuration-only; §8:397 and §12.6:667 are Redis/EventBus swaps. — EVIDENCE: grep "configuration-only|StoreRouter" spec/
FACT: Edit 2i's deleted clause matches spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:144; docs retry rows (docs/api/admin.md:843, docs/operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md:317) carry no "resumes from the last persisted phase" text. The only other "last persisted phase" is docs/runbooks/tenant-deletion-overdue.md:73 (tenant deletion, unrelated).
FACT: The new SPEC-1 tail sentence (writer runs the terminal steps, binding holder releases the pod afterwards) matches the tree: AfterTerminalWrite runs on the writer (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:52-80) and ReleaseTerminalBinding on the holder only releases or evicts (terminal_binding.go:43-79). A remote-bound session therefore gets no seal-and-export at all, which conflicts with the §7.1 seal-and-export invariant (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:115); that is a P2 code residual, not a defect in SPEC-1's text.
FACT: Summary D18's "§11.8 audits every admin operation" resolves: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:519 "All admin operations (revocations, holds, invalidations) are written to the append-only audit trail".
WATCHOUT: Edit 2g's retry "sets processing_restricted again" sits awkwardly with §12.8 Step 0 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:838: the preflight block sets the job `failed` with failure_phase legal_hold_preflight and does NOT set the flag). A retry of such a job, with the holds still in place, would leave the flag set. In the tree the preflight runs only in the handler before any job exists (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:105-133), so no such failed job is ever created. The text tension predates this round (Edit 2a vs Step 0) and I judged it below the bar. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:838, spec/16_observability.md:504
OPEN: Should Step 0's "set the job status to `failed`" be reconciled with Edit 2a's one-transaction start, given that the design creates no job record on a preflight block? A later pass or the owner should decide.

### [spec.2.review-performance.1]
FACT: Round-2 spec hunks (SPEC-1 drain tail sentence, Edits 1b/1c, 2e, 2g, 2i, 4a) add no per-session or per-request write onto etcd, Redis, or a hot Postgres key; the only new write is one audit row per suspend request (operator-rate). EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:35, :93, :133
FACT: The only spec sites that call a Tier 4 swap configuration-only are §12.6 Scaling extension interfaces and §16.5 Tier 4 definition, both staged; §12.6 EventBus (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:667) and §8 delegation-budget Redis routing (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:397) concern swaps the fence does not touch, and docs/operator-guide/scaling.md:324 and docs/reference/configuration.md:463 make no configuration-only claim. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:387, spec/16_observability.md:603
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-7 item 4 bounds each RunPending job by `StartedAt + Deadline`, and CODE-6 `Restart` resets neither field. A retry issued after the tier deadline (T4 is 1h) therefore runs under an already-expired context, so the drain listing fails at once and the job can never complete. §12.8 Erasure SLA enforcement only fires `ErasureJobOverdue` on a missed deadline and does not abort the job. What is true instead: the per-job context must not be cut at the SLA deadline. Either drop the deadline bound or use a per-run budget, and keep the SLA as an alert only. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:227 ("bounded by the job's remaining deadline (`StartedAt + Deadline`)"), non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 Restart comment; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:602 wires the resolver in production.
WATCHOUT: pgstore.List has no LIMIT and no non-terminal filter, so each drain listing (two per drain, every repeat suspend, every Phase 2 tick) reads the tenant's whole session history on the primary. The spec text does not mandate listing terminal rows, so this is code-loop territory. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/sessionstore.go:1108-1125, non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 3

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The round-2 spec diff touches SPEC-1 Drain (the last sentences), Edits 1b and 1c (§12.6 and §16.5 swap scope), Edit 2e, Edit 2g (retry to `initiated` plus re-set restriction in one transaction), Edit 2i (§24.12 retry row), and Edit 4a (every suspend audited). The Edit 1b, 1c, and 2i target strings exist verbatim. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:387, spec/16_observability.md:603, spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:144
FACT: Retry-to-`initiated` matches Edit 2h's resume rule ("jobs in `initiated` re-run the session drain"). A failed job never carries a written receipt, because a receipt or `Complete` error leaves the job in progress (CODE-7 item 3). So restarting from `initiated` cannot produce a second receipt with different content, and Edit 2e's identical-duplicate claim still holds. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 items 1-3
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-7 item 4 bounds each `RunPending` job by `StartedAt + Deadline`, and `Restart` (CODE-6 interface comment) does not touch `StartedAt`. A retry, or a crash resume, that runs after the SLA deadline therefore starts with an already-expired context. The drain listing then fails and the job fails again on every retry, and the T4 deadline is 1 hour (§12.8 Erasure SLA enforcement, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811). Fix: bound each tick's run by a per-tick budget, and leave overdue jobs to `ErasureJobOverdue` alerting rather than cancelling them. Do not reset the legal deadline on retry. Add a tier-1 test in which a job past its deadline is retried and completes.

### [spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the `gdpr.erasure_blocked_by_hold` payload has one spec home, §16.7 (Edit 4f, unchanged); Edit 2j's step-0 block now points to it with "with the payload [Section 16.7](...) lists" — BECAUSE §16.7 is the audit-event catalog, already lists the full payload (spec/16_observability.md:709), and already cites §12.8 Step 0 for when the event fires — ALTERNATIVES: keep the list at step 0 and point 4f there (would leave the catalog as the one entry without its payload); keep both copies (the drift the finding names).
CORRECTS [remedy.2026-10-16.erasure-entry-points WATCHOUT]: "Edit 4f repeats only the payload qualifier in the same words" is no longer true. Edit 2j no longer states the payload; the `job_id` retry-only qualifier exists only in Edit 4f.
DECISION: a duplicate erase for a user with an in-progress job answers `202` with that job's id only when the preflight finds no hold; one admitted only through `acknowledgeHoldOverride` answers `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`, writes nothing, and emits no `gdpr.legal_hold_overridden` (Edit 2g, row E2 HTTP cell, CODE-7 item 6, DOCS step 4, T8.5, summary D15) — BECAUSE the in-progress job's E6 re-check exempts only its own `OverrideReceipt`, so a 202 would tell the admin an override took effect that is neither recorded nor audited, contradicting §12.8's "compliance teams see every override" (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:840); the handler already discriminates on `overrideReceipt != nil` (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:118,172); INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION is the code the retry path already uses for an in-progress conflict — ALTERNATIVES: see the WATCHOUT below.
WATCHOUT: merging a duplicate's override into the in-progress job's `OverrideReceipt` and auditing it was rejected. It needs a guarded update racing the runner tick, a receipt-merge rule, and it can arrive after E6 already ran, so it exempts nothing. `409 ERASURE_BLOCKED_BY_LEGAL_HOLD` was rejected because the override was valid and the cause is the in-progress job. Keeping 202 and documenting the drop contradicts §12.8's override paragraph. Do not move the duplicate check ahead of the preflight: a held duplicate without the override must keep answering `ERASURE_BLOCKED_BY_LEGAL_HOLD` (non-spec line about check order in the entry-point table section). — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:840; non-spec row E2.
FACT: summary watch-out "The legal-hold preflight covers less than §12.8 step 0 names" said "Edit 2j keeps step 0's lists unchanged"; after this round the event tuple list lives only in §16.7, so it now reads "Edit 2j keeps step 0's hold list unchanged, and Edit 4f keeps the event's tuple list unchanged." — EVIDENCE: summary.md watch-out section.

### [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the `gdpr.erasure_blocked_by_hold` payload has one spec home, §16.7 (Edit 4f, unchanged). Edit 2j's step-0 text replaces its inline field list with a pointer to §16.7. BECAUSE §16.7 is the audit-event catalog, already lists the full payload (spec/16_observability.md:709), and its entry already cites §12.8 Step 0 for when the event fires. ALTERNATIVES: keeping the list at step 0 and cutting it from 4f (rejected because §16.7 is where every event's payload lives and the tree's catalog mirrors it); keeping both copies "in the same words" (rejected because it is the drift the finding names).
DECISION: a duplicate erase that the preflight admits only through `acknowledgeHoldOverride` (holds found, valid override, user already has an in-progress job) is rejected with `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`. It writes no job and emits no `gdpr.legal_hold_overridden`. A duplicate with no active hold still returns `202` with the existing job's id. BECAUSE the in-progress job neither records nor honors an override it was not started with: its E6 re-check exempts only its own `OverrideReceipt`, so a 202 tells the admin the override took effect when it did not (the job fails at E6 or has already passed E6 with no override audit). The handler already has the discriminator: `overrideReceipt != nil` (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:118,172). ALTERNATIVES: merging the override into the in-progress job and auditing it (rejected because it needs a guarded update racing the runner's tick, a merge rule for two receipts, and an audit for an override that may arrive after E6 already ran, which is new mechanism that closes nothing a rejection does not); `409 ERASURE_BLOCKED_BY_LEGAL_HOLD` (rejected because the override was valid and a hold is not the cause); starting a second job (rejected because it violates the one-in-progress-job invariant and the D14 partial unique index).
FACT: the recovery path stays open. Once the in-progress job fails (for example at the E6 re-check on the held resources), it is no longer in progress, so a new erase with the override creates a new job through `Start`'s ON CONFLICT predicate, which covers only in-progress statuses (non-spec CODE-6 item 3). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 3 (`WHERE status IN ('initiated','store_deleting','pseudonymizing','verifying')`).
WATCHOUT: once this design lands, the standing-context WATCHOUT in [remedy.2026-10-16.erasure-entry-points] ("Edit 4f repeats only the payload qualifier in the same words") is false. Step 0 then carries no payload list, and §16.7 is the payload's sole home. Compaction should replace it with: "the blocked event's payload lives only in §16.7 (Edit 4f); step 0 points to it."
WATCHOUT: non-spec-changes.md line 55 ("A duplicate erase for a user with a hold therefore answers `409`") stays true under this design, because both hold cases now answer 409 with different codes. Do not "fix" it into a second statement of the E2 HTTP cell.

### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in round 6 — BECAUSE the round-6 diff is Edit 2k (an error-code string) plus two non-spec cross-references, and a walk of SPEC-1 to SPEC-4 under crash, restart, and failover found every recovery path either idempotent or already recorded: the fence serializes insert and marker write under FOR SHARE versus the marker UPDATE; every drain lists after its marker commits (Start, Phase 1, suspend); an already-terminal row returns ErrSuperseded rather than a failure; a Phase 2 drain failure stays in phase and TenantDeletionOverdue (spec/16_observability.md:512) bounds the stall; a lost erase response is answered idempotently by the single-in-progress rule (Edit 2g); a receipt-then-crash produces a duplicate receipt that the post-restore reconciler replays idempotently (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:943-946) — ALTERNATIVES: filing Edit 2e "same content" against the `verifying` resume rule (rejected: the resumed verification re-checks only that no billing row carries the user_id and that the salt is NULL, both stable; the in-memory-salt sub-check that cannot survive a crash is pre-existing in the phase-field paragraph and the verification step, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:805, :895); filing the remote-binding tail after a successful drain (rejected: D9 and the spec.1 OPEN already carry it for a human).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-7 item 2 sets `ErasedAt` "in the Update that records the final billing outcome", which item 1 says is the same Update that advances to `verifying`, and "When Run or RunPending finds ErasedAt set, it skips straight to the receipt and Complete". A crash after that Update and before `Verify` runs therefore resumes past the salt-deletion verification, and the receipt is written with no verification outcome. That contradicts §12.8 **Erasure job phase field** ("jobs in `verifying` re-run the salt-deletion verification check") and the verification step ("The verification outcome is recorded in the erasure receipt"), spec/12_storage-architecture.md:805, :895. What is true instead: set `ErasedAt` only after `Verify` succeeds (or persist the verification outcome on the job and short-circuit on that), so a job resumed in `verifying` re-runs `Verify` before the receipt.
WATCHOUT: the user-erasure completion receipt is written while the persisted phase is still `verifying` (the 0042 enum has no receipt phase), so the Edit 2e crash window resumes through the `verifying` rule of the phase-field paragraph. Any change to what `verifying` re-runs changes the duplicate-receipt content claim in Edit 2e. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:805; non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 items 1-3
USEFUL [spec.1.review-reliability.1]: its ErrSuperseded FACT settled that concurrent drains (suspend, Phase 2, erasure) over one session do not inflate `failedSessions` or fail a drain.
