### [spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: a fence-refused derive deletes the derived session's copied objects and manifest row in the request, stated once in SPEC-1 Edit 1a *Creation fence*; the mechanism sits in CODE-2 item 4 (blob `DeleteBySession(ctx, tenantID, derivedID)` asserted on `s.blobs`, manifest `DeleteByUser(ctx, tenantID, "", []string{derivedID})` added to `CheckpointManifestWriter`), pinned by T2.1 and the widened X1 clause — BECAUSE derive is the only create path that writes user data before the insert, and nothing enumerates a copy keyed to a session ID with no row (§12.8 step 7 and `DeleteByUser` take the user's session IDs; the §12.5 backstop sweeps only `partial = true` manifests, and the derived manifest is finalised `partial=false`) — ALTERNATIVES: insert-then-copy as replay does (contradicts §7.1 derive rule 2 atomic row persistence); a lock across the copy (blocks the marker writer and still races); an orphan sweep (objects and manifests carry no user key); editing §7.1 rule 2 (a second edit site for an obligation only the fence creates); citing "as §7.1 derive rule 2 does" (describes its target, and the tree does not implement the rule-2 delete, derive.go handleDerive copy-failure branches).
WATCHOUT: the derive cleanup runs only on `errors.As(err, &ref)` for `*sessionstore.CreationRefusal`. Any other `Create` error can follow a committed insert, and deleting then destroys a live derived session's checkpoint. T2.1's "a non-refusal `Create` error leaves both" is the discriminating assertion. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go `handleDerive` main `s.store.Create(r.Context(), derived)` branch (line ~401).
FACT: derive writes before the insert; replay inserts before it copies. Chunk copy and manifest Put/ConfirmChunk/Finalise(partial=false) keyed `SessionID: derivedID` happen in `deriveChunkedCheckpoint`; the legacy single-object copy sets `dst.SessionID = derivedSessionID`. Blob `DeleteBySession` sweeps every object_type prefix of the session (checkpoint and workspace included), so it covers both destinations. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go `deriveChunkedCheckpoint`, `parseDeriveURIs`; pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go `DeleteBySession`; pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/pgstore/pgstore.go `DeleteByUser` (ignores userID, deletes by tenant and session_id list).
FACT: `CheckpointManifestWriter` has one production wiring (cmd/lenny-gateway/sessionsrv.go, `w.partialManifests`) and one test wiring (tests/tier4_integration/checkpoint_chunk_helpers_test.go, a real store); `pgstore.Store` and `partialmanifeststore.MemoryStore` already have `DeleteByUser`, so widening the interface breaks no fake. Blob `DeleteBySession` is absent from `blobstore.Store`; it is on MemoryStore, FilesystemStore, miniostore, and cataloging.Store, hence the type assertion.
UNVERIFIED: CODE-2 item 4 and its Targets say "derive (both branches)" / "both `store.Create` error branches", but the second derive `Create` is inside `persistDeriveFailure`, which has no ResponseWriter and cannot write a refusal. Pre-existing and outside this group; the next non-spec loop should check it.

### [spec.8.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 Edit 2b's replace target now runs through the end of §12.8 Article 18 item (2), and item (2) becomes a pointer to the §15.1 **Error code catalog** row for `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` — BECAUSE the catalog row (`details.userId`, `details.jobId`) under the §15.1 `{"error": {...}}` envelope is the one home of the error body; the old item (2) top-level literal contradicted it and Edit 2b's widening applied it to every creation path, including MCP and adapter-native envelopes — ALTERNATIVES: a corrected nested JSON literal (second copy of the catalog row, wrong for native adapter envelopes); a separate edit for item (2) (splits one sentence across two edits); deleting item (2) (dangling numbering).
FACT: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:807 item (2) states the ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS body with top-level `code`, `userId`, `jobId`; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1071 catalog row and pkg/gateway/sessionserver/user_gate.go use `details.userId`/`details.jobId` — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1005 (**Error code catalog** label), :1071.
WATCHOUT: Edit 2b's target ends at item (2)'s closing `.`; Edit 2c's target starts at `The restriction is enforced`. Keep them disjoint, and keep the replacement on one physical line.

### [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: a fence-refused derive deletes what its copy wrote, stated once as one sentence in SPEC-1 Edit 1a *Creation fence* after "the check inside the insert transaction is the authoritative one", with the mechanism in CODE-2 item 4 (derive main branch) and the pins as one clause each in T2.1 and the X1 checker — BECAUSE derive is the one creation path that writes user data before the insert, and the derived manifest is finalised `partial=false` so the §12.5 partial-manifest backstop never sweeps it — ALTERNATIVES: derive inserts first and copies after, as replay does (contradicts §7.1 derive rule 2 **Atomic row persistence**; far larger); a mandatory early check under a lock (the marker can still commit during the copy); a sweep for objects whose session has no row (no user key to scope it; a new mechanism); editing §7.1 rule 2 instead (a second spec edit site for a rule that only the fence creates).
WATCHOUT: clean up only on `*sessionstore.CreationRefusal`, never on every `store.Create` error. A non-refusal error can follow a committed insert (connection loss after COMMIT), and deleting then destroys a live derived session's checkpoint. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:401 (the bare `store.Create` error branch).
FACT: `DeleteBySession(ctx, tenantID, derivedID)` on the blob store covers both derive destinations: the chunked copy under /{tenant}/checkpoints/{derivedID}/... and the legacy single-object copy, whose dst URI has `SessionID = derivedSessionID`. The manifest row is removed by `DeleteByUser(ctx, tenantID, "", []string{derivedID})`, which pgstore and MemoryStore both implement. `CheckpointManifestWriter` has only Put/ConfirmChunk/Finalise. — EVIDENCE: pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:749; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:582-583; pkg/gateway/checkpoint/partialmanifeststore/pgstore/pgstore.go:479; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/checkpoint_seams.go:118.
FACT: the tree does not implement §7.1 derive rule 2's "partially written destination object ... is deleted by the gateway" on a copy failure. Both copy-failure branches return without deleting anything. That is a pre-existing build gap outside 0094, so the new sentence does not point at rule 2 as a precedent. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:330-342, 356-371.
OPEN: a gateway crash between the fence refusal and the cleanup still leaves an unowned copy that no sweep or user-scoped eraser reaches. This is the same residual class as rule 2's in-request copy-failure delete. A durable close needs a user key or a pending-derive record on the manifest, which is larger than this finding. A later round or a human should decide whether v1 accepts it.
UNVERIFIED: CODE-2 item 4 and its Targets line say "derive (both branches)", but the second `store.Create` in derive is inside `persistDeriveFailure`, which has no `http.ResponseWriter` and only records `outcome=error` (Edit 1d). `writeCreationRefusal` cannot run there. The next review-edit-sites lens should check this.

FACT: the tenant-deletion runner and the erasure runner both run inside lenny-gateway (cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go header comment), so Edit 3b's "the controller runs the drain again on its next pass" assigns the §24.11 forced transition to a process that owns the session store. EVIDENCE: schemas/ocsf-mapping.yaml:247-254; spec/25_agent-operability.md:4463
DEFERRED [0094_fix_data-lifecycle-user-erasure-tenant-deletion-and-suspension-f.non-spec-changes.md]: row E5 states, as post-landing behavior, that clear-processing-restriction clears the flag "in any job phase" and "checks no job phase". While a job is in progress, that clear reopens the creation fence after the drain has run. A session created then survives the erasure, and Edit 2e's completion later finds no matching `erasure_job_id`. The spec scopes the clear to a failed job (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:892 "after a failed erasure job"; spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md:145). The fix: make E5, the clear handler, refuse a job that is not `failed` (409 ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED, matching E3), and add a T8.5 case for it.
WATCHOUT: §7.1 **Atomicity of session creation (steps 2–8)** (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:23) says any failing step returns 503 SESSION_CREATION_FAILED. A tier-3 test author who reads it alone will expect a fence refusal at persist to be a 503. Edit 1a's 403 rule is the one that applies, and T2.1 already asserts the 403 mapping.

### [spec.8.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in round 8 — BECAUSE the round-8 spec delta (Edit 1d derive-audit `error` outcome, the Edit 2g duplicate-with-override `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION`, the Edit 2j payload pointer to §16.7) adds no retry, resume, lease, or drain path. A walk of SPEC-1 to SPEC-4 under crash, restart, and failover found each recovery path idempotent, operator-repeatable, or already DEFERRED to the non-spec loop. The checks: the §24.11 transition returns (row, state, false, nil) on an already-terminal row, so a session finishing during a drain is not reported as a failed session. A Phase 2 drain failure stays in phase (CODE-5 item 4 matches Edit 3b). A retry's TOCTOU window up to the first eraser is covered by the E6 re-check, which exempts no hold once `PhaseLog` records a failure — ALTERNATIVES: filing the lost-ack retry of an override erase (rejected: see WATCHOUT); re-filing the advisory-lock exclusion against Edit 2h's "One executor advances a given job at a time" (rejected: the sentence is a correct requirement, the shortfall is code, and it is already DEFERRED at review-log line 141).
FACT: `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate` runs the terminal pipeline (`onTerminal`) synchronously on the drain's context and is idempotent on a terminal row — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:51-97
FACT: the tenant-deletion lock is `pg_try_advisory_lock` on one pooled connection held for the tick, while `reconcileOnce` runs on other pool connections — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:226-243
CORRECTS [review-log line 399, spec.6/7 reliability "a lost erase response is answered idempotently by the single-in-progress rule (Edit 2g)"]: since round 7 this holds only for an erase with no override. A client retry of an override erase whose commit ack was lost now answers `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` without the job id. No GET-by-user endpoint exists (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:890 is GET by jobId only), so the operator recovers the id from the `admin.user.erasure_initiated` audit row or the `ErasureJobFailed` alert labels. The job itself runs to completion, so nothing is lost or applied twice.
WATCHOUT: the lost-ack override retry is a design preference rather than a spec defect. Do not re-file it unless the 409 is also shown to strand the job. The performance lens reached the same verdict (spec.8.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT).

### [spec.9.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the reservation-ended insert refusal answers `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` with `details.userId` only; Edit 1a names the code in its reservation sentence, and new Edit 4k widens the §15.1 `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` catalog row to cover the case and makes `details.jobId` conditional on `processing_restricted` — BECAUSE `CreateReserved` runs the fence before `DELETE ... RETURNING`, so a set marker answers first and the reservation-only refusal fires only after the owner's erasure completed (which clears both `processing_restricted` and `erasure_job_id`) or after an operator clear, where the old catalog condition (pending job and flag set) does not hold and `jobId` is empty — ALTERNATIVES: a dedicated code (rejected in the creation-path remedy; race outcome only); a cross-store lookup of the latest erasure job for `jobId` (extra read for a race outcome); erasure stamping the job ID on the reservation (a crashed writer never collects it); naming the code only in Edit 1a (catalog row still contradicts it, and Edit 2b makes the row the authority); `500 INTERNAL_ERROR` (misreports a policy refusal; C11 has no copy-failure answer).
FACT: the clear path empties both fields, so no `jobId` exists once the reservation-only branch can fire. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go `UPDATE users SET ... processing_restricted = false, erasure_job_id = ''`.
FACT: Edit 4k's anchor `` has a pending GDPR erasure job and `processing_restricted: true` is set. `details.userId` and `details.jobId` are included. `` matches spec/15_external-api-surface.md exactly once (checked by grep 2026-10-16). Edit 4c anchors on the same row's leading code cell, which Edit 4k leaves unchanged.
WATCHOUT: do not append the reservation case to Edit 1a's ordered marker-code list; that list is keyed on set markers, and the reservation refusal applies only to an insert the check already admitted. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edit 1a, sentence "an insert the check admits is refused with `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`".

RETURNING`), so this refusal fires only after the erasure completed or after an operator clear, when `erasure_job_id` is already '' — ALTERNATIVES: a dedicated code (already rejected in [remedy.2026-10-16.creation-path-table]); reading the latest erasure job id from the session store (a cross-store read for a race outcome); erasure stamping the job id on the reservation instead of deleting it (a crashed writer never collects the row).
FACT: once a user's erasure completes or the restriction is cleared, the user row's `erasure_job_id` is ''. Any "jobId from the user row" on a refusal that no marker answers is therefore always empty. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:187

### [spec.9.review-security.1]
FACT: `requireActiveUser` gates the PRINCIPAL on both §11.4 invalidation and `ProcessingRestricted` (answers `USER_INVALIDATED`, then `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`), so a restricted caller cannot evade the fence by sending an empty or foreign `userId`: the principal gate refuses first on C1-C4, C8, and C10. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/user_gate.go:35-60.
FACT: the owner the session row stores is client-chosen on C1-C4 (body `userId`, OpenAI `user`) and on derive C8 (`DeriveRequest.UserID` overrides the source owner). None of these body fields is defined in spec/15 (grep for `userId` in the §15.1 session rows finds none), and §11.2 billing schema `user_id` reads "User who initiated the session". The fence (Edit 1a "the row of the user who owns the new session") reads the same owner the row stores, as the directive requires. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_handler.go:20, create.go:241, derive.go:46-48 and :310-312, translator/open_responses.go:288, translator/openai_chat.go:242; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:98.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md REC-1]: add an OPEN unassigned finding: an unrestricted principal can create a session attributed to another user (C1-C4 body owner) and can derive any tenant session (no source-ownership check in `handleDerive`, pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:140-200) into an owner of its choice; the derived copy of a restricted or erasure-target user's workspace is then owned by someone else and is not reached by that user's erasure (§12.8 step 7 deletes artifacts "for all sessions owned by the user"). §10.2 (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:283) says a `user` cannot access other users' sessions. This is an authorization gap in code with no spec basis for the override fields; its remedy is code, so it was not filed as a spec finding.
DECISION: no spec finding under the security lens this round — BECAUSE every marker the fence reads is a Postgres column on the tenant or users row (migrations/0144_tenant_suspend.up.sql, 0003_users.up.sql `disabled`/`deleted_at`, 0009 `processing_restricted`), not a pod self-report or a Redis value; marker read errors fail closed (Edit 1a, Creation-path table Gates rule); the hold re-check read error fails the job (row E6); the §12.8 deletion sequence has no step that deletes the user registry row, so "a user with no registry row carries no user marker" cannot open the fence mid-erasure — ALTERNATIVES: filing the derive-owner bypass against Edit 1a (rejected; the bypass needs the code-only `userId` override or a missing ownership check, both code remedies).
DECISION (directive, D25): the session reservation is the smallest fail-closed design I could find for C8/C11: the erasers enumerate session IDs by owner, so any pre-insert write needs an owner-keyed record that erasure reads; a narrower variant (reserve only on C8) leaves C11's export unreachable after a crash.
WATCHOUT: if P5b adds the user-registry-row eraser to user erasure, Edit 1a's "a user with no registry row ... carry no user marker" fails the fence open between that step and `Complete`, and the completion transaction then clears a flag on a deleted row. P5b must order the registry-row deletion after `Complete` or keep a tombstone. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:261-284 (`DeleteByUser` hard-deletes the row); summary Non-goals "Erasing the user registry row during user erasure ... belongs to P5b".

RETURNING` returns no row. A derive whose owner resolves empty (`req.UserID` empty and `source.UserID` empty; Edit 1a admits sessions with no owning user) therefore gets a spurious 403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS with an empty `details.userId`. So a derive (C8) whose `req.UserID` and `source.UserID` are both empty, or a delegation (C11, with `fileExport`) whose parent has an empty `UserID`, always answers `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` with an empty `userId`. The spec (Edit 1a: "reserves the ID for the session's owner"; Edit 4k: "the user's erasure ended the reservation") does not require this outcome, so the fix belongs in the code staging. Ownerless sessions are reachable: row C1 reads "Empty: no user row is read."

Edit 4c catalogs interaction resolution for `USER_INVALIDATED`, but Edit 4k's `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` row names only session creation. The gap predates this proposal, and §12.8 Article 18 does not say whether interaction resolution is restricted. A human or a later proposal should decide whether that path is restricted (add it to the row) or over-blocked (code fix). This round did not file it, because it is pre-existing and the remedy is a design choice.

### [spec.11.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: the §12.8 tenant-deletion "background controller" is a gateway-hosted reconciler (tick plus Postgres advisory lock), not a Kubernetes controller, so Edit 3b's "the controller runs the drain again on its next pass" crosses no process boundary and touches no CRD status. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:706-723 (`tenantdeletion.Reconciler` built inside the gateway); pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:31-39 (`SessionTerminator` seam).
FACT: the staged spec edits write no CRD spec or status field, add no finalizer, webhook, or etcd-backed coordination; the fence and drain are Postgres row locks plus a primary listing. Pod release after a drained session is level-triggered through the coordination Sweeper's `ReleaseTerminalBinding`, so no request path blocks on a controller reconcile. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:112,533; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:42.
FACT: on C1 to C4 a fence refusal after a pod claim already rolls the SandboxClaim back (`rollbackClaim`, `rollbackBinding`) per the Creation-path table, so a refused insert leaves no claimed pod. EVIDENCE: non-spec Creation-path table rows C1, C3.
DECISION: no Kubernetes-idiom finding this round, including on Edit 4k (Postgres-only, no K8s surface) — BECAUSE every K8s-adjacent claim in Edits 1a, 3a, 3b, and 4a checked out against the tree — ALTERNATIVES: filing the synchronous per-session drain inside the suspend request as controller-on-hot-path (rejected; it calls the gateway's own forced transition and apiserver deletes, never waits on a reconcile, and CODE-4 runs it on a detached context).

### [spec.11.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: no spec finding under the end-to-end mechanism lens in round 11 — BECAUSE the round-10 delta (Edit 1a's reservation-ended code sentence, new Edit 4k) traces to one consistent outcome: `CreateReserved` runs the fence before `DELETE ... RETURNING`, so while `processing_restricted` is set the marker refusal (with `jobId`) answers first, and the reservation-only refusal fires only after completion or an operator clear (E5), when the user row's `erasure_job_id` is empty; Edit 4k's "`details.jobId` is included when `processing_restricted` is set" matches CODE-1 item 2 rule 2 and `requireActiveUserForAction` — ALTERNATIVES: filing tenant deletion as a second reservation-ender that Edit 4k omits (rejected; §12.8 tenant lifecycle ends in `TenantState` `deleted`, so the fence answers `TENANT_NOT_ACTIVE` before the reservation check, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:909-919).
FACT: the only session-store `DeleteByUser` caller is the erasure framework (pkg/gateway/storage/erasure/erasure.go:116 via contract.go:74), so "only the owner's erasure ends a reservation its writer still holds" holds for user scope. EVIDENCE: grep of `DeleteByUser(` under pkg/ and cmd/.
FACT: `persistDeriveFailure` inserts under `derivedID` (the reserved ID) with owner `source.UserID` through plain `store.Create` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:618-650), so a fence-refused C9 row is an insert under a reserved ID whose refusal is never returned; Edit 1a's undo sentence ends "before it returns the refusal", which scopes it to C8/C11's own insert. Judged a reading, not filed.
USEFUL [spec.10.review-client-surface.1 DEFERRED on empty-owner CreateReserved]: re-derived independently (derive.go:310-312 resolves an empty owner when both `req.UserID` and `source.UserID` are empty); it is a non-spec remedy and is already recorded, so not re-filed.

### [spec.11.review-performance.1]
FACT: The round-10 spec delta (Edit 1a's reservation-ended sentence now naming `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`, new Edit 4k on the §15.1 `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` row, and a Design bullet pointer) adds no write, lock, listing, or store dependency. The Edit 4k anchor matches spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1071 once. EVIDENCE: diff of scratchpad/cp-snap/0094-p5/spec-r9-prefix against the proposal dir.
FACT: This is the first performance pass over the D25 session reservation. Its cost is one short extra transaction (`Reserve`: tenant and user `FOR SHARE` plus one insert) and one extra row delete inside the existing insert transaction. These run only on derive and on a delegated child whose lease declares `fileExport`, which is opt-in per `delegate_task` (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md §8.7). At Tier 3 that is at most +2 row writes per such create, against a 200 creates/s budget, and they go only to Postgres. No etcd, Redis, or hot key is involved, because reservations are keyed by session ID. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 7, MIG-2; spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.3 Write IOPS estimation table.
FACT: §12.3 states RPO 0 with synchronous replication, so a Postgres failover cannot lose a committed reservation and turn an ordinary insert into a false `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS`. A lost commit ack on `Reserve` leaks one orphan row, which erasure or tenant deletion removes (non-spec Edge case 5). No code path retries `Create`/`CreateReserved` after a lost ack, so no retry can find its own reservation already gone and undo a committed session's data. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.3 "RPO: 0 (synchronous replication — no committed transaction lost)"; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go (no retry around the insert).
FACT: The erasure lister reads reserved IDs from the primary (`ReservedSessionIDs` "from the primary", CODE-7 item 8), so the replica-omission argument of the *Drain* sentence does not reopen for reservations. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 7 and CODE-7 item 8.
USEFUL [spec.6.review-performance.1]: its tier figures, its FK FOR KEY SHARE fact, and its Redis-not-engaged fact settled the hot-row and failover questions again without re-derivation.
DECISION: no findings in round 11 — BECAUSE the reservation is per-create, Postgres-only, small, and RPO-0 safe, and the round-10 delta is wording-only under this lens. The standing code-owned items (unfiltered drain listings, advisory-lock exclusion under transaction pooling, inline suspend drain against the 30s timeout) remain with the non-spec loop — ALTERNATIVES: filing the env-level residency case (a tenant whose environments span regions cannot co-locate its session rows with its users' rows). I rejected this because Edit 1a's next sentence already requires such a router to replace the fence, so the staged text is coherent, and the question belongs to the security and residency lenses.

### [spec.11.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in round 11 — BECAUSE the round-11 spec delta (Edit 1a names `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` for a reservation-ended insert; new Edit 4k widens the §15.1 row with `details.jobId` conditional) adds no retry, resume, lease, or drain path, and a crash/failover walk of the reservation sentences holds: Reserve and Start serialize on the users row (FOR SHARE vs FOR UPDATE), the erasure lister reads reservations from the primary, a crash before undo keeps the reservation so erasure still reaches the data, an undo deletion error keeps the reservation, and §12.3 RPO 0 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:150) rules out a lost Reserve commit producing a false reservation-ended refusal — ALTERNATIVES: filing the orphan-reservation row leak after a crash (rejected; intentional, it is what keeps the residue reachable by erasure, and rows accrue only per crash); filing a C9 audit row inserted through plain `Create` after the erasure ended C8's reservation (rejected; C9's pre-read of the source row fails once DeleteByUser removed it when source and owner are the same user, so the window needs a different source owner, whose data the row then correctly attributes).
FACT: Edit 4k's `details.jobId` condition is consistent with the mechanism: a reservation-ended refusal is reached only when the marker check admitted the insert, so `processing_restricted` is clear and no jobId exists to report. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edit 1a "an insert the check admits is refused"; non-spec CODE-1 `CreateReserved`.
DEFERRED [0094_...non-spec-changes.md, CODE-1 `Reserve`/`CreateReserved` and Creation-path table rows C8 and C11]: `Reserve` with an empty `ownerUserID` "runs the tenant half and inserts nothing", but `CreateReserved` "returns *CreationRefusal when no reservation was deleted", and C8 always inserts through `CreateReserved`. A derive whose owner resolves empty (`req.UserID` empty and `source.UserID` empty; `sessions.user_id` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT ''`, migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:77; derive.go:310-312) is therefore refused with `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` every time, and so is a C11 export child of an ownerless parent if it uses `CreateReserved`. True instead: an empty owner takes no reservation and inserts through `Create` (or `CreateReserved` skips the reservation check when the row's `UserID` is empty); the spec needs no edit because Edit 1a reserves "for the session's owner" and refuses only when a reservation "has already ended". Add a tier-1 case for an ownerless derive.
USEFUL [spec.1.review-reliability.1]: its ErrSuperseded FACT and the round-6/8 walks let this round confine the crash walk to the reservation sentences.

### [spec.11.review-security.1]
FACT: the round-10 to round-11 spec delta (Edit 1a names `403 ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` for a reservation-ended insert; new Edit 4k widens the §15.1 `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` row and makes `details.jobId` conditional) removes no control and adds no fail-open path. The refusal fires only for an insert the marker check already admitted, so it is strictly additional. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md Edit 1a reservation sentences, Edit 4k; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1071.
DECISION: no spec finding under the security lens this round — BECAUSE every marker the fence and the gates read is a Postgres column on the tenant or users row, the reservation table is Postgres (MIG-2), no bound is sourced from a pod self-report (C11's owner is `parent.UserID` from the parent row and `DelegationDepth` is gateway-computed), and every read error fails closed — ALTERNATIVES: filing Edit 2b's narrowing of item (1) from "for the affected user_id" to "a new session owned by the affected user_id" (rejected; the original text and the catalog row's "target `user_id`" were already ambiguous between requester and owner, and `requireActiveUser` still refuses a restricted principal in code, user_gate.go:35-60).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-7 item 10 and CODE-6 item 4 / Erasure entry-point table row E5]: the clear's "job must be `failed`" check is a handler read outside the users-row lock, and `ClearProcessingRestriction(jobID)` checks only that `erasure_job_id` is empty or equals `jobID`. A retry of the same job that commits between the handler's read and the clear (Restart sets `initiated` and `erasure_job_id = jobID`, row E3) passes the clear's check, and the session-local bypass skips the 0042 trigger, so the flag is cleared while that job is in progress: exactly the fail-open D26 closes. True instead: `ClearProcessingRestriction` must re-read the job's status inside its transaction after `FOR UPDATE` on the users row (Restart writes the same row, so the two serialize) and refuse unless it is `failed`; T7.2 or a tier-7a interleaving test should pin clear-vs-retry of one job. An alternative spec-side remedy, if a later spec round wants it: Edit 2f could drop the clear endpoint's trigger exemption, because after Edit 4i every legitimate clear finds no in-progress job and the 0042 trigger would then enforce the rule at the database level. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 (line ~311), CODE-7 item 10 (line ~340), row E3 flag cell; migrations/0042_erasure_jobs_processing_restriction_trigger.up.sql:90-92.
WATCHOUT: the 0042 trigger function is not `SECURITY DEFINER` and `erasure_jobs` has `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` keyed on `app.current_tenant`, so a users UPDATE run without that setting sees no erasure_jobs rows and the trigger admits the clear. Pre-existing and code-only; relevant to anyone relying on the trigger as the backstop for the race above. EVIDENCE: migrations/0042_erasure_jobs_processing_restriction_trigger.up.sql:66-69,95-101.
USEFUL [spec.9.review-security.1]: its FACT that `requireActiveUser` gates the principal on `ProcessingRestricted` and its DEFERRED on the body-owner authorization gap settled the directive's owner question without re-deriving it.

### [spec.13.review-performance.1]
FACT: The round-12 spec delta is Edit 2b (item (1) now cites the fence without a fixed code, item (2) scopes the details to an `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` refusal) and Edit 2c (now a deletion of the "session creation gate upstream" sentence). Neither edit adds a write, a lock, a listing, or a store dependency. EVIDENCE: diff of scratchpad/cp-snap/0094-p5/spec-r11-prefix against the proposal dir.
USEFUL [spec.6.review-performance.1]: its tier figures (Tier 3: 10,000 sessions, 200 creates/s), its FK FOR KEY SHARE fact on the tenant row, and its Redis-not-engaged fact settled the hot-row and failover questions again. I re-checked that no users-row writer runs per request (pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:136,186,240 are admin and lifecycle paths only).
FACT: §12.3 states the Tier 3 primary write ceiling as about 1,300/s sustained. The fence adds two row-lock WAL records per insert, and the reservation adds an insert and a delete per derive or fileExport child. Together these stay well under 10% of that ceiling at 200 creates/s. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:72.
WATCHOUT: erasure jobs now run sequentially inside the single advisory-locked tenant-deletion tick, after `ReconcileAll` (CODE-7 items 4 and 5). A long tenant-deletion phase, or a mass erasure, can therefore delay a T4 user's job past the 1-hour deadline in §12.8 **Erasure SLA enforcement** (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:811). Shipped erasure ran one goroutine per job. The remedy is code (a separate executor, or a budget for the deletion reconcile), so this is not a spec-loop finding. It is the "single-leader tick" item already listed as code-owned in [spec.6.review-performance.1].
DECISION: no findings in round 13 — BECAUSE the delta is wording-only under this lens, and the standing costs (unfiltered drain listings on the primary, advisory-lock exclusion under failover and transaction pooling, the inline suspend drain against the 30s timeout, the single-leader erasure tick) are code-owned and already deferred to the non-spec loop — ALTERNATIVES: filing Edit 2h "One executor advances a given job at a time" under the failover lens (rejected; review-log line 558 already judged it a correct requirement whose shortfall is code, and concurrent executors stay bounded because the phase steps are idempotent).

### [spec.13.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in round 13. BECAUSE the round-13 spec delta (Edit 2b item (1) now cites the fence instead of fixing a code, Edit 2c deletes the "session creation gate upstream" sentence) adds no retry, resume, lease, or drain path. A re-walk of SPEC-1 to SPEC-4 under crash, restart, and Postgres failover found every remaining gap's remedy to be code. ALTERNATIVES: filing Edit 2d ("A drain failure fails the job") against CODE-7 item 4 (a budget-interrupted drain stays in `initiated`). Rejected because Edit 2h's `initiated` resume rule covers an interrupted drain the same way it covers a crash, so the two do not contradict each other.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, "One erasure executor" and CODE-7 item 5]: the claim that the advisory-locked tick gives one executor is false under Postgres primary failover. The lock is session-level on one pooled connection (cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:227-243). `reconcileOnce` never re-checks the lock, and its other pool connections reconnect to the new primary. A second replica can then take the lock there while the first replica's tick is still running `RunPending`. `Runner.advance` and `Runner.fail` are blind read-modify-write `Update`s with no expected-phase or status guard (pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:164-202). Two executors can therefore each persist a different `ReceiptAt`, which breaks Edit 2e's "same content", and a stale `fail` or `advance` can overwrite a `completed` job. What is true instead: Edit 2h's sentence "One executor advances a given job at a time" needs a fence in code. Each runner `Update` should be conditional on the phase that executor read, and a mismatch should make that executor drop the job. Round 1 judged the consequence bounded, so this is filed for the non-spec loop rather than as a spec defect.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-3 item 3 / CODE-7 item 4]: the erasure drain runs on the per-tick budget context. `ForceTerminate` calls `onTerminal(ctx, ...)` on that same context after the terminal row commits (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:88-93). If the budget expires mid-pipeline, the remaining steps (workspace seal, executor or pod release, audit, and billing) are cut short. The next drain does not run them again, because the row is terminal and returns ErrSuperseded. What is true instead: the drain should run each `ForceTerminate`, or at least `onTerminal`, on `context.WithoutCancel` with its own bound, as CODE-4 item 3 already does for suspension (`dctx := context.WithoutCancel(req.Context())`).
USEFUL [spec.1.review-reliability.1]: its advisory-lock FACT (tenantdeletion.go:226-243) and its ErrSuperseded FACT located both deferrals above without re-deriving them.

### [spec.13.review-security.1]
DECISION: no spec finding under the security lens in round 13 — BECAUSE the round-12 rewrite of Edits 2b and 2c removes no control: the owner the fence reads is the session row's `user_id`, which §11 (session record field table, `user_id`: "User who initiated the session (or parent session owner for delegations)") defines as the initiator, so "refuses new sessions owned by the affected `user_id`" covers what the old "all session creation endpoints ... for the affected `user_id`" covered; the markers are Postgres columns, read errors fail closed, the hold re-check fails the job, and acknowledgment gates (override on duplicate erase, no override on retry) fail closed — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Edit 2c deletion as removal of a mandatory upstream gate (rejected; Edit 1a keeps refusal at insert and allows an earlier check, the Creation-path table gates C1-C4 before the pod claim, and a spec that permits a late-only check is less strict rather than wrong).
USEFUL [spec.12 DEFERRED on the E5 clear-vs-retry race, review-log line ~708]: saved re-deriving it. The spec-side alternative (drop the clear endpoint's exemption in Edit 2f) is available but not required, because Edit 4i states the failed-job precondition and the code remedy (re-read the job status under the users-row lock in `ClearProcessingRestriction`) satisfies it.
USEFUL [spec.?.review-security on derive-owner bypass, review-log line ~608]: confirmed again; `handleDerive` has no source-owner check and `DeriveRequest.UserID` overrides the owner (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:46-48, 310-312). Neither is spec-defined, so both remedies are code.
OPEN: deriving or replaying a session whose SOURCE owner is under erasure into a session owned by another user (spec-legal for a caller authorized on the source, such as a tenant-admin) copies the restricted user's workspace into a session no eraser of that user reaches. The fence keys on the new owner only, and the current Article 18 text has the same scope, so this is pre-existing rather than a regression. A human or P5b should decide whether Article 18 also fences derive/replay by the source owner. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:310-312; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:807.
WATCHOUT: Edit 1a makes the early check optional ("An endpoint may run the same check earlier, before it claims a pod"). On C3 (`POST /v1/sessions/start`) the work before the insert includes the pod claim, the credential lease, and the runtime launch, so a spec-only implementation could launch a suspended tenant's or restricted user's runtime before refusing. The staged code gates C1-C4 early, so the tree is fine; do not file this as a spec defect unless a later round removes those gates. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Creation-path table row C3.

DECISION: the section preamble said the open-decisions phase "supplies the recommendations", which only OD4 carries; it now states that OD4 has a recommendation and OD1 to OD3 state the staged default.
FACT: the three out-of-scope markers of this firing (residency routing, REC-1 item 13; the `ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED` catalog row, REC-1 item 14; `CountActiveDelegatedChildrenByUser`) are already entries of **Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage**.
WATCHOUT: the first Defects entry and D20 describe P5b's membership and ordering after 0093, and **Impacts on other proposals** has a row for 0093 only. They agree (P5b lands after 0093). P5b is a plan entry with no proposal directory, so no row was added.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: erasure-job exclusivity rests on a guarded record write rather than the tick's advisory lock: every runner `Update` goes through one helper whose mutate returns `erasurejob.ErrJobMoved` when the stored `Phase` or `len(PhaseLog)` differs from the runner's last read or write; `ReceiptAt` is set inside a guarded mutate only when zero and the receipt is built from the returned job (CODE-7 items 1 and 3, CODE-6 item 1 `Update` comment, Edit 2h, D16, T7.6, T8.11) — BECAUSE the tick takes a session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` on one pooled connection, which the required transaction-mode pooler and a failover do not preserve, and `advance`/`fail` were blind writes — ALTERNATIVES: xact advisory lock or `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claim held for the tick (a lock lost mid-run leaves a blind writer); lease columns (new state and clock skew for no added guarantee); a `Phase`-only guard (ABA hole, below).
WATCHOUT: a guard comparing `Phase` alone admits a runner that read `initiated` before a `fail` and a `Restart`, which then skips the retry's no-override hold re-check (row E6). `len(PhaseLog)` closes it only because `Restart` appends `initiated` and nothing removes entries; a later change that truncates or rewrites `PhaseLog` reopens it. T8.11's Restart case is the discriminating assertion. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go `advance`, `fail`; staged CODE-6 item 1 `Restart` comment.
FACT: `Memory.Update` holds `m.mu` across mutate and returns the mutate error without writing, so the sentinel needs no interface change; no other type implements `erasurejob.Store.Update` in pkg, cmd, or tests (the `tenantdeletion` package has its own unrelated `Update`). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/erasurejob.go `Memory.Update`; cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `tick` (`SELECT pg_try_advisory_lock($1)` on `rn.pool.Acquire`); spec/12_storage-architecture.md PgBouncer "Pool mode: Transaction mode"; spec/10_gateway-internals.md fallback sentence "not connection-scoped like advisory locks".
OPEN: the session-level advisory lock in `tenantDeletionRunner.tick` (and the same pattern in `erasurejob/saltlockpg` `WithSaltLock`) can leak on one backend under transaction pooling, so ticks run only when their lock statement lands on that backend. That is a liveness risk for tenant deletion and, through D16, the erasure SLA. It was adjudicated a separate finding and left unedited here; a later round or REC-1 should file it for BUILD-GAPS.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]
FACT: `tenantdeletion.ReconcileTenant` advances whenever `runPhase` returns nil; only `ErrBlockedByLegalHold` and `ErrEscrowRegionUnresolvable` become pauses, and both pause helpers (`block`, `pauseEscrowUnresolvable`) write `State = TenantDeleting`. `syncBack` copies `job.State` onto the tenant row, and `ensureJob` rebuilds a `deleting` tenant at `PhaseRevokeCredentials`, a `disabling` one at `PhaseSoftDisable`. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go `ReconcileTenant`, `block`, `pauseEscrowUnresolvable`; lifecycle.go `stateForPhase`; cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `syncBack`, `ensureJob`.
DECISION: CODE-5 item 4 now wraps a drain error in unexported `errSessionsNotDrained`, matched in `ReconcileTenant`, and a new `pauseDrain` writes only `BlockedReason` and `UpdatedAt` — BECAUSE a nil return advances to Phase 3 with live sessions and a `pauseEscrowUnresolvable` copy persists `deleting`, so a restart skips the drain — ALTERNATIVES: matching `admin.ErrDrainIncomplete` in the controller (pkg/controller would import the admin package and miss listing and budget errors); `r.fail` + `Retry` (failed jobs are terminal, D11).
WATCHOUT: any future Phase 2 pause must leave `State` at `disabling`. T5.1's `State` assertion and T5.3 (syncBack + fresh job store) are the discriminating tests. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `ensureJob` `if t.State == tenantstore.TenantStateDeleting`.
WATCHOUT: do not put the tick budget on `ReconcileAll` or the whole tick — an expired context inside Phase 3.5 or 4 reaches `r.fail` and fails another tenant terminally. Only the Phase 2 drain is bounded, because only its error is a pause.
DECISION: the budget flag is renamed `--lifecycle-tick-budget` (`LENNY_LIFECYCLE_TICK_BUDGET`) and its home moved to CODE-5 item 1, because S11 (CODE-5) lands before S13 (CODE-7) and the flag now bounds tenant deletion too; CODE-7 item 4 cites it. Edge case 1 is a pointer, worded "at most one budget per drain" rather than the design's "at most the tick budget", because several tenants in Phase 2 each take one budget in the same tick.
DECISION: `DrainSessions` (CODE-3 item 3) checks `ctx.Err()` between sessions and calls `ForceTerminate` on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` — BECAUSE `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate` commits the terminal row and then runs `onTerminal(ctx, ...)`; a cut pipeline is never repeated, because a re-drain gets `ErrSuperseded` on the terminal row. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go `ForceTerminate`. The test case went in T3.1 (the drain's table), not T8.10.
OPEN: `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate` itself runs `onTerminal` on its caller's context, so the operator §24.11 force-terminate handler can still cut the pipeline when the request is cancelled. Out of this group; a later round or P2 decides whether the adapter detaches.
DECISION: CODE-7 item 5 removes the `Receipts` gate around the runner launch in `adminrouter.go`; `reconcileOnce` skips the deletion half without `Receipts` and the erasure half without a runner. New T8.12 (tier 1, cmd) drives `reconcileOnce` for the no-audit case, the ordering, and the blocked-Phase-2 starvation case. T8.11 belongs to G1's guard test. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go `if deletionReconciler.Receipts != nil { runner := &tenantDeletionRunner{...} }`.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: `ErasedAt` is set by the `Update` recording the last step's success (passing `Verify` in `verifying`; the exempt outcome in `pseudonymizing`; end of `store_deleting` with no `BillingEraser`); CODE-7 item 2 is its one home, CODE-6 item 2 is a bare pointer, and T8.1 gains the discriminating case (a `verifying` resume with `ErasedAt` zero calls `Verify` once) — BECAUSE "the Update that records the final billing outcome" was the advance to `verifying`, so a resumed `verifying` job skipped the §12.8 salt-deletion re-check (spec/12 **Erasure job phase field (crash recovery)**) — ALTERNATIVES: a separate verified flag (duplicates `ErasedAt` as skip marker); re-running `Verify` when `ErasedAt` is set (breaks item 3's byte-identical receipt retry).
DECISION: `Run` observes `lenny_erasure_job_duration_seconds` only after a terminal write that applied (`Complete` nil, or `fail` whose guarded `Update` applied); `lenny_erasure_jobs_active` moves to `Sampler.Sample` via new `AgeSink.SetErasureJobsActive(n)` from the `ListActive` count; `LifecycleMetrics` keeps only `ObserveErasureJobDuration`; Inc/Dec are deleted from gatewaymetrics at CODE-7 (not CODE-6, which would break the build between S12 and S13) — BECAUSE under `RunPending` the per-call bracket observed partial ages of unfinished jobs and read 0 for queued or budget-interrupted ones — ALTERNATIVES: terminal-only Dec with Inc per entry (gauge drifts +1 per interrupted tick); setting the gauge in `RunPending` (only the lock holder runs it, others go stale).
FACT: the tree's gauge help text says "currently in progress on this replica" (pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_erasure.go, `lenny_erasure_jobs_active` GaugeOpts). The sampler-set value is the cluster-wide count on every replica, so CODE-6 Targets now names that file for the help-text change; the design's sweep missed it. No docs, chart, or alert reads either metric (grep over docs/, charts/, pkg/alerting).
WATCHOUT: every replica publishes the same `lenny_erasure_jobs_active`, as for the age gauge; aggregate with `max`, not `sum`. A lock-loss race through `Complete` can record two duration observations for one job; accepted as metric imprecision (G1 rejected a guard in `Complete`). EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go `erasureSampler.Run`.
FACT: test fakes to retarget with the interface change: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/sla_test.go `fakeLifecycle` (inc/dec) and `fakeAgeSink` (needs `SetErasureJobsActive`), pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_erasure_test.go (Inc/Dec calls).

FACT: the remote-replica pod release is level-triggered on the session row. `Sweeper.Sweep` lists every tenant's rows with `ListFilter{}` from the read pool, and only `sweepTerminal` calls `ReleaseTerminalBinding`, for a listed terminal row that `boundHere` reports. WATCHOUT: do not extend the orphan-GC outcome to tenant suspension or tenant deletion. Suspension keeps its rows and deletion's Phase 4 runs at least two ticks after Phase 2, so the Sweeper outcome holds there and X4's suspension assertion is correct as written. — EVIDENCE: non-spec X4; SPEC-3 phase sequence
WATCHOUT: T3.1's filter assertion is per recorded listing, never "both listings": the first-listing-error case and the context-ended case make no verifying listing. T3.2 stays the only store-side `Primary` pin; T8.13 pins `HeldResourcesForUser` and the erasure lister's argument. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md T3.1, T3.2, T8.13

MIG-2's policy now copies 0158's form, and T6.2 asserts the unset-GUC error and the `__all__` read, since no global policy-form sweep exists. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.2 **Multi-tenancy** and **`platform-admin` cross-tenant database access**; migrations/0158_session_usage.up.sql CREATE POLICY.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G6.1]
DECISION: the operator clear (row E5) moves into the erasurejob store as `Store.ClearRestriction(ctx, id)`, which locks the job row `FOR UPDATE`, returns `ErrNotFailed` unless `failed`, then calls the userstore pgstore's tx-scoped `ClearProcessingRestrictionTx` in the same transaction; `ClearProcessingRestriction` leaves `userstore.Store` — BECAUSE the handler's `failed` check was a separate `erasureJobs.Get` outside any lock, so a `Restart` of the same job committing between the check and the clear left an `initiated` job with its flag emptied (fence open through drain and erase); locking the job row first matches the job-row-then-users-row order of Start, Restart, and Complete, and gives `erasurejob.Memory` true parity under its mutex — ALTERNATIVES: a status read inside userstore after the users-row lock (correct on Postgres only; userstore.Memory cannot see jobs, so the handler pre-check would remain a second decider); dropping the trigger bypass (spec Edit 2f exempts the clear; trigger tests any in-progress job, not this job); a compensating re-set after the clear (window stays open); `FOR SHARE` on the job row after the users lock (reverse lock order, deadlock risk).
FACT: tree evidence for the race — pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go `handleClearErasureRestriction` calls `r.erasureJobs.Get` then `r.users.ClearProcessingRestriction` as separate calls; pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go `ClearProcessingRestriction` sets `SET LOCAL lenny.clear_processing_restriction` before `FOR UPDATE`; migrations/0042 trigger returns NEW on that GUC before its in-progress check. Callers of `ClearProcessingRestriction`: only erasure.go; implementers: userstore pgstore, userstore.Memory, admin custom_roles_test.go `fakeUserLister` (extra method stays compilable after removal from the interface); erasure_retry_test.go `TestClearProcessingRestrictionMemoryStore_spec_12_8_764` is covered by the existing "Retarget erasure_test.go and erasure_retry_test.go" line.
FACT: retry and clear-processing-restriction are wrapped in `requireAdmin` (403 FORBIDDEN for a non-platform-admin) and GET in `requireUserAdmin` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/routes.go `registerErasureJobRoutes`; tenants.go `requireAdmin`. X5 now asserts 404 on get and 403 on retry and clear.
DECISION: T8.14 (tier 3) extends tests/tier3_contract/admin_conflict_codes over the 409 cells of rows E2, E3, and E5 "whose code the §15.1 Error code catalog enumerates"; T4.6 (tier 3) is a new tests/tier3_contract/admin_tenant_suspend suite; S10 and S13 gain tier 3 — BECAUSE test-coverage.md maps an HTTP response change to tier 3 and the existing conflict-code suite enumerates emit sites one by one. The catalog predicate self-adjusts: `ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED` is out while it has no catalog row (REC-1 item 14) and in if one is added.
WATCHOUT: the design for T8.14 carried a sentence "`ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED` joins only if OD2 adds its catalog row"; it was dropped because staged files may not reference an open decision, and the catalog predicate already carries the same effect. Do not re-add an OD reference there. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md T8.14.
WATCHOUT: T8.5 now names its cases only; every answer lives in rows E1, E2, E3, and E5. A later round that adds a T8.5 case adds a case name, never a code or event.
WATCHOUT: the clear/retry race cases live in T7.2 (tier 2, real Postgres) and reach Memory through T7.4's pointer to T7.1–T7.3; do not add them to T8.5.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G7.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 7's `WithHoldCheck` bullet now points to row E6 for the override exemption and the failure outcome, and keeps only the hook signature, the `Hold` fields, and the doc-comment instruction — BECAUSE row E6 of the Erasure entry-point table is the only rule site, and the table intro already says CODE-7 cites it — ALTERNATIVES: keeping both copies with a "must match E6" note (rejected; it keeps the duplicate the intro says does not exist).
FACT: the `admin.SessionAdmin` test doubles are `fakeSessionAdmin` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/sessions_test.go and `sessionAdminStub` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/routestub_test.go. cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin_test.go has no double and calls the real `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate`. No pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/routestub_test.go exists. EVIDENCE: openapi/routestub_test.go `sessionAdminStub`; `grep -rln 'func (.*) ForceTerminate' pkg cmd`.
DECISION: the checklist lands CODE-3 at S7, before CODE-1 at S8 and CODE-2 at S9. S8 depends on S7, S9 on S8, S10 and S11 on S7, S13 on S7 and S8 in place of S7 and S9, and S16 on S9 in place of S8 — BECAUSE T1.5 (with CODE-1) needs `admin.DrainSessions` (CODE-3), and CODE-3 item 5 forbids a test-local drain — ALTERNATIVES: adding S9 to the old S7's Depends-on (rejected; the checklist's only forward edge, and implement-proposal runs the steps in order); moving T1.5 to TEST-1 (rejected; it pins CODE-1's fence).
WATCHOUT: no proposal file outside the checklist cites S7, S8, or S9. MIG-2 Targets names only S5 and S6. A later reorder only needs to recheck the checklist's own Depends-on lines. EVIDENCE: grep '\bS[0-9]{1,2}\b' over the proposal files.
OPEN: CODE-1 item 2 Refusal rule 3 emits `details.tenantId` for `TENANT_SUSPENDED`, but the §15.1 catalog row (spec/15_external-api-surface.md, Error code catalog, `TENANT_SUSPENDED`) defines no details, and no SPEC-4 edit adds any. DOCS-1 no longer documents the detail. A later round decides whether Edit 4b also gives `TENANT_SUSPENDED` a `tenantId` detail, which the tree's quota.go `requireTenantState` emits today, or whether rule 3 drops it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the single-executor finding is closed by a guarded job write, not by a better lock. Every runner `Update` (advance, count and billing writes, `fail`, the `ReceiptAt` write) goes through one helper whose mutate returns a new sentinel `erasurejob.ErrJobMoved` when the stored `Phase` or `len(PhaseLog)` differs from the job the runner last read or wrote; the runner then drops the job for the tick and calls neither `fail` nor the failure observer. `ReceiptAt` is set inside that mutate only when zero and the receipt is built from the returned job. The rule's spec home is Edit 2h (replacing "One executor advances a given job at a time."); the code token lives in CODE-7 item 1; the store contract (mutate runs on the row read FOR UPDATE in the write's transaction, a mutate error writes nothing) lives in the CODE-6 item 1 `Update` comment. No interface change: `Store.Update` already aborts on a mutate error, and the only implementations are `Memory` (mutex held across mutate) and the new CODE-6 pgstore — BECAUSE any lock (session-level, transaction-scoped, or a `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` claim) can be lost mid-run on failover or an idle-in-transaction timeout while the executor keeps going, so correctness has to sit on the record write regardless; and §12.8 crash recovery already requires every phase step to be safely re-runnable, so duplicate step execution by two executors is the case the spec already covers once only one executor's record writes land — ALTERNATIVES: `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` in a transaction held for the tick (rejected as the guarantee; pins a backend for up to the 10-minute tick budget and still evaporates on failover); a per-job lease column (`ClaimedBy`/`ClaimedUntil`) (rejected; new field plus expiry tuning for a guarantee the guarded write gives with no new state); running `RunPending` on every replica outside the lock (rejected; N-fold eraser and dead-letter redaction work and duplicate per-row audit events in the common case where the lock does hold); phase-only guard as the reviewer suggested (rejected; ABA, see WATCHOUT).
WATCHOUT: a guard on `Phase` alone admits a stale executor across `fail` then `Restart`: it read `initiated` for attempt 1, the job failed and was retried to `initiated`, and its `initiated -> store_deleting` write passes, skipping the retry's no-override hold re-check (row E6). `len(PhaseLog)` is the discriminator because `PhaseLog` is append-only and `Restart` appends `initiated` (CODE-6 item 1). T8.11's Restart case is the assertion that discriminates. EVIDENCE: proposal CODE-6 item 1 `Restart` comment; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go `advance` and `fail`.
FACT: the only `erasurejob.Store` implementation in the tree is `Memory`; no test double implements `Update(ctx, id, mutate func(*Job) error)`. EVIDENCE: grep over pkg cmd tests; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/erasurejob.go `Memory.Update` holds `m.mu` across mutate.
FACT: under PgBouncer transaction mode the tick's lock is worse than non-exclusive: `pg_try_advisory_lock` takes the lock on whatever backend serves that statement, the deferred `pg_advisory_unlock` usually lands on a different backend and returns false, and transaction mode runs no `DISCARD ALL` by default, so the lock stays held by one backend. Later ticks then succeed only when their statement lands on that backend (reentrantly), from any replica. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `tick`; spec/12_storage-architecture.md **Connection pooling** "Pool mode: Transaction mode". The same pattern is in pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go `WithSaltLock` (session-level `pg_advisory_lock`/`pg_advisory_unlock` on one acquired conn).
OPEN: liveness, separate from this group's finding. Because of the leaked lock above, tenant-deletion ticks and (since D16 moves erasure into the tick) erasure jobs run only on ticks whose lock statement lands on the leaking backend, so an erasure can stall past the T4 1-hour SLA under the required pooler. The guarded write makes concurrent execution safe, so the cheapest remedy a later round should weigh is taking the lock with `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` inside a short transaction per tick (or dropping it for erasure and accepting duplicate work). The tick lock and `saltlockpg` are pre-existing tree defects; file for BUILD-GAPS through REC-1 if the next round confirms.
DECISION: the **One erasure executor** paragraph is deleted rather than reworded — BECAUSE its two true halves (runs only in the tick; no handler goroutine) are already stated by CODE-7 items 4 to 7, and its heading asserts the guarantee the fix withdraws; nothing in the proposal cites it by name.
DECISION: Complete is not given its own guard — BECAUSE after `ErasedAt` is set no path fails the job (a receipt error and a budget expiry both leave it in progress), `Restart` requires `failed`, and `Complete` on an already-completed job rewrites the same `CompletedAt` and clears 0 users rows. The guarded `ReceiptAt` write refuses an executor that arrives after `Complete`; one that passed it before `Complete` writes the identical second receipt Edit 2e already specifies.
USEFUL [spec.13 DEFERRED on "One erasure executor" (review-log line ~792)]: it located the blind `advance`/`fail` Updates and the failover path without re-deriving them.
CORRECTS [spec.1 DEFERRED (review-log line ~165) and the carried OPEN (line ~826)]: their suggested remedy "a transaction-scoped lock or a FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED claim" does not on its own give one executor, because the holder keeps running after the lock is lost; the guarded write is the remedy, and the lock question is liveness only.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
FACT: `ReconcileTenant` advances the phase whenever `runPhase` returns nil and fails the job on any error other than `ErrBlockedByLegalHold` and `ErrEscrowRegionUnresolvable`; both existing pauses (`block`, `pauseEscrowUnresolvable`) write `State = TenantDeleting`. `syncBack` copies `job.State` onto the tenant row, and `ensureJob` rebuilds a `deleting` tenant at `PhaseRevokeCredentials`, a `disabling` one at `PhaseSoftDisable`. So a Phase 2 pause must be matched in `ReconcileTenant` by sentinel and must not write `State`. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go `ReconcileTenant`, `block`, `pauseEscrowUnresolvable`; cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `ensureJob`, `syncBack`.
DECISION: Phase 2 pause = unexported sentinel `errSessionsNotDrained` wrapped by `runPhase`, matched in `ReconcileTenant`, handled by a new `pauseDrain` that writes `BlockedReason` and `UpdatedAt` only — BECAUSE it reuses the existing pause dispatch and the existing advance-clears-`BlockedReason` line — ALTERNATIVES: copying `pauseEscrowUnresolvable` (writes `deleting`, restart skips the drain); returning nil from `runPhase` (advances to Phase 3); importing `admin.ErrDrainIncomplete` into the controller (layering, and it misses a context error).
FACT: the runner tick runs on `context.Background()` (`go runner.Start(context.Background())`), and `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate` runs `onTerminal` synchronously on its caller's context after the terminal row commits; a row already terminal returns `ErrSuperseded`, so no later drain repeats a cut pipeline. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go runner launch; cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go `ForceTerminate`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go `AfterTerminalWrite`.
DECISION: one change closes the budget findings: `DrainSessions` checks `ctx.Err()` between sessions and calls each `ForceTerminate` on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` (CODE-3 item 3), and the Phase 2 adapter runs the drain under the tick budget (CODE-5 item 1). The detach is what makes bounding the Phase 2 drain safe — ALTERNATIVES: a budget over all of `ReconcileAll` (rejected; a context expiry in Phase 4 or 3.5 reaches `r.fail` and fails another tenant terminally); `RunPending` before `ReconcileAll` (rejected; a long drain still delays the next tick, so ordering alone bounds nothing); a separate erasure loop with its own advisory lock (rejected; contradicts D16); detaching inside the adapter's `ForceTerminate` (rejected here; it also changes the operator force-terminate handler, see OPEN below).
DECISION: the flag is renamed `--lifecycle-tick-budget` and its home moves from CODE-7 item 4 to CODE-5 item 1 — BECAUSE checklist S11 (CODE-5) lands before S13 (CODE-7), and a flag named for erasure that bounds a tenant-deletion drain misleads operators; the flag does not exist yet, so the rename costs nothing.
DECISION: the runner-start rule becomes "launch `Start` without the `Receipts` gate; `reconcileOnce` skips each half whose seam is absent", so T8.11 pins it through `reconcileOnce` with no extracted predicate — BECAUSE the deletion reconciler is always constructed in buildAdminRouter, so "whenever either is wired" was a predicate with no false case worth testing.
WATCHOUT: a budget expiry overruns by at most one session's terminal pipeline (the seal step can retry for up to `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`), because the session in flight is detached. Do not "fix" this by cancelling `onTerminal`; that is the defect finding 14 named. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go `AfterTerminalWrite`.
OPEN: `handleForceTerminateSession` passes the request context to `ForceTerminate`, so a client disconnect mid-pipeline cuts `onTerminal` the same way (claim or slot left counted, audit and billing events lost). Pre-existing and outside this group; a later round should decide whether the adapter detaches `onTerminal` itself. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go `ForceTerminate`; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/sessions.go `handleForceTerminateSession`.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: `ErasedAt` is set in the `Update` that records the last step's success (a passing `Verify`; the exempt outcome in `pseudonymizing`; the end of `store_deleting` with no `BillingEraser`), stated once in CODE-7 item 2; CODE-6 item 2 becomes a bare pointer; T8.1 gains the discriminating case (a `verifying` resume with `ErasedAt` zero calls `Verify` once before the receipt) — BECAUSE "the Update that records the final billing outcome" read as the advance to `verifying`, so every persisted `verifying` job had `ErasedAt` set and skipped the §12.8 salt-deletion re-check — ALTERNATIVES: a separate "verified" flag (rejected; `Billing.Verified` already exists and `ErasedAt` is the one skip marker); re-running `Verify` even when `ErasedAt` is set (rejected; contradicts the receipt-retry contract that re-runs no eraser).
DECISION: the duration histogram is observed only when `Run` moves a job to `completed` (after `Complete` returns nil) or to `failed` (in `fail`); the `lenny_erasure_jobs_active` gauge leaves `Run` and is set by `Sampler.Sample` to the count `ListActive` returns — BECAUSE under `RunPending` the per-call Inc/Dec bracket reads 0 for a job queued behind another or interrupted by the budget, so it stops tracking in-progress jobs (§12.8 **Erasure SLA enforcement**) — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "decrement only on completed or failed" (rejected; Inc still fires on every re-entry, so the gauge drifts upward by one per interrupted tick); keeping the bracket as an "executing now" gauge (rejected; at most 1 per replica under sequential RunPending, contradicts the spec's in-progress meaning); setting the gauge in `RunPending` (rejected; only the lock holder runs it, and a replica that loses the lock keeps a stale value).
WATCHOUT: every replica runs the sampler, so each publishes the same `lenny_erasure_jobs_active` value, as it already does for `lenny_erasure_job_age_seconds`; `max` across pods is correct and `sum` overcounts. No alert or dashboard reads the gauge today. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go `erasureSampler.Run`; grep of pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go finds only the age gauge.
FACT: the tree's Run bracket (`Run` in pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go, the `r.lifecycle != nil` block) observes `job.StartedAt`-relative wall clock, so a per-tick observation of an unfinished job is its partial age, not a step duration. Callers of `LifecycleMetrics` Inc/Dec: runner.go, sla_test.go `fakeLifecycle`, gatewaymetrics_erasure_test.go.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
FACT: the only path that releases a remote replica's pod binding for a terminal session is the coordination Sweeper's level-triggered listing: `Sweep` lists each tenant's rows with `ListFilter{}` (read pool) and `sweepTerminal` calls `ReleaseTerminalBinding` only for a listed terminal row bound here; `evictBinding` is called only from `sweepRow`. A deleted row is never listed, so its binding is never released or evicted. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go `Sweep`, `sweepRow`, `sweepTerminal`, `NewSweeper` (interval default 15s); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go `ReleaseTerminalBinding` doc comment; callers are coordination.go, resume_driver.go, and cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go `terminalReleaserSeam` only.
FACT: user erasure deletes session rows at step 17 (`erasure.FromCounting("sessions", w.sessions)`, cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go) through pgstore `DeleteByUser` (`DELETE FROM sessions WHERE tenant_id = $1 AND user_id = $2`), in the same runner pass as the drain under CODE-7 item 1. The orphan backstop is spec/04_system-components.md orphan predicate 1 (`claimOrphanTimeout`, default 5 minutes, drains the pod, skipped while another active session references the pod).
DECISION: correct the premise rather than add a quiesce. The staged SPEC-1 *Drain* sentence stops naming who releases the pod and states only that a successful drain does not wait for terminal steps or pod release; REC-1 item 6 becomes the one home of the two release outcomes; CODE-3 item 4, D9, OD1, and spec-changes edge case 5 point at it; X4 gains an erasure variant asserting the claim leaves `bound` within `claimOrphanTimeout` plus one orphan-GC pass — BECAUSE D9 already decided no quiesce mechanism under P2 ownership, and the owner's standing preference is that residual gaps become bounded departures — ALTERNATIVES: hold the job in `initiated` for one Sweeper interval after the drain (rejected: the Sweeper reads a replica with lag and its interval is per-replica configurable, so a fixed wait does not guarantee the listing, and it is the quiesce D9 rejected); have the drain verify release through `pod_assignment` (rejected: a new quiesce mechanism, P2's terminal pipeline); keep the spec naming the holder's release and add the orphan-GC case to it (rejected: spec text describing a defect's mechanism rather than the drain's guarantee).
WATCHOUT: tenant suspension keeps its rows and tenant deletion runs Phase 4 at least two ticks after Phase 2, so the Sweeper outcome stays true for them and X4's suspension assertion stays as written. Only erasure has the orphan-GC outcome. Do not extend the orphan-GC wording to suspension or deletion.
UNVERIFIED: whether the credential leases of a never-released remote binding are returned by any other path (lease TTL or orphan drain). `ReleaseTerminalBinding` states release "returns the binding's credential leases". P2 should check when it takes REC-1 item 6.
DECISION (finding 19): pin the callers' filter with recording fakes at tier 1 (one clause on T3.1, new T8.11), and leave T3.2 as the store-side pin — BECAUSE the caller argument and the store's honoring of it are separate facts and together cover the replica-lag miss — ALTERNATIVES: a tier-2 split-pool run of `HeldResourcesForUser` and the erasure lister (rejected: duplicates T3.2 through a larger fixture without discriminating anything the fake does not).
WATCHOUT: the erasure `Sessions` lister is an inline closure in cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go (`Sessions: func(ctx, tenantID, userID string)`), so T8.11 forces the implementor to make it reachable from a test.

DECISION: the ownerless reservation rule (Reserve records nothing for an empty owner; CreateReserved of a row with no `UserID` inserts as `Create` does) moves into the Creation-path table's **Reservation** column rule, and CODE-1 item 7 keeps only the mechanism (the `DELETE ... RETURNING`, the fence-then-insert order, the refusal value, and that an empty owner reads only the tenant row) — BECAUSE the table declares itself the one home and the empty-owner branch lived only in CODE-1 — ALTERNATIVES: re-synchronising both sites (rejected; the next edit to either drifts again); dropping the ownerless branch and refusing an ownerless reserved insert (rejected; derive.go resolves an empty owner when req.UserID and source.UserID are both empty, and Edit 1a refuses only an ended reservation).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
DECISION: the operator clear (row E5) moves into the erasurejob store as `Store.ClearRestriction(ctx, id)`, which selects the job row `FOR UPDATE`, returns `ErrNotFailed` unless `failed`, and then calls the userstore pgstore's transaction-scoped `ClearProcessingRestrictionTx` (bypass, other-job refusal, shared helper) in the same transaction; `ClearProcessingRestriction` leaves `userstore.Store`, and the handler stops deciding the phase from its own `Get` — BECAUSE Start, Restart, and Complete already lock the job row and then the users row in one erasurejob-store transaction, so the clear joins the same lock order and serializes with a concurrent `Restart` with no deadlock, and `erasurejob.Memory` can check the phase under its own mutex (parity) — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's status read inside `userstore` `ClearProcessingRestriction` after the users `FOR UPDATE` (correct under READ COMMITTED, but `userstore.Memory` has no job view, so the Memory path would still need the handler pre-check: two deciders); dropping the trigger bypass so migration 0042 refuses the clear (Edit 2f states the exemption, and the trigger checks any in-progress job rather than this job's phase); a compensating re-set of the flag after the clear (hair).
FACT: `pgtenant.InTx` opens a default (READ COMMITTED) transaction, so a `FOR UPDATE` that waits re-reads the latest committed row; the serialization argument for the job-row lock depends on that. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go `InTx` (`pool.Begin` with no TxOptions).
FACT: the only production caller of `ClearProcessingRestriction` is `handleClearErasureRestriction`; test callers are `pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure_retry_test.go` `TestClearProcessingRestrictionMemoryStore_spec_12_8_764` and the `fakeUserLister` method in `custom_roles_test.go`. No type in the tree fakes `erasurejob.Store`. EVIDENCE: grep for ClearProcessingRestriction under pkg, cmd, tests.
FACT: retry and clear-processing-restriction are mounted behind `requireAdmin` (403 FORBIDDEN for a non-platform-admin), and `callerMaySeeTenant` returns true for every platform-admin, so the "another tenant's job: 404" cells of rows E3 and E5 are unreachable through the router; only GET (requireUserAdmin) yields the cross-tenant 404. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/routes.go `registerErasureJobRoutes`; erasure.go `callerMaySeeTenant`; tenants.go `requireAdmin`.
WATCHOUT: a tier-1 test that asserts the E3/E5 cross-tenant 404 cell must call the handler past `requireAdmin` or it asserts 403; do not "fix" the rows by deleting the 404 cell without a finding, since the handler branch exists.
WATCHOUT: CODE-6 (S12) changes the userstore clear interface that `handleClearErasureRestriction` (CODE-7, S13) calls, so S12's tier-0 build needs the handler call updated in S12. This predates G6 (the staged `jobID` parameter already broke the call) and is a separate finding for a later round.
DECISION: T8.11 (tier 3) extends `tests/tier3_contract/admin_conflict_codes` only for cataloged 409 codes; `ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED` joins only if OD2 adds the catalog row — BECAUSE that suite asserts every admin 409 code is cataloged, and OD2's staged default leaves the row to REC-1 item 14.

### [non-spec.1.review-fresh.1]
FACT: the erasure retry and clear-processing-restriction routes are wrapped in `requireAdmin` (platform-admin only), so a tenant-admin gets `403 FORBIDDEN` before the handler; the handlers' tenant-visibility `404` is reachable only on GET. X5's "globex tenant-admin receives 404 on retry and clear" cannot pass.
