FACT: `ReconcileTenant` advances the phase whenever `runPhase` returns nil, and treats only the `ErrBlockedByLegalHold` / `ErrEscrowRegionUnresolvable` sentinels as pauses. A Phase 2 in-phase retry must be a sentinel matched in `ReconcileTenant` (not a nil return from runPhase), and it must keep `State = disabling`, because `syncBack` mirrors job.State to the tenant row and `ensureJob` restarts a `deleting` tenant at Phase 3. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:283-301, 346, 370; cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:291-298, 344-350.
FACT: in pgstore, the fence's tenant `SELECT ... FOR SHARE` runs before the INSERT, so `TenantFound == false` IS reachable in pgstore (the FK then rejects the insert afterwards); CODE-1 item 5's doc-comment claim "reachable only in memstore" is inaccurate but changes no outcome. Not filed.
FACT: only five types implement sessionstore.Store's full method set (pgstore, memstore, and the two doubles CODE-1 names, plus slotcounter's narrow fakeSource which is not a Store), so adding Reserve/ReleaseReservation/ReservedSessionIDs/CreateReserved breaks no unlisted implementer. EVIDENCE: grep for `GetActiveSlotsByPod(` implementers.
FACT: no deadlock cycle among Create (FOR SHARE tenant, then user), erasure Start (FK KEY SHARE on tenant, FOR UPDATE user), and suspend (FOR UPDATE tenant). Checked by lock-mode table.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-edit-sites.1]: its RLS-form FACT (0051/0158 hard-error form plus `__all__`) and spec/04:174-176 confirmed MIG-2's policy is off-spec.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]: its WATCHOUT on pauseEscrowUnresolvable writing TenantDeleting led straight to the ensureJob Phase-3 restart consequence.
DECISION: filed the owner-from-body REC-1 gap even though the security lens also filed it — BECAUSE the caller directive asked every lens to settle it and REC-1 items 1-14 still omit it; the fence itself reads the row's owner, so the remedy is a REC-1 item only.

### [non-spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: the remote-replica pod release after a drain is level-triggered on the session ROW: `Sweeper.Sweep` lists each tenant's rows (read pool, `ListFilter{}`) and calls `ReleaseTerminalBinding` only for a listed terminal row it binds; no other path evicts a binding whose row is gone (`stream_failure.go` eviction reads the row too). Default Sweeper interval 15s. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:246-248,398-434,530-535; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:12-19.
FACT: user erasure hard-deletes session rows at step 17 (`erasure.FromCounting("sessions", w.sessions)`, `DELETE FROM sessions WHERE tenant_id=$1 AND user_id=$2`), and the staged runner reaches it in the same `Run` call as the drain. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:556; pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:790-794; non-spec CODE-7 item 1.
WATCHOUT: D9 / CODE-3 item 4 / REC-1 item 6 say the remote replica's Sweeper releases the pod "afterwards" within Sweeper interval + seal. For user erasure that holds only if the Sweeper lists the terminal row before step 17 deletes it; otherwise the claim is reclaimed only by WPC orphan GC predicate 1 (claimOrphanTimeout, by draining the pod) and the remote replica's in-memory binding is never evicted. Tenant deletion is mostly safe because Phase 4 runs at least two 30s passes after Phase 2. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:517.
WATCHOUT: "One executor advances a given job at a time" (Edit 2h) rests on a session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` held on one pooled conn for the tick (cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go tick). A Postgres failover drops that lock while the holder keeps running RunPending (up to the 10-minute budget) on other conns, so a second replica can take the lock. Judged below the bar this round (pre-existing pattern; phases idempotent), but any later design that relies on strict single-executor (e.g. non-idempotent receipt content) must add a phase precondition on each `Update`.
DECISION: no other K8s-idiom finding — BECAUSE the proposal writes no CRD field, adds no finalizer, webhook, or etcd coordination; erasure moved off the request path into the level-triggered tick (ListActive each pass); the suspend drain is pre-existing synchronous gateway work that waits on no reconcile.
USEFUL [spec.11.review-kubernetes.1]: its FACTs on the gateway-hosted tenant-deletion reconciler and Sweeper-based release held, and pointed straight at the row-deletion gap.

### [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: `userstore/pgstore.ClearProcessingRestriction` sets `SET LOCAL lenny.clear_processing_restriction = 'true'` before it locks the row, so the 0042 trigger never checks an in-progress job on the E5 path. Row E5's "job is `failed`" check runs on a separate `erasureJobs.Get` read, and CODE-6 `Restart` re-sets `erasure_job_id` to the SAME job ID. A Restart that commits between the two passes the staged other-job check. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:165-191; migrations/0042_erasure_jobs_processing_restriction_trigger.up.sql guard clause; non-spec CODE-6 item 4, CODE-7 item 10.

A Phase 2 pause that copies that pattern therefore skips the drain after a gateway restart. CODE-5 item 4 says "Follow pauseEscrowUnresolvable".
FACT: the erasure orchestrator lists session IDs once, before every eraser, and the sessions store eraser (step 17) runs last. Deleting reservations inside `sessions.DeleteByUser` therefore never hides a reserved ID from the session-scoped erasers. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasure/erasure.go:95-127; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:560-585.
FACT: the legacy derive copy lands at `{tenant}/workspace/{derivedID}/derived-from-{src}` and the chunked copy lands under `{tenant}/checkpoints/{derivedID}/`. Both sit under prefixes that `miniostore.DeleteBySession(derivedID)` enumerates, so the C8 undo and the erasure reach them. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go parseDeriveURIs, derivedChunkPrefix; pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:749-760.
FACT: `sessionAdminAdapter.ForceTerminate` returns `(locked, state, false, nil)` for an already-terminal row (ErrSuperseded), so a session that ends naturally between the drain's listing and its transition does not land in `Failed`. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:51-95.
USEFUL [spec.13.review-client-surface.1 OPEN owner-from-body] and [line-831 OPEN]: both still apply. REC-1 does not yet file the owner-from-body gap, and this round reports it.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md T1.6/T2.1]: the ownerless `CreateReserved` branch (CODE-1 item 7, added last round) has no test. Its tier-1 or tier-2 case must assert that an ownerless derive inserts.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: the Phase 2 drain runs inside `ReconcileAll` on the runner's `context.Background()` (cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:797, pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:251-262), and CODE-7 item 4's `--erasure-tick-budget` wraps only `RunPending`, which runs after it. Each drained session runs `onTerminal` synchronously, and the seal step alone is bounded only by `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` (300s default, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:115; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:53-83). Non-spec Edge case 1's "The tick budget bounds each tick" is therefore false. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:268, :330, :331, :541.
FACT: the spec itself says advisory locks are connection-scoped and are released silently when PgBouncer recycles a connection (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:29). The tenant-deletion tick takes a session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` through the transaction-mode pooler (cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:227-243; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:41). Quote spec/10:29 when you file the single-executor defect; it is the strongest single piece of evidence.
FACT: `Runner.advance` and `Runner.fail` are blind read-modify-write `Update`s (pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:164-202), and CODE-7 item 3 sets `ReceiptAt` as check-then-act outside the mutate.
FACT: the onTerminal pipeline is synchronous and context-sensitive: seal, then signals, then executor release, then billing and session-log close (terminal_hook.go:53-83, usage.go:331-334). A cancelled context loses those steps, and the next drain skips the row because it is terminal (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go:66-68).
DECISION: reported five findings: the unbounded Phase 2 drain against Edge case 1; single-executor exclusion together with the unconditional runner `Update`s; the budget context cutting `onTerminal`; ErasedAt skipping Verify on a `verifying` resume; and the E5 clear-versus-retry TOCTOU — BECAUSE each was carried as an OPEN to this non-spec loop and each still holds against the current staging — ALTERNATIVES: the unfiltered drain listing (not filed: the shipped erasure lister, suspend drain, and Phase 3.5 hold enumerator already list every tenant row, so it is no worse than shipped, and the summary non-goal rejects the filter); the RunPending head-of-line delay (not filed: Edge case 1 accepts it).
USEFUL [reconcile.2026-10-16.index-checklist-deferred]: its list of OPENs carried into the non-spec loop pointed at four of the five findings.

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: the tenant-deletion runner (and so RunPending under CODE-7 item 5) is started with `context.Background()` (cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go `go runner.Start(context.Background())`), so a graceful shutdown never cancels a phase mid-run; a SIGTERM behaves as a crash and the session-level advisory lock is released on disconnect. Parent-cancel classification in CODE-7 item 4 is therefore not reachable in production. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:796, cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:227-243
FACT: the 0042 trigger's session-local bypass (`lenny.clear_processing_restriction`) skips the in-progress-job check entirely, so the E5 "job is failed" guarantee rests only on the handler's pre-read of the job. EVIDENCE: migrations/0042_erasure_jobs_processing_restriction_trigger.up.sql:83-91; pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:165-194
WATCHOUT: E5 clear and E3 retry of the SAME job race: the handler's failed check (separate read) passes, Restart commits with `erasure_job_id` = the same job ID, and the clear's other-job check then passes and clears under the bypass. The failed check must move inside the clear's transaction after the users-row lock (Restart takes that lock through `SetErasureRestrictionTx`). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:94, :311, :340
WATCHOUT: Runner.Run's lifecycle bracket (runner.go:259-262) observes `lenny_erasure_job_duration_seconds` and decrements the active gauge on EVERY return. Once RunPending re-enters Run each tick (budget expiry, receipt error), the histogram gets a non-completion observation per tick. CODE-7 does not mention the bracket.
FACT: BillingEraser persists and reuses the salt across an interrupted Pseudonymize (TestBillingEraserReusesPersistedSalt), so a budget interrupt inside `pseudonymizing` does not split a user's billing rows across two pseudonyms. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing_test.go:91-112
FACT: session-scoped erasers run before the `sessions` eraser (step 17), which deletes reservations in the same transaction, so a crash-resumed `store_deleting` loses no reserved ID. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:504-583
UNVERIFIED: Restart's "equivalent guarded UPDATE" (CODE-6 item 3) racing a concurrent Start for the same user raises unique_violation (the UPDATE cannot use ON CONFLICT), giving 500 instead of 409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION. Fails safe (nothing written); not filed.

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: the 0042 trigger's `lenny.clear_processing_restriction` bypass exempts the in-progress-job check entirely, so the trigger does not stop an operator clear of an in-progress job; only the staged handler check and `ClearProcessingRestriction`'s own reads can. EVIDENCE: migrations/0042_erasure_jobs_processing_restriction_trigger.up.sql (guard function, `current_setting('lenny.clear_processing_restriction', true) = 'true'` returns NEW before the EXISTS check); pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:165-199.
DECISION: filed the E5 clear-versus-retry TOCTOU (carried OPEN from spec.11.review-security.1) — BECAUSE CODE-7 item 10 checks `failed` from `erasureJobs.Get` outside the users-row lock and CODE-6 item 4 refuses only another job's ID, so a same-job retry committing in between lets the clear empty the flag of an in-progress job; the remedy is an in-transaction job-status re-read after `FOR UPDATE` on the users row, which serializes with `Restart` because `Restart` writes the users row through the same locking helper — ALTERNATIVES: a trigger change (rejected; spec-adjacent migration surface, larger than a store read).
DECISION: filed the owner-from-body REC-1 gap (carried OPEN [owner-from-body]) — BECAUSE §10.2 (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:283) scopes a `user` to its own sessions while create.go:241, create_and_start.go:277, openai_chat.go:242, and open_responses.go:288 take the owner from the request with no principal comparison, and REC-1 items 1-14 and the summary Defects list do not record it. The fence itself reads the same owner the row stores on C1-C4, C8, C9, and C11, so no fence change is needed.
FACT: `requireActiveUser` refuses a restricted or invalidated PRINCIPAL before the owner gate on C1-C4, C8, and C10, so a restricted caller cannot evade the fence by choosing another owner; the residual is an unrestricted principal placing its own data outside its future erasure scope, or creating sessions attributed to someone else. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/user_gate.go:35-65.
USEFUL [spec.9.review-security.1]: its FACTs on principal gating and client-chosen owners saved re-deriving the owner column; verified still true.
OPEN: the carried reliability OPENs (advisory lock under PgBouncer, ForceTerminate on the tick-budget context, `ErasedAt` before `Verify`) were left to their own lenses; none bypasses a security control as staged.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: the lifecycle tick polls `gatewayleader.Elector.IsLeader` (the §12.5 `lenny-gateway-leader` elector built in `startLeaderElectedSweeps`) in place of `pg_try_advisory_lock`; the runner is built in `buildAdminRouter`, handed over on a `gatewayWiringFields` field, and started in `startLeaderElectedSweeps` — BECAUSE the session-level advisory lock leaks on one PgBouncer backend (lock and unlock land on different backends in transaction mode), stalling every replica's tick until server_lifetime, and §12.5 already places gateway singletons under the Lease — ALTERNATIVES: `leaderGate.Add` (see WATCHOUT), `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` held for the tick (pins a backend up to the tick budget, second singleton mechanism), no lock (N-fold eraser work), accept the stall (unbounded, introduced by this proposal).
WATCHOUT: do not register `tenantDeletionRunner.Start` on `leaderGate.Add`. `Gate.Run` hands jobs a leader context client-go cancels on lease loss, so CODE-7 item 4 would `fail` the in-flight erasure job (only budget DeadlineExceeded is a non-failure) and `Reconciler.ReconcileTenant` would fail the in-memory tenant-deletion job on every rolling-deploy handoff. Polling keeps the tick context uncancelled — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go `Gate.Run`, `LeaseElector.Run`.
FACT: the gatewayWiring field set lives in `cmd/lenny-gateway/wiring_fields.go` (`gatewayWiringFields`), not wiring.go; `buildAdminRouter` (main.go) runs before `startLeaderElectedSweeps` (workers.go `startBackgroundWorkers`), and `Gate.Run` runs the elector even with no registered jobs — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/wiring.go:70, cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:218,275, gatewayleader.go `Gate.Run`.
WATCHOUT: T8.6 (tier 4, two replicas) runs under `AlwaysLeader` on compose, so both replicas tick and item 1's `ErrJobMoved` guard is what keeps it correct; the test stays true.
DECISION: the completion-receipt hook fails closed (nil hook = receipt failed, job stays in progress) and is wired to a sink over `policy.AuditAppender.Append` returning its error; the `Receipts` start gate stays — BECAUSE `Router.emit`/`AuditSink.EmitAdminEvent` is fire-and-forget (no error return, `ChainAuditSink` logs append failures, `emit` returns when `r.audit == nil`), so adapting `runErasureToCompletion`'s emit would let `Complete` clear the flag with no receipt; with a fail-closed receipt, removing the Receipts gate bought nothing — ALTERNATIVES: required hook parameter on `NewRunner` (signature churn for the same predicate), error return on `AuditSink` (platform-wide interface change). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenants.go:87,607-610; audit_sink.go:91-96; cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:94-111 (`auditReceiptSink.WriteReceipt`); adminrouter.go:725-727 (Receipts set iff auditAppender != nil).
DECISION: kept "`RunPending`, when the erasure runner is wired" in item 5 although the design deleted that sentence — BECAUSE the erasure runner is built conditionally in `buildAdminRouter` and the existing tenantdeletion_test.go runner literal has none, so an unguarded call panics.
MISTAKE: round 1's item 5 dropped the `Receipts` start gate so erasure could complete without an appender; that path existed only because the receipt hook was not fail-closed.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: the concurrent-executor case is the new tier-7a intent T8.15 under "Tests that land with CODE-7", and S13's tiers gain 7a — BECAUSE the Testing preamble lands each intent at its deliverable's checklist step, so a 7a test of CODE-7's guard has to land at S13 — ALTERNATIVES: appending the case to X1 (rejected: X1 is TEST-1, lands at S16, and covers the creation fence, which would leave S13 listing 7a with no test that lands there); relabelling T8.11 as 7a (rejected: that loses the deterministic tier-1 pin of the Restart ABA case); running against the Postgres store (rejected: the guard lives in the runner helper, and T7.2 and T7.6 already pin Postgres atomicity at tier 2).
WATCHOUT: T8.15 must not assert that every receipt is byte-identical. After a Restart the next attempt writes under a new ReceiptAt, so identity holds only between receipts that share a ReceiptAt. It must also check the flag only at quiescence, because Memory's job store and user store take separate locks and a mid-run read can see them out of step — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md T8.15; CODE-6 item 6 (Memory restriction writer).
FACT: `fail` appends a `failed` PhaseTransition and Restart (row E3) appends `initiated`, so in T8.15 "failure counter increments equal `failed` PhaseLog entries" and "no phase repeats between two `initiated` entries" both hold as invariants — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:184-190 (`fail`); non-spec-changes.md row E3.
OPEN (carried from the design, not fixed here): a same-token `fail` that lands between the receipt and `Complete` leaves a `gdpr.erasure_completed` receipt for an attempt that ends `failed`. T8.11 currently accepts that outcome. A later round should decide whether that is acceptable or whether it needs a compensating action.

### [non-spec.2.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: memstore reservation coverage is closed by one pointer sentence in T1.2 ("Every T1.6 case also runs against `memstore` with `WithCreationMarkers`.") — BECAUSE the gateway memory branch ships `memstore.New()` (cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go, `sessMem := memstore.New()`), so memstore Reserve/CreateReserved/ReservedSessionIDs are production fail-closed code, and T1.6 stays the one statement of the reservation cases — ALTERNATIVES: copying T1.6's case list into T1.2 (rejected; restatement that drifts); widening T1.6's "Against real Postgres" lead (rejected; split tier label); a shared sessionstore conformance suite (rejected; no existing pattern).
FACT: every T1.6 case is meaningful against memstore, including the serialization case: `userstore.Memory.Update` and `tenantstore.Memory.Update` hold `m.mu` across `mutate` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/userstore.go `func (m *Memory) Update` (Lock then mutate under defer Unlock); pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/tenantstore.go `func (m *Memory) Update`.
CORRECTS [OPEN at review-log "T1.2 (memstore parity) has no reservation coverage at all" and "memstore Reserve/CreateReserved/ReservedSessionIDs have no tier-1 test"]: closed; T1.2 now runs every T1.6 case against memstore.
WATCHOUT: do not add an exception clause to the T1.2 pointer (for example excluding the serialization or read-pool case); every T1.6 case holds for memstore, and the read-pool case reduces to returning the reservation. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md T1.2 and T1.6 bullets.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the job-write guard predicate gets ONE code home, a new exported `erasurejob.SameAttempt(stored, read Job) bool` declared in the CODE-6 item 1 Go block (equal Phase, equal len(PhaseLog)), carrying the one-sentence reason for the length term moved from CODE-7 item 1; the `Complete` comment and CODE-7 item 1 call it by name — BECAUSE the predicate was written at two sites and would be implemented three times (runner helper, pgstore Complete, Memory Complete); CODE-6 lands before CODE-7 (S12 before S13), so the store deliverable is the only home both can cite — ALTERNATIVES: home in CODE-7 item 1 with Complete citing it (rejected; the store contract would cite a later deliverable and Complete would still get its own copy in code); defining the predicate as the meaning of `ErrJobMoved` in prose only (rejected; leaves two code implementations, which is the harm the finding names); citing Edit 2h alone (rejected; Edit 2h states the requirement abstractly and does not fix the representation).
DECISION: the Restart doc comment and T7.3 point at row E3 instead of listing the mutation; the comment keeps its two sentinel sentences — BECAUSE row E3 does not name `*ErrActiveJob` or `ErrNotFailed`, so the comment is the only site that binds them. The parenthetical "(the runner's hold re-check reads it)" is dropped with the sentence: E3 says Restart "appends" to PhaseLog and E6 states the condition that reads the `failed` entry.
DECISION: E5's refusals: the CODE-6 item 1 `ClearRestriction` comment is the one site binding each E5 refusal to its sentinel, by label only ("row E5's `failed`-phase refusal", "its other-job refusal"); CODE-6 item 4 and CODE-7 item 10 cite row E5, its order paragraph, and that comment — BECAUSE the sentinel names are the one fact row E5 does not carry. This differs in kind from round 1 (which moved the phase check into the store and rewrote all six sites to agree): no mechanism changes; the conditions, the order, the HTTP cells, and the audit cell leave the code deliverables.
WATCHOUT: the same restatement class remains, outside this group, at CODE-7 item 6 (E1/E2 in-progress answers: "`202` with the existing job's id ... `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` when it does") and CODE-7 item 7 retry bullet ("Map `*ErrActiveJob` to `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` and `ErrNotFailed` to `409 ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED`"). Both agree with the table today. A later single-source round should give them the CODE-7 item 10 citation form. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 items 6 and 7.
WATCHOUT: keep CODE-7 item 10's "It does not decide the phase from its own `Get`" — the tree handler has no phase check today (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go `handleClearErasureRestriction` calls `erasureJobs.Get` then `users.ClearProcessingRestriction`), so adding one in the handler is the obvious wrong implementation of E5. Keep T8.11's last sentence too: it names the discriminating assertion.
FACT: Edit 2h (spec-changes.md, appended sentence "Each write an executor makes to a job applies only while the job is in the phase the executor read and has not been retried since") is the spec home of the guard requirement; `SameAttempt` is its representation and cites §12.8 (Erasure job phase field (crash recovery)), not "Edit 2h", in code.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: its WATCHOUT on the ABA case located the reason sentence that has to travel with the predicate.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the lifecycle tick is gated by polling the existing `gatewayleader.Elector.IsLeader` in `tenantDeletionRunner.tick`, in place of `pg_try_advisory_lock`; the `pool` field and `advisoryLockKey` are deleted; the runner is built in `buildAdminRouter`, carried on `gatewayWiring`, and started from `startLeaderElectedSweeps` with the elector it builds (CODE-7 item 5, D16) — BECAUSE §12.5 already puts gateway-scoped singleton jobs under the `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease, the session-level lock leaks on one backend under the required transaction pooler, and polling keeps the tick on an uncancelled context so a lease loss does not turn in-flight work into failures — ALTERNATIVES: `leaderGate.Add("…", runner.Start)` (rejected: client-go cancels the leader context on lease loss; item 4 treats only the budget's DeadlineExceeded as a non-failure, so a rolling deploy would `fail` the in-flight erasure job, and `Reconciler.ReconcileTenant` would `fail` the replica's in-memory tenant-deletion job, which `ensureJob` then keeps if that replica regains the lease); `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` in a per-tick transaction (rejected: pins a backend for up to the 10-minute budget); dropping the lock with no gate (rejected: N-fold eraser and Phase-4 work on every replica).
DECISION: the completion receipt is wired to a sink over the gateway's `policy.AuditAppender` that returns `Append`'s error (the `auditReceiptSink.WriteReceipt` pattern), a runner with no receipt hook counts the write as failed, and item 5 keeps the runner's existing `Receipts` start gate — BECAUSE `Router.emit` / `ChainAuditSink.EmitAdminEvent` drop the append error, so the obvious adaptation of `runErasureToCompletion` would clear `processing_restricted` with no receipt; and once a missing receipt fails closed, removing the `Receipts` gate (the old item 5) buys nothing — ALTERNATIVES: a required `NewRunner` parameter (rejected: signature churn across callers for the same predicate); keeping the gate removal (rejected: erasure without an appender can no longer complete, so the split gating in `reconcileOnce` and T8.12's first case pin a dead path).
FACT: `buildAuditPipeline` sets `auditAppender` on both branches (Postgres `pgAudit`, or the in-memory `policy.NewChainSetAppender`), so in the gateway binary `deletionReconciler.Receipts` is always wired and the `Receipts` gate only matters to tests that pass a nil appender — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/auditpipeline.go:187,263,345; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:725-727,789.
FACT: `startLeaderElectedSweeps` builds the elector as a local and always runs `go leaderGate.Run(w.watchdogCtx)`, which drives `elector.Run` and so maintains `IsLeader` even with zero registered jobs; it runs after `buildAdminRouter` (main.go:218 then :275), so the runner must cross on a `gatewayWiring` field, as `w.immediateGCSweep` already does in the other direction — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:59-84,468; pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go `LeaseElector.Run`, `IsLeader`, `AlwaysLeader.IsLeader`.
WATCHOUT: outside a cluster, or with `--gateway-leader-election=false`, the elector is `AlwaysLeader`, so every replica ticks. On the tier-4 compose stack T8.6's two replicas both run `RunPending`; item 1's guarded write is what keeps that correct, so T8.6 stays valid but does not exercise the Lease. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:59-84.
WATCHOUT: a nil `*tenantDeletionRunner` method value (`w.lifecycleRunner.Start`) is a non-nil func that panics when called; check the field for nil before starting it.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]: "CODE-7 item 5 must move that gate so erasure still runs" no longer holds; with a fail-closed receipt, erasure cannot complete without an appender, and the gateway always has one, so the gate stays.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: its FACT on the PgBouncer lock leak and its OPEN on the erasure liveness stall were accurate and made this design short.
OPEN: `erasurejob/saltlockpg.WithSaltLock` takes a blocking session-level `pg_advisory_lock` on one acquired conn and unlocks in a separate statement, the same leak under transaction pooling; a pseudonymize whose lock statement lands on another backend blocks until the tick budget ends. Pre-existing and not changed by this group; a later round should file it for BUILD-GAPS through REC-1. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go:51-64.

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: the tier-7a concurrent-executor test is a new intent T8.15 under "Tests that land with CODE-7", and S13's tiers become "0, 1, 3, 4, 7a, 8" — BECAUSE the Testing preamble lands each intent in its deliverable's step, so a 7a test of CODE-7's guard belongs to S13; T8.11 stays tier 1 as the scripted, discriminating cases (the Restart ABA case) — ALTERNATIVES: appending the case to X1 as the finding suggested (rejected: X1 is TEST-1, lands at S16, and covers the creation fence; putting it there makes the suggested S13 tier change wrong, since S13 would then land no 7a test); relabelling T8.11 as tier 7a (rejected: loses the deterministic tier-1 pin of the PhaseLog-length discriminator).
DECISION: T8.15 runs over the erasurejob `Memory` store (Mode A, build tag `load_local`) rather than Postgres — BECAUSE the guard predicate lives in the runner's Update helper, `Memory.Update` holds `m.mu` across mutate, and the Postgres atomicity of `Update`/`Complete`/`ClearRestriction` vs `Restart` is T7.2 and T7.6 at tier 2.
DECISION: T8.15's checker asserts quiescent, end-of-run invariants (failure-counter increments equal `failed` entries in `PhaseLog`; no phase repeats between two `initiated` entries; no write to a job after it is `completed`; receipts sharing a `ReceiptAt` are byte-identical; flag names the job when in progress, cleared when completed) — BECAUSE the job store and the user store take separate locks in Memory, so a mid-run cross-store snapshot can observe a torn state that is not a defect.
WATCHOUT: do not assert "every receipt for the job is byte-identical": a same-token executor's `fail` can land between the receipt and `Complete` (accepted in review-log decision on `Complete` taking `expect`), after which `Restart` clears `ReceiptAt` and a second attempt writes a different receipt. Key the identity on `ReceiptAt`. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md T8.11 ("a `fail` ... that lands between the receipt and `Complete` leaves the job `failed` or `initiated`"); CODE-7 item 3.
OPEN: that same interleaving emits `gdpr.erasure_completed` for an attempt that ends `failed` and is later retried, so the audit trail can carry a completion receipt for a job that did not complete on that attempt. Nobody has adjudicated whether §12.8/§16.7 tolerate a receipt preceding a non-completion; a reliability or compliance lens should decide (candidate remedies: `Complete` before receipt is ruled out by Edit 2h's order; a token bump on the `ReceiptAt` write was rejected for receipt byte-identity). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 item 3; review-log decision "Store.Complete takes expect Job".

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: memstore reservation coverage is closed by one pointer sentence appended to T1.2 ("Every T1.6 case also runs against `memstore` with `WithCreationMarkers`."), so T1.6 stays the one statement of the reservation cases and T1.2 (tier 1) is where the memstore store-under-test is named — BECAUSE the gateway's memory branch ships `memstore.New()` as the session store (cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:288), so its `Reserve`/`CreateReserved`/`ReservedSessionIDs` and reservation deletion are production fail-closed code — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's re-enumeration of T1.6's cases inside T1.2 (rejected; a second statement of the cases that drifts when T1.6 changes, which is how the ownerless case went missing twice); widening T1.6's lead to "Against real Postgres and `memstore`" (rejected; T1.6 is tier 2 and the memstore run is tier 1, so it needs a split tier label); a shared store conformance suite (rejected; no such pattern exists under pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore, and inventing one is new test infrastructure for one test).
FACT: every T1.6 case is meaningful against memstore with no exception. The serialization case holds because `userstore.Memory.Update` and `tenantstore.Memory.Update` hold their `m.mu` across `mutate`, and CODE-1 item 4 has memstore read markers under `s.mu`, so a blocking mutate blocks `Reserve`. The read-pool case reduces to returning the reservation, since memstore has no read pool. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/userstore.go:224-235 (`Update` locks `m.mu` before `mutate`); pkg/gateway/environment/tenantstore/tenantstore.go:599.
WATCHOUT: do not add an exception list ("other than the serialization case") to the T1.2 pointer; nothing in T1.6 is Postgres-only in effect, and an exception list is the narrowing pattern this loop has already watched fail.
FACT: memstore `DeleteByUser` and `DeleteByTenant` today delete only session rows under `s.mu` (pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/memstore/memstore.go:332, :542); the reservation map deletion is new code CODE-1 item 7 adds, and the T1.6 `DeleteByUser`-then-`CreateReserved` case run on memstore is the one that discriminates the D25 hole in memory mode.
CORRECTS [OPEN in review log, round "T1.2 (memstore parity) has no reservation coverage at all" and "memstore Reserve/CreateReserved/ReservedSessionIDs have no tier-1 test"]: decided above.

### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: CODE-7 item 8's receipt sink now writes "in the payload layout `ChainAuditSink.EmitAdminEvent` writes (the receipt under `detail` and the user as `target_resource`)" instead of "as `auditReceiptSink.WriteReceipt` does"; the Testing retarget of `erasure_test.go`/`erasure_retry_test.go` now also asserts the receipt through a recording `WithCompletionReceipt` fake rather than the Router's `AuditSink` — BECAUSE `auditReceiptSink` marshals a flat payload, which would move `jobId` off `unmapped.lenny.detail` (T8.6's `findAuditDetail`) and the receipt off the recording AuditSink (`awaitAuditEvent` in erasure_test.go) — ALTERNATIVES: retargeting the tier-4 test to a flat layout (rejected; changes the receipt's audit layout for every consumer with no spec basis).
FACT: the tree's receipt goes through `Router.emit` -> `ChainAuditSink.EmitAdminEvent`, which nests the emitter map under `detail` beside `actor_subject`, `actor_tenant_id`, `target_resource`, and appends to `ActorTenantID`'s chain ("platform" when empty), and logs rather than returns an `Append` error — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/audit_sink.go `EmitAdminEvent`; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go `runErasureToCompletion` (`r.emit(..., "gdpr.erasure_completed", subject, receipt)`); cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `auditReceiptSink.WriteReceipt` (flat map).
WATCHOUT: item 8 appends on the job's tenant chain, while the tree appends on the requesting principal's tenant chain (`ActorTenantID`, else "platform"). T8.6 queries `tenantId=acme` as `platform-admin`; if a later reviewer finds the chain choice moves the receipt, that is the item-8 "job's tenant chain" clause, not the payload layout — EVIDENCE: tests/tier4_integration/erasure_job_test.go `findAuditDetail`, erase as "platform-admin".
UNVERIFIED: what `actor_subject` the tick-time receipt carries (no request principal in `RunPending`; the job's `InitiatedBy` is in the receipt body). Next non-spec reviewer should decide whether item 8 needs to name it.
Citer sweep: summary "CODE-7 item 8 names the sink", T8.12 "receipt sink of CODE-7 item 8 returns its appender's `Append` error", T8.13, and the checklist all remain true; no other site named `auditReceiptSink`.

### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: D16 and CODE-7 items 4 and 5 keep erasure gated on the tick's session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock`. Under the spec-required PgBouncer transaction mode that lock leaks onto one backend, so ticks on every replica skip unless their lock statement lands on that backend. The shipped erasure executor (one handler goroutine per job, erasure.go:202 and :328) had no such gate — BECAUSE the r1 OPEN at review-log [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1] ("liveness, separate from this group's finding") was left for this round to confirm, and the D16 rewrite now asserts the lock "only limits duplicate work", which is false for liveness — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it as a REC-1 BUILD-GAPS entry (weaker; erasure is newly routed through the lock by this proposal); `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` held for the tick (rejected; it holds a transaction and an xmin horizon open for up to (K+1) budgets).
FACT: the gateway already runs a pooler-independent singleton primitive, the `gatewayleader` K8s Lease elector (cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go startLeaderElectedSweeps; pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader), used by the §12.5 GC and other singleton sweeps. With the ErrJobMoved guard (CODE-7 item 1), the tick no longer needs the Postgres lock for correctness. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:227-243; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:790-797 (pool: w.pgPool, the --postgres-dsn pool).
FACT: `pgstore.List` returns every session row of the tenant in any state, with no state filter and no pagination, and `sessions` has only the `(tenant_id, created_at)` and `root_session_id` indexes, with none on user_id. Each drain therefore reads the tenant's full history from the primary twice. Shipped suspend did the same from the replica, and the Phase 3.5 hold enumerator lists the whole tenant, so this is no worse than shipped. Not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:624-684; migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:100-101.
OPEN: mass-erasure throughput. RunPending runs every job serially on the one tick holder, while shipped ran one goroutine per job. N concurrent T4 jobs meet the 1-hour SLA only while N times the per-job time stays under 1 hour. Not filed, because the spec states no erasure request rate to quantify against. Owner or a later perf pass should decide whether this needs per-job leases. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 item 4; review-log WATCHOUT near line 782.
USEFUL [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1 FACT on the leaked lock under transaction mode]: it gave the leak mechanics, which I re-verified against tenantdeletion.go:235-240 and spec/12:41.

WATCHOUT: DrainSessions now calls ForceTerminate on `context.WithoutCancel`, so the budget is checked only between sessions. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/session_admin.go ForceTerminate (store.Update then onTerminal); pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:119
UNVERIFIED: under the transaction-mode pooler the spec requires (spec/12 §12.3 **Connection pooling**), the tick's session-level `pg_try_advisory_lock` can be left held on a server backend that the unlock never reaches. Later ticks then acquire the lock only when the pooler routes them to that backend. D16 treats the lock as limiting duplicate work. Because erasure now runs only in the tick, a leaked lock would also slow erasure progress. Whether this happens depends on the pooler's routing and its server_lifetime, so it was not filed. The performance or Kubernetes lens, or P5b, should check. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:227-244

### [non-spec.3.review-performance.1]
FACT: `startLeaderElectedSweeps` falls back to `gatewayleader.AlwaysLeader{}` whenever `rest.InClusterConfig()` fails, so every gateway the tier-4 harness starts (`tests/testinfra/gateway.StartWith`, a host process) reports `IsLeader() == true`. Two tier-4 replicas both run the CODE-7 item 5 tick concurrently, and no compose-stack test can exercise the Lease handover — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:58-85; pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:169-181; tests/tier4_integration/erasure_job_test.go:53.
FACT: `LeaseElector.IsLeader` is an atomic that only `Run` sets, and `Run` is driven by `go leaderGate.Run(w.watchdogCtx)` at the end of `startLeaderElectedSweeps`. A runner whose `Start` is launched there skips its first immediate tick as a follower, which is harmless — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:143-163; cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go (last statement).
FACT: the audit appender is never nil (`pgAudit` or `policy.NewChainSetAppender`), so the runner's kept `Receipts` start gate is always open in practice — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/auditpipeline.go:187,263,345; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:725-727.
WATCHOUT: summary D16 says a tick "can outlive a lease loss by up to its budget". No tick-wide budget exists. CODE-5 item 1 bounds each tenant's Phase 2 drain separately, the other tenant-deletion phases are unbounded, and CODE-7 item 4 bounds `RunPending` after them, so a stale ex-leader can start erasure work long after it lost the lease. The SameAttempt guard makes this safe for erasure, so I did not file it. Tenant-deletion phases have no such guard, and a lease overlap can double-run them (duplicate receipts). The shipped advisory lock was no better under the transaction pooler, so it is not a regression — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:247-268.
OPEN: T8.6's multi-replica resume case no longer exercises a single executor now that the tick is leader-gated (filed this round as a missing-test finding). Whoever fixes it should place the lease-holder-kill case at tier 8 on Kind, beside tests/tier8_chaos/leader_election_test.go.

FACT: The completion-receipt sink of CODE-7 item 8 writes through the raw `policy.AuditAppender` rather than the `auditscope.Validator` that wraps the admin sink. This bypasses no control: the validator passes any write whose context carries no authenticated principal, and the tick's context carries none. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditscope/auditscope.go:139-143; cmd/lenny-gateway/auditpipeline.go:157-158,187,263
FACT: `gatewayleader.AlwaysLeader` is the fallback when the gateway is not in-cluster or when `--gateway-leader-election=false`. Under that fallback, every replica runs the tick, so a multi-replica compose stack (T8.6) runs erasure concurrently on both replicas. ### [non-spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: erasure leaves the tenant-deletion tick and runs as its own sweep, `erasurejob.Runner.Poll(ctx, interval)` registered with `leaderGate.Add` in `startLeaderElectedSweeps` at `--tenant-deletion-interval`; `RunPending` runs each active job not already running on this runner in its own goroutine (mutex-guarded running-ID set), applies no budget, and returns when its runs return; `fail` writes nothing once `ctx` has ended (CODE-7 items 4 and 5, D16) — BECAUSE `reconcileOnce` runs `ReconcileAll` (Phase 4 `DeleteByTenant`, unbudgeted) ahead of anything else in the same goroutine, a budget that cancels `store_deleting` restarts a whole-user re-walk (erasure.go DeleteByUser walks every session per eraser; miniostore lists 7 prefixes per session), and SIGTERM cancels `w.watchdogCtx`, which the old DeadlineExceeded-only exemption turned into a terminal `failed` — ALTERNATIVES: IsLeader-polling erasure loop (gate already supplies and cancels a leader ctx); RunPending before ReconcileAll (still delays jobs submitted during a blocked Phase 4); budget-between-phases with a round-robin cursor (shared sequential budget, head-of-line); sequential unbudgeted RunPending (one large job delays others past the T4 1-hour SLA); goroutine per job with no running set (overlapping Poll ticks double-run a job on one replica); launching on context.Background (goroutine with no exit, no stop on lease loss).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a budget or a WithoutCancel `fail` for erasure. Each earlier round bounded erasure inside the shared tick (deadline ctx, then per-tick budget, then a DeadlineExceeded exemption), and each produced the next finding. The rule now keys on `ctx.Err()` for any cause. `--lifecycle-tick-budget` bounds only the Phase 2 drain (CODE-5 item 1). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go `Gate.Run` (each job in its own goroutine on leaderCtx); cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `reconcileOnce`; cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go `go leaderGate.Run(w.watchdogCtx)`.
WATCHOUT: the tenant-deletion runner stays off the gate and keeps IsLeader polling, because a cancelled leader ctx fails its in-memory job (`Reconciler.fail` is terminal). Do not "unify" it onto `leaderGate.Add`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go runPhase error path to `fail`.
FACT: `erasurejob.Runner.Start(ctx, tenantID, userID)` already exists, so the new sweep method is `Poll`. `gatewayleader.Gate.Add(name, run func(context.Context))` takes a closure, so registration wraps `Poll` with the interval. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go `func (r *Runner) Start`; gatewayleader.go `func (g *Gate) Add`.
DECISION: staged text cites row E4 rather than D16 at CODE-7 item 4 and omits "(D16)" at item 5, a small departure from the design wording — BECAUSE no staged non-spec file cites a summary decision number, and D16 holds the reasons — ALTERNATIVES: the design's "(row E4, D16)" (adds a pointer from staged text into the summary's decisions).
OPEN: the erasure sweep has no concurrency limit, as the shipped per-job goroutines had none. A concurrency-limit flag would be a non-spec operator default; raise it only if a load tier shows many concurrent erasures exhausting the pool.
CORRECTS [remedy.2026-10-16.creation-path-table DECISION on the applied DIRECTIVE: "`RunPending` runs under a per-tick budget ..., `fail` writes on `context.WithoutCancel`"]: superseded. Erasure has no budget, `fail` writes nothing on an ended ctx, and the sweep is on the leader gate. The flag named there as `--erasure-tick-budget` was in fact staged as `--lifecycle-tick-budget` and now bounds only the Phase 2 drain.
CORRECTS [standing-context DIRECTIVE on CODE-7 item 4 "make every fail write use a context that is still live"]: replaced by the ended-context rule above. The SLA stays alert-only, and T8.10 still pins a past-deadline retry that completes.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: `Restart` maps a unique violation on `idx_erasure_jobs_in_progress_per_user` to `*ErrActiveJob` after rolling back and re-reading, and carries no NOT EXISTS guard; `Start` keeps `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` plus a same-transaction read (CODE-6 item 3) — BECAUSE an `UPDATE` has no `ON CONFLICT`, and under READ COMMITTED a read-before-write guard cannot see a concurrent uncommitted `Start` or `Restart`, so the index is the only arbiter that answers row E3's 409 in both commit orders — ALTERNATIVES: users-row `FOR UPDATE` first (deadlocks with `Complete` and `ClearRestriction`, which lock job then users); advisory xact lock (second serializer beside the index); SAVEPOINT (more mechanism, nothing left to write); guard plus fallback mapping (two paths for one outcome).
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce "Do not catch a unique-violation error" or a users-row-first lock order for `Start`/`Restart`; `Restart` must keep locking the job row first because CODE-6 item 4 and T7.2 depend on that order. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 items 3 and 4; T7.2.
WATCHOUT: `Restart`'s re-read after the violation can find no in-progress job when the other job finished in between; row E3 still answers 409 and needs no job ID, so this is not a defect. Do not file it.
CORRECTS [non-spec.2.review-fresh.1 UNVERIFIED on Restart's guarded UPDATE]: confirmed in round 5 and fixed by the mapping above; the suggested user-row lock was rejected as a deadlock.
DECISION: `handleEraseUser` calls `Runner.Start(ctx, tenantID, userID, initiatedBy, overrideReceipt) (Job, error)`, and `admin.ErasureRunner` shrinks to that one method (CODE-7 item 6) — BECAUSE `Runner.Start` is the only job builder that applies `WithDeadlineResolver` (wired only on the runner), so a handler-built Job persisted `Deadline` 0 against §12.8 **Erasure SLA enforcement** — ALTERNATIVES: a resolver on the admin Router (second copy of the T4/72h rule); `Store.Start` stamping the deadline (no tier read in the store); keeping `Run` on the interface (no admin caller after the goroutines go).
FACT: `admin.ErasureRunner` implementers are `*erasurejob.Runner` and `erasureRunnerStub` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/routestub_test.go; `Runner.Start` callers are erasurejob runner_test.go, sla_test.go, completion_hook_test.go, and the erase handler. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:22-25, :166; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:208-227; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:594-604.
DECISION: the CODE-6 `Start` doc comment and T7.1 now cite row E1's cells in the `Restart` form, keeping only the `*ErrActiveJob` payload, which row E1 does not name but its in-progress `202` with that job's ID needs.

DECISION: a runner with no drain hook or no hold-check hook counts the missing hook as that hook's failure (CODE-7 item 1, `initiated` bullet), so row E6's failure outcome applies; T8.2 pins the rule at tier 1, and T8.6's existing `completed` assertion pins the production wiring at tier 4 — BECAUSE the runner nil-skips every optional hook (runner.go `if r.memoryPreflight != nil {` pattern) and `erasurejob.NewRunner` is built inline in `buildAdminRouter`, so no cmd-level test can prove production wires a hook; fail-closed turns the deterministic journey assertion (tests/tier4_integration/erasure_job_test.go `job["phase"] != "completed"`) into the wiring test — ALTERNATIVES: extending T8.12 with a wiring clause (needs an extracted builder and still does not prove buildAdminRouter calls it); a hold-after-202 case in T8.6 (races a ticker of unknown phase); required `NewRunner` parameters (already rejected for the receipt hook on signature churn).

### [non-spec.5.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: Replaced the summary watch-out that restated the refusal-only undo and keep-the-reservation rule with a pointer to the **Undo** and **Reservation** column rules of the **Creation-path table** — BECAUSE those column rules (non-spec-changes.md, rule list under the table) are the one home of the predicate and its reason, matching the reduced E6 watch-out — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the bullet (loses the flag on a recurring trap); listing the watch-out in the table intro's citer list (the Erasure entry-point table intro does not list its watch-out either).

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: erasure leaves the tenant-deletion tick and becomes its own sweep on the §12.5 leader gate (`leaderGate.Add` with new `Runner.Poll(ctx, interval)` on `--tenant-deletion-interval`). `RunPending` runs each active job not already running on this runner in its own goroutine, deduplicated by a mutex-guarded running-ID set, and erasure has no budget. `fail` writes nothing when `ctx` has ended, so the job stays in its phase. The `--lifecycle-tick-budget` flag now bounds only the tenant Phase 2 drain. This one change closes G1's findings 1, 2, and 3 — BECAUSE the earlier rounds tried to bound erasure inside the shared tick (first a deadline context, then a per-tick budget, then an exemption for the budget's DeadlineExceeded), and each bound became the next finding. Removing the bound and the coupling is a different kind of answer. The gate's own cancellation now limits how long a deposed leader keeps running, which was the budget's other job, and it ties the goroutines' exit to a context as code-best-practices requires — ALTERNATIVES: launching on context.Background (rejected; the goroutine has no exit and lease loss would not stop a deposed executor); a budget checked between jobs and phases, with steps on WithoutCancel and a round-robin cursor (rejected; it keeps one shared sequential budget and adds cursor state); an erasure loop that polls IsLeader (rejected; the gate already supplies the leader context and its cancellation); a sequential RunPending with no budget (rejected; a large job still delays every other job).
FACT: the shipped tree runs each erasure job in its own goroutine on context.Background with no deadline context (`go r.runErasureToCompletion` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go handleEraseUser and handleRetryErasureJob). `Runner.Run` applies no deadline, and `WithDeadlineResolver` only stamps `Job.Deadline`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:131-137,208-221.
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate.Run` starts each registered job with the leader context, which client-go cancels on lease loss. Under AlwaysLeader that context is `w.watchdogCtx`, and main cancels it on SIGTERM. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go Gate.Run; cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go `go leaderGate.Run(w.watchdogCtx)`.
FACT: the name `erasurejob.Runner.Start(ctx, tenantID, userID)` is already taken, so the polling method needs another name (`Poll`). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:208.
FACT: a tenant-deletion tick cancelled by SIGTERM only fails the in-memory job. syncBack writes nothing for PhaseFailed, and the restart rebuilds the job from the tenant row. Leaving the tenant-deletion runner's launch context unstated is therefore harmless. Only lease loss, where the process keeps running, makes the in-memory failure stick. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go syncBack; pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go fail.
WATCHOUT: once erasure has no budget, a leaked `saltlockpg.WithSaltLock` lock under transaction pooling blocks that job's pseudonymize until lease loss or shutdown, where the tick budget used to end the wait (see OPEN at review-log `erasurejob/saltlockpg`). Because jobs now run concurrently, the leak no longer blocks other jobs. The leak was already present before this design, and REC-1 should still file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go:51-64.
WATCHOUT: this design treats a generic "tick" in erasure context (rows E1, E3, E6, CODE-7 items 1 and 3) as the erasure sweep's poll and leaves those cells as they are. Only "lifecycle tick", "tenant-deletion tick", and "share one tick" were made wrong. Do not rewrite every "tick".
CORRECTS [review-log FACT at ~1066 and ~1286, "RunPending under CODE-7 item 5 is started with context.Background()"]: the FACT is about the tenant-deletion runner and still holds for it. Under this design erasure runs on the leader context, and CODE-7 item 4's `fail` rule handles cancellation.
CORRECTS [review-log WATCHOUT at ~1103, "do not register tenantDeletionRunner.Start on leaderGate.Add ... would fail the in-flight erasure job"]: the erasure half no longer applies, because erasure is on the gate and a context that ends is not a failure. The tenant-deletion half stands.
OPEN: concurrency of erasure jobs is unbounded (one goroutine per active job, at most one active job per user). That matches the shipped per-request goroutines. A mass per-user erasure burst would need a bound, and a bound is a non-spec default that requires a flag. Left for a later round only if a lens files it.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: `Restart` maps a unique violation on `idx_erasure_jobs_in_progress_per_user` to `*ErrActiveJob` (roll back, read the in-progress job in a new transaction) and carries no read-before-write in-progress guard; the index is the one arbiter for both `Start` (ON CONFLICT) and `Restart` (error mapping) — BECAUSE an `UPDATE` has no ON CONFLICT and any READ COMMITTED guard misses an uncommitted `Start`/`Restart` — ALTERNATIVES: lock the users row `FOR UPDATE` first in `Start` and `Restart` (the round-2 UNVERIFIED's lead; rejected: `Complete` locks the job row, marks it completed, then waits on the users row, while a users-first `Start` waits in the index check on `Complete`'s xmax, which deadlocks, and `ClearRestriction` locks job-then-users, so `Restart` users-then-job deadlocks with it and breaks T7.2's job-row-lock cases); a `pg_advisory_xact_lock(tenant,user)` taken by `Start` and `Restart` (rejected: a second serialization mechanism beside the index); a SAVEPOINT around the UPDATE (workable, but rollback-and-read is shorter and needs no in-transaction continuation).
WATCHOUT: the fresh read after the rollback is outside the aborted transaction, so it must use the same all-tenants read pattern `Get` uses (`lenny_tenant_guard`, see [non-spec.2.review-fresh.1] FACT on `InAllTenants`). If the other job finished between the violation and the read, the read finds none; `Restart` still returns `*ErrActiveJob` (the handler's 409 for E3 needs no job field). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 3; migrations/0057_tenant_guard_pooler_mode.up.sql
FACT: today only `erasurejob.Runner.Start` builds a job (ID, `StartedAt`, `Deadline` from `WithDeadlineResolver`, initial `PhaseLog`), and the T4/72h resolver is wired only on the runner. `admin.ErasureRunner` has exactly two implementers: `*erasurejob.Runner` and `erasureRunnerStub` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/routestub_test.go. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:208-227; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:599-604; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:22-25
DECISION: the erase handler reaches `store.Start` only through `Runner.Start(ctx, tenantID, userID, initiatedBy, overrideReceipt) (Job, error)`; `admin.ErasureRunner` shrinks to that one method (`Run` loses its only admin caller once the goroutines go) — BECAUSE the runner owns the deadline resolver, and §12.8 **Erasure SLA enforcement** requires the job to record the deadline — ALTERNATIVES: a resolver on the admin Router (rejected: second copy of the T4/72h rule); `Store.Start` stamping the deadline (rejected: the store has no tenant-tier read); computing the deadline at read time (rejected: the spec has the job record it).
DECISION: summary watch-out "A unique violation aborts the Postgres transaction..." is deleted rather than reworded — BECAUSE CODE-6 item 3 is where the implementor meets the trap, and the watch-out's ON CONFLICT prescription is false for `Restart`.
USEFUL [non-spec.2.review-fresh.1 UNVERIFIED on the guarded UPDATE]: located the defect; its suggested users-row lock is refuted above.

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: the production wiring of `WithSessionDrain` and `WithHoldCheck` is pinned by making both hooks fail closed when nil (one sentence in CODE-7 item 1's `initiated` bullet: a missing hook counts as that hook's failure, so row E6's failure outcome applies), with T8.2 reused for the tier-1 missing-hook case and one sentence on T8.6 naming its `completed` assertion as the discriminating one — BECAUSE the proposal already chose this for the receipt hook (CODE-7 item 3 "a runner with no receipt hook leaves the job in progress", review-log ledger entries at the round that decided the receipt sink), so the tier-4 journey's existing `completed` assertion then fails over the real `buildAdminRouter` wiring when either hook is unwired; a cmd-level unit test of an extracted helper proves only that the helper wires, not that `buildAdminRouter` calls it — ALTERNATIVES: extending T8.12 with a wiring clause (rejected; the runner is built inline in `buildAdminRouter`, so the test needs a new extracted builder and still does not prove the inline caller uses it); a T8.6 case that sets a hold after the `202` and before the tick (rejected; the tick interval is a ticker with an unknown phase, so the case races the first tick and is flaky); required `NewRunner` parameters (rejected for the receipt hook earlier for signature churn; same predicate).
FACT: `erasurejob.NewRunner` has exactly one production caller, the inline chain in `(*gatewayWiring).buildAdminRouter`, and every optional runner hook is nil-skipped. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:594; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go:271 `if r.memoryPreflight != nil`.
FACT: cmd wiring in this package is tested through extracted `build*` helpers at tier 1 (`buildQuotaEraser`, `buildSessionScopedErasers`), never through `buildAdminRouter` itself. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/quota_erasure_wiring_test.go; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:969.
WATCHOUT: after the missing-hook rule lands, every existing runner test that drives a job through `initiated` with no drain or hold-check fake fails its job with `store_delete`; the implementor attaches no-op fakes (24 `NewRunner(` sites under pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob tests, plus pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure_test.go). This is not staged text, because the failures are self-revealing. EVIDENCE: grep `NewRunner(` in *_test.go.
WATCHOUT: the memory preflight hook stays nil-skippable: production attaches it only when `w.memories != nil` (adminrouter.go, `WithMemoryPreflight` block), so do not extend the missing-hook rule to it.
DECISION: T8.2's drain-error case moves into T8.9's per-cause list, and T8.9 asserts row E6's cells by pointer — BECAUSE row E6 is the only outcome home and the table intro says the T8 tests cite it; T8.2 is reused for the missing-hook case rather than deleted, so no number gap appears — ALTERNATIVES: deleting T8.2 (leaves a gap and gives the new case no home).
OPEN: X2's "store error in the drain listing" fault hits the eraser's session lister too, and X2's "restriction stays set" assertion holds whether or not the drain ran. G1 owns X2 this round; a later round should judge whether X2 needs a drain-specific assertion.

FACT: the only goroutine launch inside `startLeaderElectedSweeps` uses `w.watchdogCtx` (`go leaderGate.Run(w.watchdogCtx)`), and `watchdogCancel` is deferred in `main` and fires at process shutdown, after `runServers` returns. ### [non-spec.5.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: reported one finding: CODE-6 item 3's "equivalent guarded `UPDATE`" for `Restart`, together with "Do not catch a unique-violation error", answers a `Restart` that races a `Start` (or a second job's `Restart`) for the same user with a raw unique violation of `idx_erasure_jobs_in_progress_per_user`, not row E3's `409 INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` — BECAUSE an `UPDATE` has no `ON CONFLICT`, its `NOT EXISTS` guard cannot see the other transaction's uncommitted job, and the partial-index check waits and then raises 23505 once that job commits; the users-row `FOR UPDATE` comes after the job write, so it does not serialize the guard. This closes UNVERIFIED in [non-spec.2.review-fresh.1] — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as hardening (rejected; Edit 2g and row E3 state the 409 for "another job in progress", and T7.3 pins only the sequential case).
FACT: the r4->r5 diff touches only CODE-7 item 6 and the item 7 retry bullet. Every cell they cite exists: E1 has the in-progress 202 and the `Start`-error 500, E2 has the in-progress 409, and E3 has the two `Restart` sentinel answers. Neither bullet introduces a new mechanism. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:88-90, :292-296, :335, :339
FACT: before this proposal the tenant-deletion tick took `pg_try_advisory_lock` once per tick and released it, while each replica kept its own in-memory `tenantdeletion` job store. Any replica could therefore win a tick and re-run the deleting span from its own job state. Concurrent or duplicate Phase 6 receipts across replicas are pre-existing, and the move to the leader gate does not make them worse. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go:227-245, :273-317
FACT: session inserts go through exactly one SQL statement (`INSERT INTO sessions` at pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:174) and eight `.Create(` call sites, and rows C1 to C11 cover all of them. Only `delegation.Service.buildChildSession` writes a non-zero `DelegationDepth` on a session row; the other `DelegationDepth:` literals are elicitation provenance or eval records. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/service.go:1446; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1355
WATCHOUT: in §17.4 Source Mode (`--sqlite-path`), the snapshot layer persists the session, tenant, and user memory stores, but it does not persist the erasurejob `Memory` store or a new memstore reservation map. After a restart, a user can therefore keep `processing_restricted` naming a job that no longer exists, and a derive's reserved ID is forgotten. A new erase request recovers the first case. I did not file this because it is a dev-mode posture. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:314-342
WATCHOUT: the edge-case 1 bound ("at most one budget per drain") covers only Phase 2 drains and `RunPending`. Phase 3.5 escrow migration and Phase 4 `DeleteByTenant` still run unbounded on the tick ahead of `RunPending`. This is a close variant of the fixed "Phase 2 drain runs unbounded" finding, so I did not re-file it. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:407-490

### [non-spec.5.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed two findings, both on running erasure inside the lifecycle tick. (1) RunPending's budget cancels a phase in flight and Edit 2h/CODE-7 item 1 restart that phase from its first step, so a `store_deleting` whose no-op re-walk exceeds the budget never completes and, being sequential, starves every job after it. (2) Tenant-deletion Phase 4 (and 3.5) run unbudgeted in `ReconcileAll` before `RunPending`, so a large tenant's Phase 4 halts all erasure; Edge case 1's bound covers only Phase 2 drains — BECAUSE the shipped design ran each erasure in its own goroutine (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:202,328) with neither coupling — ALTERNATIVES: fence hot-row and write-amplification findings (not filed, see FACTs); Phase 2 full-tenant primary listing every paused tick (not filed: suspend at the merge base already lists every tenant row, and the earlier non-goal rejects a NonTerminal filter).
FACT: `miniostore.Store.DeleteBySession` issues one ListObjects per object type (7 prefixes) for every session even when it is empty, and the orchestrator walks every session ID for every session-scoped eraser before any user-scoped eraser — EVIDENCE: pkg/blobstore/miniostore/miniostore.go:749-771; pkg/gateway/storage/erasure/erasure.go:95-123; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:969-1013.
FACT: the fence's tenant-row lock adds no new hot-row class. `sessions.tenant_id` already references `tenants(id)`, so every session insert already takes FOR KEY SHARE on the tenant row; no frequent writer updates the tenants or users rows (only admin handlers, the tenant-deletion syncBack, and erasure call `tenantstore`/`userstore` Update) — EVIDENCE: grep "UPDATE tenants|UPDATE users" pkg cmd; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/*.go `users.Update` callers.
FACT: `ListActive` on every replica's sampler is cheap: MIG-1's partial unique index has exactly the in-progress predicate, so the planner can scan it; 30 s interval per replica (cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:702-703).
WATCHOUT: Phase 4's `sessions.DeleteByTenant` is one `DELETE FROM sessions WHERE tenant_id = $1` in one transaction (pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:810-825), and CODE-1 adds the reservation delete to that transaction. Its duration scales with the tenant's whole session history, which no spec section bounds.
UNVERIFIED: `ErasureJobOverdue` is `lenny_erasure_job_age_seconds > scalar(lenny_erasure_job_deadline_seconds)` (pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:1136), and every gateway replica's sampler sets the deadline gauge, so with two or more replicas `scalar()` sees several series, returns NaN, and the alert never fires. This predates 0094, but CODE-7 item 4 makes that alert the only signal for a budget-stalled job. Someone with Prometheus should confirm and, if it holds, file it for BUILD-GAPS.

### [non-spec.5.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: reported one finding: CODE-7 item 5 moves the `Start` launch into `startLeaderElectedSweeps` without naming its context, and CODE-7 item 4 exempts only the budget's `context.DeadlineExceeded`, so a tick launched on `w.watchdogCtx` (the only context that function holds, cancelled by `defer w.watchdogCancel()` at main.go:269 on SIGTERM) fails the in-flight erasure job durably on every graceful shutdown of the leader — BECAUSE item 5's own reason for forbidding `leaderGate.Add` is that cancellation turns in-flight work into failures, and the review-log FACTs that say "the runner starts on context.Background()" describe the merge base (adminrouter.go:797), which item 5 replaces — ALTERNATIVES: filing the lease-overlap of tenant-deletion phases (not filed: pre-existing under the per-tick advisory lock, `syncBack` never regresses a `deleted` row, per [non-spec.3.review-security.1]); the lost `gdpr.legal_hold_overridden` on a crash between `Start` and the handler emit (not filed: `Router.emit` already drops append errors at the merge base, and the security lens owns it); the unbounded `fail` write on `WithoutCancel` (not filed: TCP keepalive bounds it).
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1 FACT and non-spec.3.review-test-coverage.1 FACT "the lifecycle runner starts on context.Background()"]: true only at the merge base. After CODE-7 item 5 the launch site is `startLeaderElectedSweeps`, whose launches all use `w.watchdogCtx` (leader_sweeps.go:468), and the proposal names no context, so "parent-cancel classification is not reachable in production" no longer follows from the tree.

### [non-spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `RunPending` is bounded by one runner-wide in-flight limit over the existing mutex-guarded running-ID set (oldest `StartedAt` first, a job with no free slot waits for a later call), set by `WithMaxConcurrentJobs` from new flag `--erasure-max-concurrent-jobs` (env `LENNY_ERASURE_MAX_CONCURRENT_JOBS`, default 2, unset runner limit 1); startup refuses a flag below 1 or, with `pgPool` set, not below the pool's `MaxConns`. D16 is the reason's one home — BECAUSE each pseudonymize holds one pooled connection in `saltlockpg.WithSaltLock` and needs a second for `tenantpg` Get/Update on the same pool, so N concurrent holders >= MaxConns deadlock whatever the tenant mix — ALTERNATIVES: per-tenant serialization (reviewer's fix; distinct-tenant holders still exhaust the pool); sequential `RunPending` (round-5 head-of-line starvation; limit 1 is that case); semaphore-blocking `RunPending` (Poll piles blocked goroutines); silent clamp to MaxConns-1 (hides misconfiguration); fixing saltlockpg itself (out of scope).
FACT: the hold-and-wait is real: `saltlockpg.WithSaltLock` acquires a pool conn, runs `pg_advisory_lock`, and calls `fn` with the conn held; `BillingEraser.Pseudonymize` calls `tenants.Get`/`tenants.Update` inside `fn`; `stores.go` builds `tenantpg.New(pool)` over the same `pgxpool.New` pool as `pgPool`, with no MaxConns set (pgx default max(4, NumCPU)). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go `WithSaltLock`; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing.go `withSaltLock` call sites; cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go `pgPool = pool`, `tenantpg.New(pool)`; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go `WithRotationLock(saltlockpg.New(w.pgPool))`.
OPEN: the admin salt-rotation route (`adminRouter.WithErasureSaltRotation(billingEraser)`) takes the same lock with the same hold-and-wait, outside the runner's limit. At `--erasure-max-concurrent-jobs = MaxConns-1`, one concurrent rotation of another tenant makes MaxConns holders. The default (2 under a pool of at least 4) leaves headroom; whether the startup check should reserve a connection for rotation (limit < MaxConns-1) is for a later round to judge. Not staged here.
WATCHOUT: summary's CODE-7 files-touched line had dropped `cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go` when the per-tick budget flag was removed; this round re-adds it for `--erasure-max-concurrent-jobs`. A later round removing the flag removes it from that line and the CODE-7 **Targets:** line together.

Only review of that call site catches it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go `Gate.Run` launches jobs only from the elector's onStarted.
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate.Add(name, run)` registers before `Run`; `Run` launches each job on the leader context in onStarted and the elector cancels it on loss. `startLeaderElectedSweeps` takes no parameters and constructs `leaderElector` locally. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go `NewGate`, `Add`, `Run`; cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go `startLeaderElectedSweeps`.

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: bound RunPending by one runner-wide in-flight limit, the size of the existing mutex-guarded running-ID set, set by `WithMaxConcurrentJobs` from a new flag `--erasure-max-concurrent-jobs` (default 2; unset runner limit 1); the gateway refuses to start when the flag is below 1 or, with the Postgres pool configured, not below `pgPool.Config().MaxConns`; listed jobs start oldest `StartedAt` first and a job with no free slot waits for a later call — BECAUSE the reviewer's per-tenant serialization does not close the hang: N distinct-tenant jobs in `Pseudonymize` at once each hold one pooled connection in `saltlockpg.WithSaltLock` and each need a second for `tenants.Get`, so N >= MaxConns deadlocks without any lock contention. A global limit below MaxConns guarantees a holder a connection whatever the tenant mix — ALTERNATIVES: per-tenant serialization (insufficient, above, and a second rule beside the set); reverting to sequential (reintroduces the head-of-line starvation round 5 removed; limit 1 is that degenerate case and stays available to operators); rewriting saltlockpg to do the work on the lock connection or to poll `pg_try_advisory_lock` (holders still need a second connection; out of scope); blocking RunPending on a semaphore (overlapping Poll calls pile up blocked goroutines while a long job runs); clamping the flag silently to MaxConns-1 (hides operator error; refusing is fail-closed).
FACT: pgxpool's pool is built with no MaxConns (`pgxpool.New(ctx, *postgresDSN)`), so its size is max(4, NumCPU) unless the DSN sets `pool_max_conns`; `tenantpg`, `saltlockpg`, and the session stores share it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:141,160,277; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:642; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go:51-67; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing.go:146,157,177.
FACT: the tier-4 compose stack reaches Postgres directly on 15432; the PgBouncer endpoint (16432) runs in session mode for one leak test, so a tier-4 erasure test does not hit the transaction-pooler advisory-lock leak. EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/compose/compose.go:170-181.
WATCHOUT: the existing T8.10 case "a second RunPending ... completes another job" needs a limit of at least two; with the unset limit of 1 it cannot start the second job. The fixer folds the limit into that case.
WATCHOUT: the limit bounds only what the erasure sweep holds. `issuedtokenstore.WithSubjectLock` uses the same hold-a-connection-while-waiting pattern on the same pool, so erasure plus admin-token rotations can still contend; that is not this proposal's finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:123-135.
CORRECTS [review-log OPEN "the erasure sweep has no concurrency limit, as the shipped per-job goroutines had none ... raise it only if a load tier shows"]: the limit is needed without a load tier: the salt lock's hold-and-wait makes unbounded fan-out a deterministic deadlock once concurrent pseudonymize runs reach the pool size, independent of load.
USEFUL [review-log WATCHOUT "do not reintroduce a budget or a WithoutCancel fail for erasure"]: kept the fix to a slot count; no budget returns.
OPEN: the session-level advisory lock in `saltlockpg.WithSaltLock` under the required transaction pooler (review-log OPENs at the `erasurejob/saltlockpg` entries) is still unfiled in REC-1; it is a separate finding for a later round, and it can hang a pseudonymize at any limit.

WATCHOUT: `DrainSessions` calls `ForceTerminate` on `context.WithoutCancel(ctx)` (CODE-3 item 3), so a budget-test fake that blocks on `ctx.Done()` hangs forever. FACT: in the T5.3 budget case, the single assertion "job stays at `PhaseTerminateSessions` with `State` `disabling`" fails both defects the finding names: without `WithTimeout` the drain succeeds and the job advances; with a `fail` mapping `State` becomes `failed`. Asserting `DeadlineExceeded` wrapping duplicates T3.1. The second tenant advancing in the same tick pins Edge case 1's "other tenants" bound, which T5.1 (single `ReconcileTenant`) does not.
