### [non-spec.6.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate.Run` launches each registered job with `go job.Run(leaderCtx)` and never waits for it, and the elector sets `ReleaseOnCancel: true`, so on SIGTERM the Lease is released while erasure goroutines started by `Poll` may still be in flight. The next leader can therefore start the same job before the old goroutine returns; the CODE-7 item 1 `SameAttempt`/`ErrJobMoved` guard (Edit 2h) is what keeps that correct, not the gate. The running-ID set dedups only within one runner. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:133 (`ReleaseOnCancel: true`), :222-229 (`Gate.Run`).
FACT: `leaderGate.Run` starts at cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:468, after every `Add`; the new `Poll` registration must sit above that line (Add is not concurrency-safe). `--tenant-deletion-interval` defaults to 30s (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1332), so the erase handler's loss of the immediate goroutine kick delays a job by at most one interval.
UNVERIFIED: row E6's status and counter cells ("Drain failure: `store_delete`", "Incremented once") read as unconditional, while CODE-7 item 4 makes a failure observed after `ctx` ended write nothing and count nothing. Not filed under this lens (item 4 is the more specific rule and T8.10 pins it); a mechanism or single-source lens should judge whether row E6 or E4 needs the ended-context exception as its one home.
UNVERIFIED: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:891 says the salt's KMS decrypt is scoped to "the erasure job's service account — gateway replicas and runtime pods cannot decrypt it", yet the shipped and staged executor runs inside the gateway process. Pre-existing in the tree and in every earlier draft; not introduced by this round's sweep change. Owner of a later salt/KMS proposal should check.
DECISION: no findings this round under the Kubernetes-idiom lens — BECAUSE the r5→r6 diff moves erasure onto the existing leader gate (leader ctx, cancellation on lease loss, goroutine exits tied to ctx), introduces no CRD status writes, finalizers, webhooks, or controller work on a request path, and keeps the tenant-deletion runner off the gate for a reason the tree confirms — ALTERNATIVES: filing "no test proves Poll is gate-registered rather than ungated" (rejected; close variant of the refuted leader-failover-test finding, and an ungated Poll is still correct under the SameAttempt guard).

### [non-spec.6.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: the round-6 rewrite moved erasure execution out of the tenant-deletion tick onto `leaderGate.Add` (`Runner.Poll`), and `RunPending` now runs every listed job in its own goroutine with no concurrency bound. `gatewayleader.Gate.Run` launches each job with the leader-scoped context and relaunches it on re-acquire. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:191-229; non-spec-changes.md:333-334.
FACT: Postgres `Pseudonymize` holds one `pgPool` connection blocked in `SELECT pg_advisory_lock` per waiter, and the lock holder then needs a second `pgPool` connection for `tenants.Get` and `tenants.Update`. `tenantpg` sits on the same pool, and pgxpool's default `MaxConns` is max(4, NumCPU) because no DSN or chart sets `pool_max_conns`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go:51,57,67; pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/billing.go:146,157,177; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:641-642; cmd/lenny-gateway/stores.go:141,160,277.
WATCHOUT: with the unbounded fan-out, enough same-tenant jobs reaching `pseudonymizing` together hold every pool connection in lock waits. The holder cannot acquire a connection, nothing times out because the leader context has no deadline, and every gateway Postgres caller stalls. The memory-mode billing eraser has no lock at all (adminrouter.go:641), so same-tenant pseudonymize calls also interleave on the tenant salt there; `tenantstore` copies the salt on read (tenantstore.go:578), which keeps that interleaving from zeroing another job's salt. Reported as a finding; the smallest fix is per-tenant exclusion inside the runner's existing running-job set.
FACT: the merge-base tree already ran concurrent jobs, one handler goroutine per erase request, so the hazard predates the proposal. The round-5 sequential `RunPending` removed it, and round 6 put it back.

EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:222-229; cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go:218,275; cmd/lenny-gateway/workers.go:269

Not a finding at GDPR request volumes. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/erasure.go:202,228,328

### [non-spec.6.review-reliability.1]
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate.Run` launches each registered job as `go job.Run(leaderCtx)` and never waits for it; the gate runs under `w.watchdogCtx`, which `main` cancels only by a deferred `watchdogCancel()` at return. A `Poll` registered on the gate therefore sees its context end on lease loss (client-go cancels the leader context) and at process shutdown, and nothing else. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go `Gate.Run`, `LeaseElector.Run` (ReleaseOnCancel: true); cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go `go leaderGate.Run(w.watchdogCtx)`; cmd/lenny-gateway/main.go `defer w.watchdogCancel()`.
FACT: `Runner.Run(ctx, jobID)` re-reads the job with `Get` by ID and returns on a terminal phase, so a stale `ListActive` snapshot in an overlapping `RunPending` does not re-run steps of a job that completed or failed between the listing and the in-flight-set check. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/runner.go `Run`.
FACT: `tenantDeletionRunner.Start` falls back to 30s when `interval <= 0`; CODE-7 item 5 registers `Poll` at `--tenant-deletion-interval` without stating that fallback, and `time.NewTicker` panics on a non-positive interval. The flag default is 30s and no chart or doc sets it, so this was judged below the bar. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/tenantdeletion.go `Start`; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go `tenant-deletion-interval`.
DECISION: no reliability finding on the round-6 redesign (erasure on its own leader-gated `Poll`, the ctx-ended `fail` rule, the per-runner in-flight set, and `Restart` relying on the unique-index violation) — BECAUSE the ctx-ended rule keeps lease loss and graceful stop resumable, cross-replica overlap is fenced by `SameAttempt`, and the tenant-deletion tick's overlap after a lease loss is no worse than the tree, where the advisory lock alternates between replicas that each rebuild their own in-memory job from `TenantState` — ALTERNATIVES: filing the missing per-job deadline in `RunPending` (rejected; the tree's erasure goroutine already runs on `context.Background()` with no deadline, so a hung dependency is not a regression this proposal introduces).
WATCHOUT: rows E1, E3, E4, and E6 of the Erasure entry-point table still say "the tick", although erasure now runs in `Poll` rather than in the tenant-deletion tick. The meaning still holds (it is Poll's interval), so this is wording rather than a finding. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Erasure entry-point table rows E1, E4, E6.

### [non-spec.7.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `RunPending` admits a listed job only when no run of its tenant is in flight on this runner and fewer runs than `--erasure-max-concurrent-jobs` are in flight; the in-flight set is a map job ID to tenant (CODE-7 item 4) — BECAUSE every pseudonymize of a tenant runs under that tenant's `erasure_salt_migration` lock (billing.go `withSaltLock` around Pseudonymize and the rotation), so a second in-flight job of the tenant can only wait on its own tenant's lock and would hold a slot doing nothing; under the tenant rule a waiting tenant holds at most one slot — ALTERNATIVES: a per-run bound reusing `--lifecycle-tick-budget` (rejected; erasure is unbudgeted by design, see the standing WATCHOUT, and a budget would only rotate the stuck jobs); unbounded goroutine-per-job as the shipped tree had (rejected in r6 for pool hold-and-wait).
WATCHOUT: the tenant rule means one tenant's jobs never pseudonymize concurrently, so any test of the pool hold-and-wait limit must use distinct tenants (T8.6 now erases one user in each of eight tenants). A same-tenant fixture passes trivially and pins nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go `WithSaltLock`.
WATCHOUT: T8.10's discriminating assertion for the tenant rule is the later `globex` job completing while an `acme` job blocks in `Pseudonymize` with a limit of two; under the bare limit the later `acme` job would take the second slot and `globex` would not start. T8.10's first case now completes "a job of another tenant", because a same-tenant second job is held back by the tenant rule.
FACT: `saltlockpg.Lock.WithSaltLock` takes `pg_advisory_lock` and `pg_advisory_unlock` as two separate `conn.Exec` statements on one pooled pgx connection; behind a §12.3 transaction-mode pooler the two can land on different backends, so the lock can leak. No BUILD-GAPS entry records it (only the 0040-era entry that introduced the seam). REC-1 item 15 files it, and the summary's shipped-tree defects list carries it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go `WithSaltLock`; spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.3 **Pool mode**.
UNVERIFIED: whether the leaked-lock residual (as many tenants with leaked locks as the limit halts all erasure) deserves more than a REC-1 finding; the owner's rule makes it a bounded departure, and nobody has checked whether the gateway's DSN actually routes through PgBouncer in the shipped chart.

### [non-spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: RunPending admission becomes one predicate over one in-flight map (job ID -> tenant ID): a listed job starts only when no run of its tenant is in flight on this runner and fewer runs than the limit are in flight; "not already running" is subsumed. The global limit and its MaxConns startup refusal stay. D16's "one large erasure does not hold up the rest" is deleted and replaced by the one-clause reason for the tenant rule; T8.10's last clause and T8.6's "eight users of one tenant" are rewritten; REC-1 gains one item filing the saltlockpg pooler leak — BECAUSE a tenant's pseudonymizes already serialize on `erasure_salt_migration:{tenant_id}`, so a second in-flight job of that tenant can only hold a slot (and a pooled connection) waiting; with the tenant rule a tenant's lock wait (rotation migration, leak) costs at most one slot, and the global limit still caps distinct-tenant holders below MaxConns — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's per-run bound reusing `--lifecycle-tick-budget` (rejected: it is the erasure budget rounds 4-6 removed; cancelling `store_deleting` restarts a whole-user re-walk, see review-log WATCHOUTs at ~1297 and ~1519); try-lock-and-yield in Pseudonymize (rejected alone: single-pass oldest-first admission re-admits tenant A's second job into the freed slot every Poll, so tenant B still starves; it also changes the `SaltRotationLock` interface the rotation route shares); fair interleaving of the listing by tenant rank (rejected: does not help once slots are occupied by waiters); per-tenant cap of limit-1 (hair); a bound on the salt-lock wait only (a new budget-like mechanism for a defect whose fix is in saltlockpg).
FACT: round 6 rejected per-tenant serialization only as a REPLACEMENT for the global limit ("distinct-tenant holders still exhaust the pool"). Adding it beside the limit as one predicate over the same set is not that rejected alternative. EVIDENCE: review-log ~1479.
FACT: `saltlockpg.Lock.WithSaltLock` issues `pg_advisory_lock` and `pg_advisory_unlock` as two autocommit statements on one pgxpool connection; under a transaction-mode PgBouncer (§12.3 requires `pool_mode = transaction`; the chart's default `connectionPooler` is `pgbouncer`) they can reach different backends, so the unlock misses and the lock leaks. No BUILD-GAPS finding records it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/erasurejob/saltlockpg/saltlockpg.go:50-67; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:41; charts/lenny/templates/_helpers.tpl:306; grep of BUILD-GAPS.md for saltlock finds only the F-12.8 salt resolution at ~23949.
WATCHOUT: residual after this fix: L distinct tenants each with a leaked salt lock and a pending job still occupy all L slots (limit 1 makes one such tenant enough). That is the REC-1 item's residual, bounded by the saltlockpg fix, not a reason to reintroduce a run budget.
WATCHOUT: T8.6's pool case must use distinct tenants after this fix; with one tenant the tenant rule serializes the jobs and the case no longer exercises the pool hold-and-wait the limit exists for.
UNVERIFIED: whether the gateway's `LENNY_POSTGRES_DSN` in any shipped answers file points at the PgBouncer service rather than Postgres directly; the REC-1 finding's verifier should check. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:1358-1368 (dsn is operator-supplied).
USEFUL [review-log ~1466/~1479 round-6 DECISION]: its ALTERNATIVES list saved re-deriving why per-tenant alone and try-lock alone fail.

### [spec.14.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: OD4's source-owner refusal is serialized by extending the C8 reservation to the source owner (`session_reservations.source_user_id`, read `FOR SHARE` by `Reserve` and by `CreateReserved` after `DELETE ... RETURNING source_user_id`; `ReservedSessionIDs` and `DeleteByUser` match `user_id` or `source_user_id`), and `requireSourceOwnerNotRestricted` stays as the early refusal — BECAUSE the early check alone runs once, unlocked, before an unbounded copy, so a cross-owner derive (bob deriving alice's session) admitted before alice's flag commits inserted after the flag, or after her erasure, and her erasers never listed derivedID — ALTERNATIVES: see WATCHOUTs below.
WATCHOUT: an insert-time source-owner read without the recorded `source_user_id` does not close the window. An insert that commits during restriction before the erasers run consumes the reservation, and the source owner's lister then never sees derivedID. The column without the read misses the same insert. Both halves are needed, mirroring fence plus `CreateReserved` for the owner (D25). EVIDENCE: non-spec **Reservation** column rule; CODE-1 item 7.
WATCHOUT: scoping Edit 2b item (2) to requests that arrive while the flag is set (an accepted residual) lost because the chunked copy has no deadline and a crash leaves alice's copy indefinitely, the reason D25 rejected the derive crash residue. Applying `CreationMarkers.Refusal` to the source owner lost because it would refuse derives from a soft-disabled or invalidated user's session, a rule beyond OD4. A second reservation row per user lost because `CreateReserved` would need to know how many rows to expect.
WATCHOUT: replay (C10) needs no store change. Its new owner is `source.UserID`, so the fence's owner read already serializes with the flag. Do not add `source_user_id` handling to `pgstore.Create` or row C10. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replay.go row literal; non-spec row C10.
FACT: `Reserve`'s signature gains `sourceOwnerUserID`, and C11 now passes `""`. The design's site list missed the C11 **Gates** cell call `store.Reserve(tenantID, owner, childID, true)`, which the signature change falsified; it now reads `store.Reserve(tenantID, owner, "", childID, true)`. EVIDENCE: non-spec row C11.
FACT: a missing source-owner row admits at `CreateReserved` (matching the **Gates** rule for `requireSourceOwnerNotRestricted`), and a source-owner read error aborts the transaction as `Create`'s read errors do (fail-closed). EVIDENCE: non-spec CODE-1 item 7 `CreateReserved` paragraph.
DECISION: the caller-side `ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS` refusal gets its spec home in Edit 2b item (3), now "each session-creation, derive, replay, or interaction-resolution request the affected user makes is rejected", and Edit 4k's causes read "the user makes the request, owns the session being created, or owns the source session of a derive or replay" — BECAUSE Edits 2b and 2c had removed the only spec basis for `requireActiveUser`'s restricted-caller branch (user_gate.go reads `principal.Subject` and refuses on `ProcessingRestricted`), which T2.2 pins — ALTERNATIVES: deleting that branch (fail-open: a restricted user could create sessions owned by another user); a separate item (5) (two items stating the interaction-resolution refusal). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/user_gate.go `requireActiveUser`; called from create.go, create_and_start.go, derive.go, and replay.go.
FACT: Edit 4k's reservation-ended sentence now says "an erasure ended the reservation", because the source owner's erasure also ends a C8 reservation. Its pointer stays on **Lifecycle fence and drain**.
FACT: the SPEC design bullet on user erasure listed what the restriction refuses beyond the fence and was falsified by the item (3) rewrite; it now names the user's own creation, derive, replay, and interaction-resolution requests.

### [spec.14.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the cross-owner derive race (bob derives alice's session into a bob-owned session; the source-owner pre-check passes, then alice's flag commits, then the copy and insert run) is closed fail-closed by extending the existing reservation to the source owner, not by moving or deleting `requireSourceOwnerNotRestricted`. `session_reservations` gains `source_user_id`. On C8, `Reserve` records it and reads that row FOR SHARE. `CreateReserved` takes `DELETE ... RETURNING source_user_id` and reads that row FOR SHARE, refusing when it is restricted. `ReservedSessionIDs` and `DeleteByUser` match `user_id OR source_user_id`. The pre-check stays as the early refusal ahead of the isolation-downgrade audit event — BECAUSE the insert-time read alone misses an insert that commits during restriction before the erasers run (the reservation is consumed and alice's lister never sees it), and the recorded source owner alone misses the same insert. Both halves together are the exact parallel of fence plus `CreateReserved` for the owner (D25). — ALTERNATIVES: scope item (2) to requests that arrive while the flag is set and file a residual (rejected: the chunked copy is unbounded and a crash leaves the residue indefinitely, the same reason D25 rejected the derive crash residual); a second reservation row per user with PK (tenant, session, user) (rejected: `CreateReserved` would have to know how many rows to expect); applying `CreationMarkers.Refusal` to the source owner (rejected: it would refuse a derive from a soft-disabled user's session, which is a new rule beyond OD4, and it would break the summary watch-out against folding).
FACT: replay (C10) needs no store change. Its new owner is `source.UserID` (replay.go `UserID: source.UserID` in the row literal), so the fence in `Create` already reads the source owner's row FOR SHARE and serializes with the flag. Its transcript copy runs after the insert into a session alice owns, which is the generic in-flight class, not this race. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replay.go handleReplay row literal and `s.store.Create`.
FACT: in `handleDerive` the owner is never empty while the source owner is set (`userID := req.UserID; if userID == "" { userID = source.UserID }`), so "records nothing for an empty owner" never drops a source owner. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go `handleDerive` owner resolution.
DECISION: the caller-side refusal gets its spec home in Edit 2b item (3), rewritten in place to "each session-creation, derive, replay, or interaction-resolution request the affected user makes is rejected", and Edit 4k's causes become "makes the request, owns the session being created, or owns the source session". OD3's substance stays (interaction resolution is refused) — BECAUSE `requireActiveUser` refuses a restricted principal whoever owns the new session (user_gate.go reads `principal.Subject`), and T2.2 pins that. Deleting the caller check instead would let a restricted user create sessions for another user — ALTERNATIVES: deleting the processing_restricted branch of `requireActiveUser` (rejected: fail-open, and it contradicts the current §12.8 text).
WATCHOUT: Edit 4k's reservation-ended sentence must not say "the owner's erasure" any more, because the source owner's erasure also ends a C8 reservation. The **Reservation** column rule's "Only the owner's erasure ... removes another writer's reservation" is the non-spec twin, and both change in the same edit.
OPEN: the **Gates** column rule and CODE-2 item 1 call `requireActiveUser` "the §11.4 check on the caller", but it also implements §12.8 Article 18 item (3). That label was incomplete before this round, so it is a separate finding: it is cosmetic unless an implementor writes the wrong `// spec:` citation from it.
CORRECTS [remedy.2026-10-18.executor-resource-table OD4 DECISION]: "run ... before ... any reservation" still holds for the early check, but it is no longer the whole OD4 mechanism. An in-flight derive is refused at `CreateReserved` and undone (summary D29 after this round).

### [spec.14.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: `Reserve` refuses a restricted source owner (option (b) of the finding), and the **Reservation** column rule now states the refusal by code and details plus its order: before the fence's refusal in `Reserve`, after the fence and insert in `CreateReserved`. CODE-1's Reserve doc comment, `pgstore.Reserve` sentence, and CreateReserved paragraph cite that rule; T1.6 gains the `Reserve` case with a disabled new owner as the order-discriminating case — BECAUSE Edit 2b item (2) has the reservation read the source owner's row under the fence's shared lock, and a `Reserve` that admits a flag already committed can record a reservation after the erasure's `DeleteByUser` ran, which then survives the erasure — ALTERNATIVES: option (a), dropping `Reserve` from the source-owner read (rejected; leaves that window open and contradicts Edit 2b item (2)).
WATCHOUT: "whatever the new owner's markers" in the **Gates** rule holds for `requireSourceOwnerNotRestricted` and for `Reserve`, not for `CreateReserved`: at insert time the fence runs first, so a race where the source owner's flag commits after `Reserve` and the new owner is also marked answers the fence's code. Edit 2b item (2)'s "whoever would own the new session" concerns the owner's identity and is not contradicted. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md **Reservation** column rule; CODE-1 item 7.
DECISION: Edit 4k's `details.userId` now names "the restricted user, or, for an ended reservation, the new session's owner", and CODE-1 says the missing-reservation refusal's `userId` is the row's owner — BECAUSE `DeleteByUser` of the source owner also ends a reservation, and `DELETE ... RETURNING` then returns no row, so the store cannot name the erased user.
DECISION: Edge case 4's in-progress parenthetical now reads "(the fence, or `CreateReserved`'s source-owner read)", since the fence reads only the new owner.
FACT: citer sweep over **Reservation**, **Gates**, Edit 4k, `Reserve`, `details.userId`, and "whatever the new owner's markers" across all proposal files: summary D29 and the "Undo and ReleaseReservation" watch-out, CODE-2 item 3, T2.3, T2.6, the spec-reference map row for Edit 4k, and the docs deliverables naming Edit 4k only name their targets and stay true. No summary, problem-statement, or DEFERRED change was needed.

### [spec.14.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed two findings on the owner-decision edits. (1) Edit 2b item (2) states the source-owner refusal as holding "while this flag is set", but the staged check (row C8 Gates, CODE-2 item 9) runs once, before the copy, outside the insert transaction. A derive into another owner that is admitted before the flag commits inserts after the flag, the drain, and possibly the erasure, carrying the restricted user's copied workspace under an owner whose erasure never runs. (2) Edit 4k's cause list for ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS omits the restricted CALLER on session creation, yet `requireActiveUser` stays on C1 to C4, C8, and C10 (CODE-2 item 1), and T2.2 asserts that a restricted caller replaying an active user's session is refused. BECAUSE replay's new owner is `source.UserID` (row C10), so the fence covers replay at insert, and only a derive whose owner differs from the source owner has the window. The reservation is keyed to the new owner, so the source owner's erasure does not reach it. ALTERNATIVES: filing the unstated precedence between the source-owner check and Edit 1a's code order (rejected; a derive into an invalidated owner from a restricted source is an edge case, and stating the order would be over-specification); filing Edit 4i's "a request for any other job" wording (rejected; wording only).
FACT: derive copies at derive.go:329 (`deriveChunkedCheckpoint`) and inserts at derive.go:401, so the window between the start-time source-owner check and the insert is the full copy duration. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/derive.go:310-312,329,401
FACT: `requireActiveUserForAction` checks the principal and answers ERASURE_IN_PROGRESS on create, start, derive, and replay, and on interaction resolution. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/user_gate.go:35-63; create.go:88, create_and_start.go:118, derive.go:140, replay.go:65, interactions_resolution.go:37
USEFUL [spec.13.review-docs-alignment.1 WATCHOUT on requireActiveUser for a restricted principal]: pointed at finding (2). Edit 4k's new enumeration is what turns that WATCHOUT into a staged contradiction, because it now lists causes that leave the caller out.

### [spec.14.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 14 — BECAUSE the round-14 delta (Edit 2h's conditional-write sentence replacing "One executor advances a given job at a time", Edits 2b items (2) and (3), 2l, 4i's ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED clause, 4k, and new 4l) traces cleanly under crash, lease loss, and two concurrent executors: `failed` is a value of the §12.8 `phase` enum (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:805), so a stale executor's write after another executor's `fail` does not apply; "has not been retried since" closes the ABA case where a retry returns the job to a phase the stale executor read (EX3 `PhaseLog` append); a stale `fail` records nothing, which matches Edit 2d and EX4; a lost retry or clear response is answered safely (a re-sent retry gets 409 ERASURE_JOB_NOT_FAILED while the job runs; a re-sent clear of the same failed job is admitted and is a no-op) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the D29 source-owner check as a TOCTOU (a C8 derive with a `req.UserID` override that passes the check before `Start` commits and inserts after it). Rejected: replay (C10) is still fenced because its new owner is the source owner, and a derive in flight at `Start` has the same outcome as one that committed just before it, which OD4 accepts. Also rejected: dropping the clear endpoint's trigger exemption (Edit 2f) to close the clear-versus-retry race; round 13 security judged it optional, and the code remedy (re-read the job inside the users-row lock) satisfies Edit 4i.
FACT: after Edit 2h, concurrent executors are permitted and fenced rather than excluded. Two executors in `verifying` can each write a receipt before one `Complete` applies, and D17 (identical receipts, EX7) is what keeps that consistent with Edit 2e's "same content". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md EX7 row; spec-changes.md Edit 2h, Edit 2e.
WATCHOUT: Edit 2h says "the phase the executor read"; the code reading (CODE-7 item 1, `SameAttempt` against the job the runner last read or wrote) is the intended one. Do not file the wording as blocking an executor's second advance. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-7 item 1 (line ~360).
USEFUL [spec.13.review-security.1]: its USEFUL on the E5 clear-versus-retry race let this round settle the Edit 2f exemption question without re-deriving it.
USEFUL [spec.11.review-reliability.1]: its reservation crash walk still holds; the round-14 delta does not touch the reservation sentences.

### [spec.15.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding in spec round 15 — BECAUSE the round-14 delta (design bullet, Edit 2b items (2) and (3), Edit 4k, summary D29) keeps every check fail-closed and sourced from trusted rows: the source owner is `source.UserID` from the stored source session (not client input), `Reserve` and `CreateReserved` read it `FOR SHARE` in the primary transaction so it serializes with the flag's exclusive write, a source-owner read error aborts, and the source owner's `DeleteByUser` ends the reservation so a late `CreateReserved` refuses — ALTERNATIVES: filing the unlocked caller check in `requireActiveUser` against item (3) "while this flag is set" (rejected; a create admitted just before the flag commits produces a session another user owns with no restricted-user data copied, the same outcome as a request completed before the erase, and below the bar).
FACT: Edit 2b item (3) holds on C1 to C4: MCP `create_session` and the built-in adapters go through `CreateSessionService` / `CreateAndStartService`, which replay `handleCreate` / `handleCreateAndStart` against a recorder, so `requireActiveUser` runs on them. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/service.go `CreateSessionService` (`s.handleCreate(rec, httpReq)`); create.go:88; create_and_start.go:118.
FACT: `requireActiveUserForAction` admits a request with no principal, an empty subject, or no registry row, and fails closed on a user-read error. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/user_gate.go:35-50.
FACT: the erasure sequence never deletes the user row (the flag is cleared on it at completion), so `CreateReserved`'s "missing source-owner row admits" cannot be reached mid-erasure through a deleted row. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md §12.8 **`DeleteByUser` dependency-ordered deletion sequence** steps 1 to 14.
USEFUL [spec.14.fix-design-G1.1 FACT on replay]: replay's owner is `source.UserID`, so Edit 2b item (2)'s "the insert ... also read the source session owner's row" holds for C10 through the ordinary fence; it saved re-deriving why "when one is taken" does not open a replay gap.

DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 17 — BECAUSE the staged spec edits are unchanged since d0fbf0399 (round 16 found nothing), and a re-walk of SPEC-1 to SPEC-4 under crash, lease loss, and Postgres failover found every recovery path idempotent or operator-repeatable: the drain re-runs from `initiated` (Edit 2h) and force-terminate is a no-op on a terminal row; a Phase 2 drain failure stays in phase (Edit 3b) and §12.8 **Idempotency and resumption** resumes the last incomplete phase; a repeat suspend re-runs the drain (Edit 4a); the receipt-then-complete order (Edit 2e) is crash-safe through CODE-7 items 2 and 3 (`ErasedAt` skip, `ReceiptAt` chosen once), so the resumed `verifying` job does not need the in-memory salt again — ALTERNATIVES: filing Edit 2e's "same content" against §12.8 billing step 3 (verification needs an in-memory salt copy a crash loses; rejected, CODE-7 item 2 skips to the receipt once `ErasedAt` is set); filing the C11 fence refusal as a tree-budget leak (rejected, `Delegate`'s deferred `treeBudget.Return` fires on any error after the reserve); filing reservations kept on a plain copy failure (rejected, non-spec Edge case 5 accepts it and Edit 1a says nothing false about it).

### [spec.17.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security-lens finding on the staged spec edits (SPEC-1 to SPEC-4) — BECAUSE every marker the fence reads is a Postgres column on a tenant-scoped row (tenants.suspended/state; users.disabled, deleted_at, processing_restricted with PK (tenant_id, subject) and RLS), a marker read error fails the insert, the legal-hold changes only tighten (retry applies no override; runner re-check fails closed), the clear endpoint is narrowed to failed jobs, and no bound is sourced from a pod self-report — ALTERNATIVES: filing the cross-user `details.userId`/`jobId` disclosure in Edit 4k (rejected; the current spec already discloses both to any caller creating a session for the restricted user, so it is no regression).
FACT: the user registry row is never erased by user erasure; no `users` eraser is wired in the DeleteByUser orchestrator, so the flag cannot vanish mid-job and "a user with no registry row carries no user marker" opens no window during erasure. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go eraser list (leases, semantic_cache, experiment_sticky, billing_buffer, quota, tokens, credential_pool); summary.md Non-goal on the registry row (P5b).
FACT: hard disable is the `deleted_at` tombstone and `IsActive` checks both columns; userstore `Get` does not filter tombstoned rows, so a hard-disabled owner is read as a row with a marker rather than as "no registry row". EVIDENCE: migrations/0003_users.up.sql; pkg/gateway/environment/userstore/userstore.go `IsActive`; pgstore.go (the `deleted_at IS NULL` filter is on a list query, not Get).
FACT: §24.11 force-terminate "releases the assigned pod to the pool", so the drain's pod release rides the existing reuse/scrub controls; the terminal-tail residual is OD1's claim row, not a new finding. EVIDENCE: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md §24.11 force-terminate row.
